## PROBLEM-naming-conventions — Component names must carry the correct type `PREFIX_`
**Symptom:** every author-named component's **`name` attribute** (not the JCR node tag — tags are AEM-internal ids like `panel_77897ce3…` and never conform) should follow `PREFIX_<CamelCaseName>_<shortUuid>` with `PREFIX` fixed per component type. **Owner-relaxed rule (2026-07-21): only the leading `PREFIX_` is enforced** — the `<shortUuid>` is **optional** and the `<CamelCaseName>` is **left exactly as-is (never invented)**. So the only defect is a name whose leading token is not the correct prefix for its type: legacy/lowercase repeat prefixes (`CP_…`, `RP_…`, `panel_…` → `RCP_`/`PN_`), titledraws named `ST_…` (→ `TTL_`), signatures named `TXT_…` (→ `SIGN_`), numericboxes named `NUM_…` (→ `NB_`), `guidedatepicker` named `DP_…` (→ `DATE_`), case slips (`Date_…`, `Pn_…`), timestamped auto-names (`textdraw1780315242122` → `ST_…`). **260 / 286 packages affected, 2,177 violations** (`wrong-prefix` 835 · `raw` 1,342; 1,892 high-confidence, 285 low). Names already carrying the right prefix are **ok** regardless of a missing uuid/CamelCase (`BT_Remove`, `PN_BankingRelationship`, `PN_958d2a4a` all pass).
  **Authoritative prefixes (owner, 2026-07-21) — the ONLY allowed leading tokens; any other must convert to one of these:** `RB_` `DATE_` `TXT_` `CB_` `DD_` `PN_` `RCHT_` `RCP_` `RCBP_` `RCHP_` `NB_` `BT_` `ST_` `TEL_` `TTL_` `TXTM_` `EML_` `IMG_` `CRT_` `SPT_` `ITXT_` `ETXT_` `TBL_` `BARCODE_` `QRCODE_`. Type→prefix: textbox/guidetextbox→TXT, checkbox→CB, radiobutton→RB, dropdownlist→DD, datepicker **and guidedatepicker**→DATE, numericbox→NB, telephone→TEL, email→EML, textboxMultiline→TXTM, titledraw→TTL, textdraw→ST (ITXT/ETXT also accepted), *button→BT.
  **Reconciled scope (owner decisions — do not silently change):**
  - **`SIGN_` is an owner-allowed exception** for `signature` components ("allow SIGN for signatures for now") — not in the authoritative table, but valid; a `TXT_`-named signature converts to `SIGN_`. `DP_` is **not** allowed (guidedatepicker→`DATE_`).
  - **Exempt** system/fixed-name components (guideContainer, rootPanel, toolbar, submit, next/previtemnav, summary, dorOptionsUBS, metadata, letterhead, carousel\*, messagebox\*, previewbutton, signaturebox, footnote placeholders, layouts), the toolbar's **`fwbSaveProgress`** Save Progress button (exempt by *name*, not resourceType — its type is the generic `guidebutton`; the eBanking handler resolves it as `guide.guideRootPanel.toolbar.fwbSaveProgress`, so a `BT_` rename breaks the click contract — see [PROBLEM-nav-save-progress-required](#) / #135), the UBS **"Preview"** panel+button (`name="preview"`, referenced by the preview feature), and `{name}Title` wrapper panels (engine artifacts tracking their parent).
  - **Exempt fragment-referenced fields**: any node with a `fragRef`, an `affrg`-containing name, or an `AF_`-prefixed name (renaming breaks the fragment binding contract).
  - **Repeat panels** (a panel with `minOccur`/`maxOccur` or a `repeatable_*` tag) expect the `RCP_` family; a non-repeat panel carrying `RCP_` is flagged back to `PN_`.
**Detect:** script: `find_naming_violations.py`  _(walks the whole form tree; a name is `ok` iff its leading token is a valid prefix for the node's type/role, else `wrong-prefix` (leading token is a canonical prefix, wrong type) or `raw` (no canonical prefix). Each carries a `confidence`: high for type→prefix + clear panel roles; low for repeat-container sub-roles that need human eyes. Affected = ≥1 non-ok, non-exempt node. `--form AABI_019|AF_AABI` for one form; `--verbose`/`--limit` for the review surface. Idempotent: a fixed form reports 0.)_
**Fix:** script: `set_naming_prefix.py`  _(deterministic prefix normalisation — for each flagged node it (1) strips a leading lowercased resourceType word (auto-names like `tertiarybutton1597…`, `panel_Foo`), else (2) swaps a known/legacy leading prefix (`CP_`/`RP_`/`NUM_`/`PN_`-on-repeat/`ST_`-on-titledraw → the canonical prefix), else (3) prepends the prefix to a real name word (`Client_Advisor`→`TXT_Client_Advisor`) — **keeping the rest of the name verbatim**. It then rewrites **every reference** to the old name in one boundary-safe whole-identifier pass (`.NAME`, `(NAME`, `NAME.instanceManager`, slash-paths, `##NAME##` translation ids), skipping XML tag positions. **High-confidence only by default**; low-confidence repeat-container sub-panels are reported under `flagged_low` and left for a manual pass (`--include-low` to also rename them). Collisions (new name == a surviving name, or two names colliding, or a duplicate name classified to >1 prefix) are reported under `collisions` and skipped — never merges two fields. Prints `{renamed, flagged_low, collisions, remaining_high, ok}`; `ok` = no collisions AND 0 high-confidence violations remain.)_
**Verify:** the fix's own `ok` (no collisions + 0 high-confidence violations remain) is the primary check; re-run `find_naming_violations.py --form <F>` to confirm only `flagged_low` (if any) remain. The `delta` gate must stay green (a broken reference would surface as a new schema violation). **No `Max changed lines` bound** — renaming rewrites every reference, so the diff is intentionally large.
**Runs automatically:** no
**Origin:** manual (owner directive — the attached *AEM Naming Conventions* doc)
**Tracking:** #64
**Status:** swept 2026-07-21 (260/260 addressed — 252 fully conformant + 8 partial-fixed, one accepted shared-name node each; PR #65) — pilot of 6 (AAAA/AAAB/AAAE/AAAI/AAAL/AAAM_019) reviewed (found + fixed the escape-boundary orphan bug), then `--continue` deployed the rest. All via the staged manual procedure (not `run_sweep`: base can't equal the sweep branch; scripts/registry live on `sweep/naming-conventions`). Every deployed form passed an orphan gate (0 references to any renamed-away name, tags excluded) and a corpus re-scan confirms **0 unexpected high-conf violations** among deployed forms. Colliding rename targets (new name already in use / two sources → one target) are disambiguated with a short random uuid suffix (`PREFIX_<Camel>_<8hex>`). **NOT swept:** (a) **8 accepted-as-is exceptions** (owner decision 2026-07-21) — a single `name` is shared by two structurally-different nodes nested in the same branch (e.g. a plain section panel `PN_X` and, several levels below it, a repeatable row panel also `PN_X` that by convention "should" be `RCP_X`). The forms **work correctly**: every reference is either fully path-qualified (`…PN_Authorization.PN_X` vs `…_Table_DYN.PN_X`) or a repeat-only API (`.instanceManager`/`.instanceIndex`) that only the repeatable node has, so AEM resolves each to the right element. A global string rename would break the plain sibling, and a node-scoped rename is per-form fragile for a purely cosmetic prefix — so these are **left as-is, not renamed**: AABT_033, AACQ_019, AAHM_af_ab_019, AAHU_019, AAKL_019, AARW_033, BANR_033, BAXT_019. (b) **low-confidence repeat sub-panels — DONE** (2026-07-21): the `--include-low` pass renamed wrong-prefixed repeat-container sub-panels to the `RCP_` family across 117 forms (all deployed; 2 transient deploy-fails retried OK). A final corpus scan now reports **only the 8 accepted forms as affected** (71 residual violations, all within those 8) — every other previously-affected form is 0-violation. Because of (a), the detector will always report these 8 as affected — so before flipping to `swept`, add them to a documented accept-list (or scope the regression guard to exclude them). The 8 accepted forms were **partial-fixed** (2026-07-21): the fixer applied every fixable rename (skipping only the shared-name conflict) and deployed, leaving **exactly one accepted node each** — AABT_033 `PN_Relazione`, AACQ_019 `PN_Thirdparty_Account`, AAHM_af_ab_019 `ST_Subtitle0`, AAHU_019 `PN_GenericSignature`, AAKL_019 `PN_SignatureIndividual`, AARW_033 `TXT_ShareOfOwnership`, BANR_033 `TTL_Authorized_User`, BAXT_019 `TXT_Accountholder`. **Final corpus state: every form 0 actionable violations.** These 8 nodes are recorded in `find_naming_violations.py`'s `ACCEPTED` accept-list (keyed by form+name), so the detector reports them under `accepted` (not `affected`) and the regression guard stays green. To retire an exception later, fix the form (give the shared-name nodes distinct names) and drop its entry from `ACCEPTED`. **Fixer corrected + full redo (2026-07-22):** the reference-rewrite in `set_naming_prefix.py` was doing a whole-XML text replace (guarded only against XML tag positions), so it clobbered any *visible* string that happened to contain a renamed name — it turned `jcr:title="Investment"` into the field name, etc. Corpus impact was a single form, **AACQ_019** (one `jcr:title`: `PN_StpPortfolioNo` wrongly dragged to `RCP_StpPortfolioNo`); the bug bites new intake forms harder (real-word names in titles/labels). The fixer is now **attribute-scoped**: it rewrites only `name="…"` and `fd:*` rule/script references, never `jcr:title`/`jcr:description`/rich-text `_value`/dropdown `options`/`fd:translationIds`/`bindRef`. All **260 forms regenerated from the pre-sweep zips** with the corrected fixer (`--include-low`) and redeployed; the regenerated corpus is byte-identical (XML) to the previous deployment except AACQ_019's restored title (collision-uuid suffixes re-rolled, being nondeterministic).

## PROBLEM-default-translations — Add the engine's default UI translations missing from form dictionaries
**Symptom:** the conversion engine merges a fixed set of **default UI translations** into every form's translation dictionary at package-write time (`blueprint/core/src/aem/package_writer.rs::assemble_package`, from `profiles/ubs/aem/translations/{de,en,fr,it,sp}.toml`): the toolbar buttons (Back / Next / Submit / Preview / Save Progress / Add), the submission messages, the two Summary labels, and three custom-element labels (For internal bank use only / Signature(s) verified… / Company) — 15 master keys. Older / hand-built forms predate that step and are **missing some or all** of these entries from their per-locale dictionary files (`…/guideContainer/assets/dictionary/<locale>.xml`). This sweep adds the missing default entries to each form's **existing** dictionaries. **Engine-authoritative for this problem** (owner-directed: "these translations get added to the translation key xml files … add them if not present"); the TOMLs are vendored to `.claude/scripts/data/translations/` so the sweep is self-contained (refresh by re-copying if the engine's defaults change).

Two facts where the *deployed corpus* (the ground truth we edit) overrides the *current* engine source: (1) the entry element name is a **UUIDv3** (MD5, no namespace) of the raw master text — every existing `fd_<uuid>` in the corpus is v3, while today's `package_writer.rs` computes v5 (older-lineage / stale-binary split); (2) inside attribute values `>` is left **unescaped** (`&lt;p>…`) while `<`, `&`, `"` are escaped — matching the engine's `reformat_attributes`.

**Scope (strict engine mirror):** only the locales the engine emits defaults for — **de, de-ch (→de), en, fr, it, sp, es (→sp)** (config.toml `[language_synonyms]` de→de-ch, sp→es; master `en`). Regional extras that exist in some forms (en-us 25, en-gb 3, it-ch 4, fr-ch 1, default 4, pt 1) are **left untouched**. **Additive only:** an entry is inserted only if its `sling:key` (`fd_<master>`) is absent — an existing key is never overwritten (form-content translations take precedence, e.g. a form-authored `fd_Next`). Dictionaries are **never created** for a language the form lacks. **228 of 286 packages affected (2,670 missing entries); 58 packages have no translation dictionary at all → out of scope** (synthesising a whole dictionary is a larger change than filling existing ones; 0 already-complete — nearly every legacy form misses at least the custom-element labels).
**Detect:** script: `find_default_translations.py`  _(per form, per targeted existing dict file: which default `fd_<master>` keys are absent; affected = ≥1 missing; emits `missing:{locale:[master,…]}` as the review surface. Idempotent: a filled form reports 0. Forms with no targeted dictionary are counted `no_dictionary`, not affected.)_
**Fix:** script: `set_default_translations.py`  _(for each targeted existing dict file, inserts the missing entries before `</jcr:root>`, byte-formatted exactly like the engine (v3 element name, engine attribute escaping, message = the vendored translation for that locale's language). Rewrites only the changed dict entries in place via the `zip` CLI, like `form_xml.py` repack. Prints `{added, changed:{locale:count}, remaining, ok}`; `ok` = a re-scan self-check shows 0 default keys still missing.)_
**Verify:** the fix's own `ok` (0 default keys remain missing after the insert). **Note:** `check_form.py` / `form_diff.py` only read the form `.content.xml`, which this fix never touches — so a `must`/`must-not` regex or a line-count bound can't see the dictionary edits; the fix's `ok` is the real gate. `delta` (engine MCP) still applies — additive well-formed dictionary entries introduce no new schema violations.
**Max changed lines:** omit — the edits live in the dictionary files, invisible to `form_diff` (`.content.xml` only); gate on the fix's `ok`.
**Runs automatically:** yes
**Origin:** engine-diff (engine adds these defaults to every form; legacy forms lack them)
**Tracking:** #72
**Status:** swept 2026-07-23 (228/228 fixed + deployed, 0 flagged, PR #73 merged) — piloted (6) AAAA/AAAB/AAAE/AAAI/AAAL/AAAM_019 reviewed OK, then --continue for the remaining 222 fixed + deployed. Every form `fix ok:true` (0 default keys still missing on re-scan) and all targeted dictionary files well-formed; deploy verified via packmgr install. Run via the **staged manual/worktree procedure, not `run_sweep`** (scripts + registry live on `sweep/default-translations`, not master — master push is blocked; `run_sweep` would `git checkout master` and lose the branch-only scripts, and its end-of-run worktree can't share the branch the main checkout is on). Element-name UUIDs match the deployed corpus (v3/MD5, not the engine source's v5); additive-only, so no form-authored translation was overwritten. **Enrolment note:** the detector re-scans the corpus each run and will report these 228 as affected again if any regenerate from pre-sweep zips; the deployed corpus is the source of truth (the 58 no-dictionary forms stay out of scope — they legitimately have no dictionary to fill). 0 already-complete going in (nearly every legacy form lacked at least the custom-element labels).

## PROBLEM-formconfig-private-person-default — Form configurator: preselect the individual option
**Symptom:** the form configurator asks what the form is for and each option reveals a different panel; on many forms it opens with **nothing selected**. QA wants it opening on the individual option — `Private Person` on a German form, `Individual` / `Individuo` on an Italian one.

**Which choice, and which option.** The same control PROBLEM-configurator-reset-on-change acts on — a radio whose labels are one of the reviewed sets and which decides two or more panels — and the option whose label names an individual rather than a company (`Private Person`, `Individual`, `Individuo`, `Individuale`, `Persona`; `Persona giuridica` is not one). Its **key** goes into `_value`, and the key differs per form.
**Mechanism = the `_value` property, NOT a `default` attribute.** The component's `widget.jsp` renders an option `checked` when `option.key == guideField.value`, and in this legacy guide runtime the field's initial value is `_value`. **Ground truth: 9 corpus forms already preselect Private Person via `_value="1"`** (AABO/AACP/AACR/AAFC/AAHD/AAHE/AANJ/AANM/AANN_019), and AANV_019 carries `_value="4"` for its GbR-only variant. **A static `default` attribute is NOT read by this runtime — verified empirically: `default="1"` deploys to the JCR (AEM-confirmed) but the radio still renders unselected.** (First pilot used `default` and failed the visual check; corrected to `_value`.)

**Corpus:** 205 configurator choices in 155 forms. 193 already carry a `_value` and are left alone, 10 in 9 forms open with nothing selected, 2 offer no individual option.
**Detect:** script: `find_formconfig_default.py`  _(affected = a form whose configurator choice carries no `_value` and offers an option naming an individual; a choice that already carries one is skipped as somebody's preselection, and one with no individual option is never forced to an option it does not have. Idempotent.)_
**Fix:** script: `set_formconfig_default.py` _(quote-aware rewrite of that one open tag: drop any stray `default`, insert `_value="<key>"` — the key of the individual option, which differs per form. Which choice and which option is `formconfig_default.py`.)_
**Max changed lines:** 2   _(one attribute inserted on one tag = +1 add; form_diff may count the reflowed tag line as remove+add = 2. Flag anything more.)_
**Runs automatically:** yes
**Origin:** manual (QA/owner directive; engine emits no initial value)
**Tracking:** #74
**Status:** swept 2026-07-23 (29/29 fixed + deployed, PR #75 merged) — mechanism corrected to `_value` (a `default` attribute deploys but does not preselect). **Reworked 2026-09-01:** the rule matched the name `RB_FormularAdressat` and forced the literal key `1`, so it only ever found the German forms — an Italian one calls the same control `RB_Type`, `RB_ClientType`, `RB_GroupTipo` or `RB_Tipo_…`, and there key `1` is *Legal entity*. It now identifies the choice the way PROBLEM-configurator-reset-on-change does (decides ≥2 panels, reviewed label set) and writes the key of the option naming an individual, whatever it is: `1`, `0`, `Individual`, `Private Person`. Scoped to choices with **no** `_value` at all, so a preselection somebody made is never overruled: 9 more forms (10 radios), 193 choices left alone, 2 with no individual option (`[4=GbR]`, `[0=For financial institutions,1=For natural persons]`).

## PROBLEM-save-as-draft-menu — Enable "Save as draft" (menuOptions on the DAM asset)
**Symptom:** QA wants **"Save as draft"** enabled — the complement to removing the Save Progress button ([PROBLEM-nav-save-progress-button](#) / #50) — **#50 has since been reverted** ([PROBLEM-nav-save-progress-required](#) / #135); the menu option stays enabled either way, it is an independent feature: *"hide the button and go to properties → UBS → select Save as draft"* (AABT/AADB/AAEH/AAPC). **Origin: manual (QA/owner directive).**

**Mechanism = `menuOptions` on the DAM asset metadata, NOT the guideContainer.** "Properties → UBS → Save as draft" is the multi-value `menuOptions` property on the form's **DAM asset metadata** node (`…/dam/formsanddocuments/…/AF_<code>/.content.xml`, the `<metadata>` under `dam:AssetContent`). Confirmed from the UBS app: the metadata-schema dialog (`apps/.../adaptiveform/extendedmetadata/.content.xml`) defines the item `text="Save as draft"` → `value="ajila-forms-ubs-menu-option-save"`, and `clientlibs/.../ubs-menu.js` maps that option to `saveAsDraft()`. So "enabled" ⇔ `menuOptions` contains `ajila-forms-ubs-menu-option-save`. (This edits the **DAM entry**, a different ZIP entry than the form `.content.xml` — like [PROBLEM-default-translations](#); the guideContainer autosave config — `autoSaveStrategyType`, `autoSaveInfo` — is uniform across all 286 forms and is NOT the toggle.)

**Extent:** **123 of 286 packages lack it** (no `menuOptions`); **163 already carry it** (`menuOptions="[ajila-forms-ubs-menu-option-save]"` — ground truth). No form uses the other schema option (`…-goto-start`), so `menuOptions` is absent or exactly `[…-save]`. Feedback forms AABT_033/AADB_019/AAEH_019 lack it; **AAPC_033 already has it** (already correct). **Scope note:** whether to enable on all 123 vs a subset is an owner decision (some forms may intentionally omit drafts) — pilot the feedback forms, confirm scope before `--continue`.
**Detect:** script: `find_save_as_draft.py`  _(affected = the DAM asset metadata's `menuOptions` does not contain `ajila-forms-ubs-menu-option-save`; emits per-form current `menuOptions`. Idempotent: a form already carrying it reports 0. `no_asset` = no DAM asset metadata found, not affected.)_
**Fix:** script: `set_save_as_draft.py`  _(on the DAM asset `<metadata>` open tag: insert `menuOptions="[ajila-forms-ubs-menu-option-save]"` if absent, or append the save option inside an existing `[…]`; rewrites the DAM entry in place via the `zip` CLI. Prints `{inner, changed, before, after, ok}`; `ok` = the metadata now lists the save option; idempotent.)_
**Verify:** the fix's own `ok` (metadata now lists the save option) + a pilot **visual check** (the rendered form's menu now offers "Save as draft"). `check_form.py`/`form_diff.py` read only the form `.content.xml`, which this fix never touches, so they can't see the DAM edit — `ok` + the visual check are the gate (as with [PROBLEM-default-translations](#)).
**Max changed lines:** omit — the edit is in the DAM asset entry, invisible to `form_diff` (`.content.xml` only); gate on the fix's `ok`.
**Runs automatically:** yes
**Origin:** manual (QA/owner directive; complement to #50)
**Tracking:** #76
**Status:** swept 2026-07-23 (123/123 fixed + deployed, 0 flagged, PR #77 merged) — piloted (3) then --continue — pilot AABT_033/AADB_019/AAEH_019, then the remaining 120. Every form `fix ok:true` + deploy verified. Cross-checked against the 163 ground-truth forms: `menuOptions` is the SOLE systematic difference and all 163 use exactly `[ajila-forms-ubs-menu-option-save]`, so the fixed forms are now byte-identical in this respect. **"Save as draft" is a runtime eBanking-shell menu item (ubs-menu.js) — it does NOT render in the AEM author preview on ANY form (owner confirmed a known-good form also hides it in preview), so verification is authoring parity, not a preview visual.** Enabled on ALL 123 lacking forms (owner: "if everything matches, continue"). Run via the **staged manual/worktree procedure, not `run_sweep`** (scripts + registry on `sweep/save-as-draft-menu`, master push blocked).

## PROBLEM-formmetadata-step — Every form needs the FormMetadata fragment as its first step
**Symptom:** the form must carry the global **FormMetadata fragment as a wizard step** — a fragment node (not a panel wrapping one) directly under `guideContainer › rootPanel(guideRootPanel) › items`, hidden and DoR-excluded. Canonical shape (reference: AAOV_033): `fragRef="/content/dam/formsanddocuments/afforms_global_fragmentlib/formmetadata"`, `sling:resourceType="ajila-forms-customers/ajila-forms-ubs/components/controls/panel"` (the **UBS custom panel** — flipped from the default AEM panel on 2026-08-24 together with [PROBLEM-panel-type-ubs](#) / #137, which owns every panel including `fragRef` ones), `guideNodeClass="guidePanel"`, `name="FormMetadata"`, `jcr:title="FormMetadata"`, `visible="false"`, `dorExclusion`/`dorExcludeTitle`/`dorExcludeDescription="true"`, `textIsRich="true"`, children `layout` + empty `items`, **first step**. Forms either lack it or have it with the wrong shape, so the rule inserts *or* normalises.
**Detect:** script: `find_formmetadata_step.py`  _(`insert` = no FormMetadata step; `normalise` = present but a canonical attribute is wrong or it is not first. Flags, never auto-fixed: `misplaced`, `duplicate`, `no_items`, `extra_children`. `--form <F>`, `--verbose`. Idempotent.)_
**Fix:** script: `set_formmetadata_step.py`  _(inserts the canonical node as the root `items`' first child, or normalises an existing one in place and moves it to the front. Attributes and children outside the canonical set are kept, not stripped. `--dry-run`. Prints `{inserted, normalised, moved, attrs, kept_extra_attrs, extra_children, skipped, well_formed, canonical_first, ok}`; `ok` = parses AND canonical AND first.)_
**Verify:** the fix's `ok` + a re-scan showing 0 affected + **#18 and #22 staying green** + a **visual check in AEM** (the form still opens on its real first page; no extra step in the wizard).
**Max changed lines:** omit — an insert adds the node, a normalisation edits attributes, a move relocates it; gate on `ok` + the re-scan.
**Manual follow-up:** a `panel_title…` child inside the fragment node is kept and flagged (`extra_children`); removing it is a human decision.
**Runs automatically:** yes
**Origin:** manual (owner directive; reference = AAOV_033)
**Tracking:** #88
**Canonical resourceType flipped 2026-08-24:** `CANONICAL` in `formmetadata_step.py` and `node_xml` in `set_formmetadata_step.py` now emit the **UBS custom panel**, in lockstep with [PROBLEM-panel-type-ubs](#) / #137's rollout — the FormMetadata node is a `fragRef` guidePanel, so that sweep owns its resourceType. The two are only consistent when both land together: this rule reads every form's FormMetadata node, so flipping it while any form still carried the default AEM panel would have turned this rule red on those forms. Landed in the same PR (#138). **Do not change one without the other.**
**Status:** swept 2026-08-04 (286/286 — every form now carries the canonical FormMetadata step as its first step, `canonical: 286`, PR #__)

## PROBLEM-step-title-single — Exactly one `css="stepTitle"` h2 title per wizard step
**Symptom:** each wizard step must render **one** step title. [PROBLEM-step-title-panel](#) / #18 wraps the title and [PROBLEM-jump-to-field-button](#) / #22 puts the jump button on that panel, but neither asserts **how many** nodes carry `css="stepTitle"` (#18 deliberately keys on `headingLevel="2"`), so a step can carry the marker on its wrapper panel, on sibling section panels **and** on a later h2 draw at once → problem with margins.

**Rule (owner, 2026-07-27): the canonical title is the FIRST `headingLevel="2"` draw in document order** in the step, at any depth, marked or not — it is the heading the reader sees first. The fixer puts the marker there (**adding** it if missing, so a marker on a wrong node is *moved*, not just deleted) and strips the `stepTitle` **token** from every other marked node, preserving any other class. No node, title text or other attribute is touched. First-in-order is also what #22 assumes (`title_panel()` takes `h2_draws(step)[0]`), so this **removes** a disagreement between the two sweeps; #18 never reads `css`. Both neighbour detectors report 0 affected after this rule runs.

**Extent (286 packages / 1,069 steps):** `dupe` **329** → **150 forms** (83 of them need the marker moved onto a previously-unmarked first h2) · `ok` 615 · `flag:unmarked` 91 (a title exists but nothing in the step is marked — #18/#22 territory) · `flag:ambiguous` 5 (marked nodes, no h2 at all) · `titleless` 29.

**⚠️ Open decision — do not sweep until it is made.** Of the 527 nodes that lose the marker, 166 are the step panel itself, 150 other panels and 115 later h2 draws (all uncontroversial: a panel renders no heading, a later h2 is not the first). The remaining **96 are `ST_*` textdraws that are NOT headings** (94 with no `headingLevel`, 2 with `headingLevel="3"` — `ST_JointAccount`, `ST_Statement_1..5`, `ST_Sub_Tile_Tax_resid`…). They are *styled* as step titles on purpose or by copy-paste; dropping the class changes how they look, which is a human call. Options: strip them too / restrict the fixer to panels + h2 draws / strip and restyle with a proper sub-heading class.
**Detect:** script: `find_step_title_dupes.py`  _(per-step verdicts above; emits `fix[] {form,step,keep,strip[]}` + `flag[] {…,reason}` as the review surface. `affected` = forms with >=1 `dupe`, so a flag never blocks a form. `--form <F>`, `--verbose`. Idempotent.)_
**Fix:** script: `set_step_title_single.py`  _(byte-offset splices, back-to-front; strips the token — dropping an emptied `css` — and adds it to the first h2 when absent. The insert handles both single-line and multi-line open tags. Prints `{steps_fixed, stripped, marker_moved, flagged, remaining, well_formed, ok}`; `ok` = parses AND 0 `dupe` steps remain.)_  Shared classifier in `step_title_dupes.py` (expat + byte offsets + quote-aware tag scan, so a literal `>` in a rich `_value` can't derail an edit).
**Verify:** the fix's `ok` + a re-scan showing 0 `dupe` + an **AEM visual check on a form from the 83 "marker moved" set** — that group changes *which* heading is styled as the step title, and no script can assert it. The first pilot passed every automated gate while marking the wrong node (see the PR body); that is why this line matters.
**Max changed lines:** omit — a variable number of per-step token edits; gate on `ok` + the re-scan.
**Runs automatically:** yes
**Origin:** manual (owner/QA directive 2026-07-27 — an invariant #18/#22 assume but never enforced)
**Tracking:** #83
**Status:** swept 2026-08-04 (150/150 — 944 steps with exactly one `stepTitle` h2, `dupe: 0`, PR #__)
**Prerequisites for a sweep:** review of the three scripts, a Tracking issue, the `ST_*` decision above, a ~6-form pilot across both panel lineages. Overlaps #18/#22 `_merged.zip`s → sequence the rollout. Tested end-to-end through the intake app on BAGH_019 (2 `dupe` + 2 `ok`, 0 flags; step 1 moves the marker to `TTL_FormConfiguar` and strips it from `PN_FormConfigurator`, `PN_AccountHolder` and `TTL_Kundendaten`).

## PROBLEM-default-label-leak — Wrong-language leak of engine-default labels (FLAG-ONLY report)
**Symptom:** some forms authored a UI label (`jcr:title`/`custom`) as the **non-English translation** of one of the 15 engine-default strings, so its dictionary key is `fd_<foreign text>` — which the form's *other* languages don't translate, leaking the wrong language on those versions. E.g. nav buttons `jcr:title="Avanti"`/`"Indietro"`/`"Anteprima"` (Italian) show Italian on the DE/EN/FR versions; a company field `jcr:title="Firma"` (German "Company") shows German on the EN/IT versions; a panel `jcr:title="Solo per uso interno della banca"` (Italian "For internal bank use only"). **Origin: manual (owner directive).**

**FLAG-ONLY — no automated fix (owner directive 2026-07-23).** This entry only *detects and reports* the leaks; it does **not** re-key or edit forms. An earlier auto-fix (re-key the authored text to the English master so [#72](#)'s dictionary entries render it) was **removed** because it is unsafe: `fd_<master>` is not always correctly translated — some dictionaries carry an untranslated **stub** (`message == English`, which #72's additive fill never overwrites, e.g. `fd_Company`="Company" in a de dict → German would show "Company"), a **form-specific variant** (`fd_Company`="Razón social" in an es dict), or is **missing** (#72's strict-mirror `it-ch` → would fall back to the English master). A pilot hit exactly this on AAGK_019 (German rendered "Company"), so the fixer was scrapped. The correct target text is a **translation decision**, so leaks are handed to the translation team, not auto-changed.

**Extent (report):** **52 of 286 packages** carry ≥1 leaking default-string label. Leaks: Avanti/Indietro/Anteprima (Italian nav) 29 each, Firma→Company 22, Weiter/Zurück/Vorschau (German nav) 2 each, Solo per uso interno della banca 4, Riepilogo delle informazioni del modulo 1. 1 label sits on a path-`translationId` element (listed under `flagged`).
**Detect:** script: `find_default_label_leak.py`  _(flag-only report: per form, the `jcr:title`/`custom` labels that are a non-English default translation rendering wrong in ≥1 locale, with `wrong_in` locales + the target English `master`; plus a `flagged` list for path-translationId labels. Reads `default_label_leak.py` (reverse map of the 15 defaults' translations, unambiguous only).)_
**Fix:** manual — hand the report to the translation team: for each leaking label, add the correct per-locale translation to the form's dictionaries (or re-author the label in the master language once the dictionaries are sound). **No fix script** (removed 2026-07-23).
**Verify:** n/a (no fix). The report itself is the deliverable.
**Runs automatically:** no — the tooling ignores this entry entirely.
**Origin:** manual (owner directive; wrong-language leakage of engine-default strings)
**Tracking:** #78
**Status:** swept

## PROBLEM-signature-panel-ref — Signature name-calc must resolve its repeatable panel
**Symptom:** on the signature page the signer's name should pre-fill from the first/last name entered earlier; on many forms it silently stays empty. The signature fragment resolves the person's repeatable data panel **by name** (`var pnIndividual = PN_AHRP.instanceManager.instances[…].PN_IndividualBasic.PN_Name_Individual;` + `//Expecting Account Holder panel fragment with name PN_AHRP`). These forms carry **no `bindRef`** — the component `name` *is* the binding — so when the host form calls that panel `RCP_AHRP` the identifier is undefined, the calc throws `ReferenceError`, and the field stays blank. **Owner directive 2026-07-27: the fragment's name wins** — rename the host form's panel, accepting that it breaks the repeat-panel `RCP_` convention. That requires the `SIGNATURE_CONTRACT_NAMES` exemption in `find_naming_violations.py`; **without it the guard flags the renamed panel and `apply_sweeps.py` reverts the fix** (verified both ways). **Ordering: the exemption must land with this entry.**

**Two modes, because the fragment is sometimes inlined and sometimes referenced.** (1) *Script* — where the conversion inlined the fragment, the name-calc is visible and states its own contract. (2) *Contract* — where the form only carries a `fragRef`, the calc lives inside the fragment and is absent from the package, so the name comes from **[signature-fragment-contract.md](signature-fragment-contract.md)**: 15 rows **derived from the fragment library sources** by `extract_signature_contract.py`, never hand-maintained, keyed `<library>/<fragment>` because the same fragment name differs per library (`affrg_UBSEuropeSignature1` resolves `PN_EURP` in germany, `PN_AHRP` in italy). AAAZ_019 is this case. **The table never overrides direct evidence:** some forms inlined a fragment version that expected `RCP_` (AAHG_019 reads `RCP_AHRP` and resolves), so a role already satisfied by the form's own script is skipped.

**The fragment's code is authoritative, not its comment.** 5 of the 15 fragments name a different panel in their `//Expecting …` comment than the one their code resolves (germany/`affrg_ARSignature1` resolves `PN_ARP`, comment says `PN_LGA`), so the comment is used only as a scope signal. `affrg_SignatureGeneric1` resolves two panels and is excluded from the table — no single answer, so no guess.

**Only the root identifier is checked.** `.instanceManager` exists only on a repeatable panel, so that name must be in this form. The rest of the path (`…PN_Name_Individual.TXT_LastName`) lives inside the fragment, is not shipped in the package, and is never asserted — which is why the pilot needs a human visual check. Scope is limited to the name-retrieval calc: checking every `X.instanceManager` yields **635 false positives across 186 forms** (scripts legitimately drive panels inside fragments). Inferring the target from the only person-details fragment panel was also rejected — it proposed `RCP_ARP`→`PN_AHRP` on AABV_019, i.e. the wrong person's name.

**Extent: 58 of 286 packages actionable — 82 renames** (`RCP_AHRP`→`PN_AHRP` ×57, `RCP_LGA`→`PN_LGA` ×24, `RCP_ARP`→`PN_ARP` ×1). Verified by simulating the whole sweep: all 58 report `ok`, 82 renames applied, and a re-scan then reports **0 affected** — so the guard returns to green on its own, with no per-form accept-list to maintain.

**A further 109 findings across 84 forms are NOT auto-fixable and are reported under `flagged`, never as affected.** 12 are provably impossible (the form has no repeatable panel at all, so nothing `.instanceManager` could bind to); the rest **need human revision and a fix may well exist** — repeatable panels are present but none matches the wanted role token, and the detector's person-details heuristic looks for a `fragRef`, so it misses panels with inlined content (e.g. AAAB_019 carries `RCP_RCP_StpLastNameFirstName`, plausibly the right target). They are a review queue (`--flagged`), not failures, and not suppressed data: the same split as [PROBLEM-default-label-leak](#), which is report-only for exactly this reason.
**Detect:** script: `find_signature_panel_ref.py`  _(per unresolved identifier a verdict: `rename` = exactly one repeatable panel matches by **role token** (name minus prefix: `RCP_AHRP` → `AHRP` ← `PN_AHRP`) and its name is unique; else `flag:no-target` / `ambiguous-target` / `shared-name` / `unprefixed-identifier`. **Affected = ≥1 `rename`**, i.e. exactly what the Fix repairs — so the sweep has no no-ops and the guard clears itself; non-actionable findings go to `flagged`. Idempotent: after the fix the identifier resolves and the finding disappears.)_
**Fix:** script: `set_signature_panel_ref.py`  _(renames the panel and rewrites every reference, attribute-scoped like `set_naming_prefix.py`: `name=` plus `fd:*` code only, never `jcr:title`/`_value`/`options`/`fd:translationIds`/`bindRef`. Applies only `rename` verdicts; the rest are listed under `skipped`. Prints `{inner, renamed, skipped, remaining, ok}`; a flag-only form reports `ok:true, n_renamed:0` — not a failure.)_
**Verify:** the fix's `ok` + a re-scan showing 0 actionable + `find_naming_violations.py` staying green (the exemption's job) + a pilot **visual check in AEM: the signature page pre-fills the name.** That check is the real gate — a resolving identifier proves the reference is valid, not that the fragment's inner path returns data.
**Max changed lines:** omit — one rename touches the definition plus every reference, so the count scales with reference density (AAAZ_019: 8 lines for 2 renames). Gate on the fix's `ok` + the re-scan.
**Runs automatically:** yes
**Origin:** manual (owner directive — the fragment's hardcoded name wins over the `RCP_` convention)
**Tracking:** #80
**Status:** swept 2026-08-04 (58/58 — 0 affected; the 109 `flag:no-target` are reported, not fixed, PR #__)
**Superseded in substance by PROBLEM-fragment-library-consolidation (2026-08-21), still enrolled.** This rule exists because a MARKET signature fragment hardcodes the name of the data panel it reads (`PN_AHRP.instanceManager…`), so the host panel had to be renamed to match. The consolidation replaces every one of those fragments with `ubs/affrg_SignatureGeneric1`, whose own calc ships `enabled: false` and whose fill the HOST authors — PROBLEM-signature-name-fill writes it, from the panel names the UBS table dictates. Nothing is left to satisfy. **Measured on 2026-08-21: 0 actionable rows on the pristine corpus and 0 on the swept one**, and the review queue shrinks as the consolidation runs (171 `flag:no-target` → 135), so the two rules cannot fight over a name. Kept enrolled because 108 forms still reference the market libraries; **retire it once the consolidation rollout empties them** — but not `data/signature_contract.json`, which `signature_name_fill.py` reads for each signature fragment's name-field paths.
_Enrolment: `Runs automatically: yes` — the rollout is done (58/58) and the detector reports 0 actionable rows, so the guard is green. The old test (the word "swept" on line 1 of `Status:`) survives only as the fallback for entries that carry no `Runs automatically:` field. Validated on AAAZ_019 (`RCP_AHRP`→`PN_AHRP`, `RCP_LGA`→`PN_LGA`; `ok:true`, 8 changed lines, no leftover references, display attributes byte-identical, re-detect clean, guard green) and on a synthetic Mode-1 form. **Rollout:** `run_sweep.py --slug PROBLEM-signature-panel-ref --only AAAZ_019` → deploy → visual check → `--continue` for the remaining 57 → change line 1 to `swept <date> (58/58 …)`, which also switches the rule on in the intake app. Re-run `extract_signature_contract.py` whenever the fragment library changes; the affected set moves with the table._

## PROBLEM-banking-relationship-margin — Banking-relationship wrapper panel must carry `css="ubs-margin-20"`
**Symptom:** the panel **containing** the banking-relationship fragment — the `PN_BR` wrapper from [PROBLEM-banking-relationship-wrap](#) / #29 — must carry the layout class **`css="ubs-margin-20"`** so the banking block gets the standard vertical spacing. `set_banking_wrap.py` synthesises the wrapper with no `css` at all, so every wrapper it created is affected. Target is the **wrapper**, not the `fragRef` panel #10 owns (owner-confirmed 2026-07-27).
**Extent:** **259 of 286 packages** carry a `PN_BR`; **none** has the class (`already_ok: 0`). The other 27 have no wrapper (later-page banking / no banking) and are out of scope — a form with no `PN_BR` reports clean here.
**Depends on #29 — run it first.** A form whose first-page banking panel is unwrapped reports clean here, but #29 reports it **red**, so it can never look all-green; and `apply_sweeps.py`'s fixpoint loop wraps (#29 is earlier in this file) then adds the class in the same pass. Deliberately left implicit rather than adding a script that detects on the fragment.
**Detect:** script: `find_missing_attr_on.py --select 'name="PN_BR"' --require-attr css=ubs-margin-20`  _(affected = a `PN_BR` open tag lacking the class; the selector includes the closing quote so `PN_BRxxx` can't match. **Single-quoted since 2026-08-26**, when `check_regressions` moved to `shlex.split` like `run_sweep` — before that the unquoted form worked in CI but `run_sweep` stripped it to `name=PN_BR` and detected nothing. 276 `PN_BR` tags selected, all with the class.)_
**Fix:** script: `set_attr_on.py --select 'name="PN_BR"' --set-attr css=ubs-margin-20`  _(**quotes required** here: the Fix verb is `shlex.split()`. Note `set_attr_on.py` replaces a `css` value wholesale — it does not merge class tokens. Harmless today since no wrapper has any `css`, but re-check the detector's list before a rerun.)_
**Verify:** the fix's `all_ok` + a re-scan showing 0 affected + a pilot **visual check** (banking block has the standard margin, #29's summary/DoR exclusions still hold). A corpus-wide `must:` on the class is too weak — it occurs on other panels.
**Max changed lines:** 2   _(attribute insert = +1; 2 if an existing `css` value is replaced)_
**Runs automatically:** yes
**Origin:** manual (owner directive 2026-07-27, as an addition to #10)
**Tracking:** #82
**Status:** swept 2026-08-04 (259/259 — every `PN_BR` wrapper now carries `css="ubs-margin-20"`, `already_ok: 259`, PR #__)
**Piloted** on BAGH_019 locally: `matched:1, added:1, ok:true`, 2 diff lines, package otherwise byte-identical, re-scan clean, second pass a no-op. That pilot also turned up the `set_attr_on.py` single-line-tag bug and the Detect-quoting trap, both fixed alongside this entry — the PR body has the full write-up.

## PROBLEM-toolbar-nav-handler — Submit and Back must run the UBS navigation helpers
**Symptom:** the wizard toolbar's **Submit** button submits through the rule editor's built-in **Submit Form** action, bypassing the UBS validation/error path, and the **Back** button has no handler at all. UBS directive (2026-07-28, definitive, all forms): both must call the UBS navigation helpers, as the reference form **AAOV** (italy) already does. Target shape per button — an empty `<fd:rules>` plus an `<fd:scripts>` child whose `fd:click` holds one SCRIPTMODEL:
`toolbar.submit` → `window.forms.ubs.navigation.submit(submitErrorMessage);` · `toolbar.previtemnav` → `window.forms.ubs.navigation.previousStep(this);`

**`nextitemnav` is deliberately OUT OF SCOPE.** 285 of 286 forms run `window.com.ajila.forms.ubs.navigation.nextStep(this)` — the superseded namespace — and UBS did not ask for it. Including it "for consistency" would rewrite the whole corpus for no requested benefit, so the corpus stays intentionally mixed: Next on the old namespace, Submit/Back on the new. Raise it with UBS separately if that matters.

**Four states occur, each a different edit.** `replace-rule` **260** — a visual rule (`SUBMIT_FORM`) in `fd:rules`, no script: the rule is dropped and the script added. `add-node` **282** — the Back button element is *self-closing*, so it is opened and given an `<fd:scripts>` child. `replace-script` **27** — a non-canonical payload (25 submit + 2 back on the old namespace). `ok` **1**. `skip:no-button` **2** — no Back button at all; never fabricated, a single-step form legitimately has none. `fd:navigationChange`, present on all 286 submits, is never touched.

**Conformance is an exact payload match**, so all forms converge on one byte-identical value — which also normalises a stale editor `field` path (2 forms carry `guide.Preview.toolbar.previtemnav`). Wholesale replacement is safe because every existing payload holds exactly **one** script, verified corpus-wide; a payload with more than one is `flag:multiple-scripts` and left alone, as is any click script we did not author (`flag:unexpected-script`). Both are 0 today.

**Extent: 286 of 286 packages, 569 edits.** Simulated end to end: 286/286 `ok`, re-scan reports **0 affected** (570 `ok`, 2 `skip`), `fd:navigationChange` intact on all 286, 0 `SUBMIT_FORM` rules left, every display attribute (`jcr:title`/`_value`/`options`/`fd:translationIds`/`bindRef`/`css`) byte-identical, and `find_naming_violations.py` still 0 affected. The generated payload is **byte-identical to AAOV's**, and AAOV itself classifies as `ok` on both buttons — the reference validates the rule.
**Detect:** script: `find_toolbar_nav_handler.py`  _(per button a verdict: `ok` = `fd:click` equals the canonical payload; else `replace-rule` / `replace-script` / `add-node` / `add-attr`, or `flag:unexpected-script` / `flag:multiple-scripts` / `skip:no-button`. Affected = ≥1 actionable verdict, i.e. exactly what the Fix repairs, so the guard clears itself. Idempotent.)_
**Fix:** script: `set_toolbar_nav_handler.py`  _(quote-aware open-tag scan — the JSON and rich-text values contain literal `>` — with byte-offset splices applied back-to-front. Drops the `fd:rules` visual rule, writes the canonical `fd:click`, and opens a self-closing button to add the child node, deriving indentation from the form itself. Prints `{inner, applied, skipped, remaining, ok}`; `ok` = well-formed AND nothing actionable left.)_
**Verify:** the fix's `ok` + a re-scan showing 0 affected + `fd:navigationChange` still present + a pilot **functional check in AEM: actually submit a form and confirm it arrives, and click Back through the wizard.** This replaces the native submit action with a JS helper on 260 forms — if the helper does not submit, those forms stop submitting. A visual check is not enough; the submission must be exercised.
**Max changed lines:** 8   _(both buttons = 2 removed + 6 added; 281 forms hit exactly 8, the rest 2–6. Flag anything more.)_
**Runs automatically:** yes
**Origin:** manual (UBS directive 2026-07-28; reference form AAOV)
**Tracking:** #87
**Status:** swept 2026-08-04 (286/286 — 570 buttons ok, 2 `skip:no-button`, PR #__)

## PROBLEM-datepicker-current-date-default — Uncheck "Set current date as default value" on date pickers
**Symptom:** date pickers carry `defaultToCurrentDate="true"`, so the field opens pre-filled with today's date. UBS wants it unchecked: the user must enter the date deliberately, and a pre-filled today silently becomes wrong data on any form not submitted the same day. **Origin: manual (UBS directive).**

**The unchecked state is the attribute being ABSENT, not `false`.** Across the corpus `defaultToCurrentDate` occurs **only** with the value `true` — never `false` — so the 436 date pickers that do not pre-fill simply lack it, and removing the attribute reproduces exactly the shape the engine emits when the box is unchecked. Same mechanical shape as [PROBLEM-dor-exclude-hidden](#), which is why this entry needs **no new code**: it reuses the generic `find_affected.py --grep` detector and `remove_attr.py` fixer.

**Scoped by construction.** The attribute appears on **nothing but date pickers** (both `sling:resourceType` variants — `datepicker` 509 nodes and `guidedatepicker` 43), so `--attr defaultToCurrentDate` cannot touch another component type. No selector is needed.

**Extent: 71 of 286 packages, 116 attributes.** Both entities (64 nodes on `_019`, 52 on `_033`). Most forms carry one; a few carry several (AAUS_033 and BBES_033 have 8 each). Simulated end to end: 71/71 forms fixed, 116 attributes removed, 0 failures, every form still well-formed and passing the `must-not`, and a re-scan reports **0 affected**.
**Detect:** grep: `defaultToCurrentDate="true"`
**Fix:** script: `remove_attr.py --attr defaultToCurrentDate`
**Verify:** must-not: `defaultToCurrentDate=` _(the fixer prints no `ok` key, so `run_sweep` gates on this regex — same as PROBLEM-dor-exclude-hidden. Plus a pilot **visual check in AEM: open a step with a date picker and confirm the field is empty, not today's date.**)_
**Max changed lines:** 8   _(one line removed per attribute; 50 forms change 1 line, and AAUS_033/BBES_033 change 8. Flag anything more.)_
**Runs automatically:** yes
**Origin:** manual (UBS directive — a pre-filled current date becomes wrong data)
**Tracking:** _pending — create the issue and put the number here before the first `run_sweep`._
**Status:** swept 2026-08-04 (71/71 — `defaultToCurrentDate` gone from the corpus, PR #__)
_No new code: reuses `find_affected.py --grep` and `remove_attr.py --attr`, per the registry's "no code only if it reuses an existing Detect/Fix script" rule. Enrolment: `Runs automatically: yes` — the rollout is complete (71/71 deployed) and the detector reports 0 affected, so the guard stays green._

---

## PROBLEM-repeating-panel — one archetype for every repeating panel
**Symptom:** repeating panels were hand-built one at a time and **none** of the 897 in 224 of the 286 packages matches the current archetype. Their buttons carry stacks of patches (268 buttons with 2–6 click scripts, 175 with disabled ones) that hand-juggle each instance's Remove visibility, show and hide numbered title draws, and re-show themselves — and **10 identifiers those scripts drive do not exist in their form** (75 references, none declared anywhere), so the handler throws on its first line and adds nothing: some repeatables simply do not work in production. On top of that, 221 panels carry `min/max` nothing can ever add an instance to, 42 containers were marked repeating besides the panel they wrap (which would number every row twice), and `addButton`/`removeButton` are missing on all but 15, so the client library cannot relabel a reloaded draft and announces every row as the first. Full spec: project doc `claude/PROBLEM-repeating-panel-spec.md`.
**A panel nothing can add is still a repeating panel (owner directive 2026-09-02), and `skip` is gone.** The verdict used to leave a panel alone when no Add button, no script and no `bindRef` could ever give it a second instance — 177 panels, among them every UBS Europe and legal-guardian signature block. That reads a fact about the FORM as a fact about the panel: it carries occurrence bounds, so it repeats, and it needs the title, the numbering and the properties like any other. Where a repeatable elsewhere later drives it, it is already in the right shape; where nothing ever does, a repeatable that stays at its minimum costs nothing. Its buttons follow from the same reading and needed no new code: with no Add there is no Remove either, which is what `create_remove_button` and `delete_remove_buttons` already said. **What moves: 177 panels enter the plan** — 88 `title-cleared`, 41 `fix`, 18 `promoted-fragment`, 22 `manual-title`, 8 already `ok` — and 128 forms are affected (was 83).
**A panel the form hides is never asked for a title.** 87 of the 177 are the internal-bank-use blocks (`visible="{Boolean}false"`, PROBLEM-internal-bank-use-pdf-only owns them): they render no heading, so there is no title to derive and none for a person to write, and asking would have put a `(Repeatable name)` placeholder and a review flag on all 87. They take the properties and lose the numbering — the `title-cleared` treatment, for the structural reason rather than a marker.
**A twin is never invented for a name two panels share, but a twin lost to a rename is still recovered (2026-09-02).** The signature twin is found from three sources, and two of them stop making sense once two parties of a class keep the same panel name (PROBLEM-fragment-library-consolidation, same date): the fragment table names ONE `signaturePanel` per entry, so it says nothing when the form holds several panels of that fragment, and a signature named after a data panel cannot say which of two same-named panels it belongs to. Reported on AABF_019: the minor's Add was adding an instance of the CLIENT's signature block. **Both routes are believed only where the panel shows evidence of a twin it has lost** — its own Add script asks for a panel the form no longer has, which is what a rename elsewhere leaves behind, and is exactly the client's Add after the consolidation renamed `PN_SignatureIndividual` to `PN_SGN_CPGRP`. The minor's Add asks for nothing, so nothing is invented for it; the client's twin comes back. (Dropping the two routes outright was the first attempt and it cost the client its own signature — the pristine form does wire it, the rename is what hid the wiring.) Corpus effect: **4 rows in 3 forms**, every one a fix — AAAZ_019 and AADZ_033 stop claiming a `PN_SGN_CPGRP` that belongs to the other party, and AATH_033's two `PN_AHGRP` panels stop claiming **both** signatures each.
**Two things in the title carry-over had to be fixed for this to converge** (both found by the corpus idempotence check, both pre-existing but only reachable now): the placeholder is not a decision, so it is no longer carried to the node a promote hands the role to (AAUI_033 `RCP_ResponsiblePerson_Sign` wrote `(Repeatable name)` where `Signature` was there to be read); and a disputed name is compared by the title alone, because two panels of one family reading `Client` from different evidence — the wrapper from the text inside, the panel from its own title — is not a disagreement (AAGZ_033).
**Detect:** script: `find_repeating_panel.py`  _(per panel one of nine verdicts, each with the `action` Intake prints after the arrow: `ok` 0 · `fix` 637 → apply archetype · `manual-title` 27 → set title manually · `title-cleared` → keep title empty · `skip` 182 → skip — never repeats · `bounds-removed` 42 → remove spurious bounds · `promoted-fragment` 6 → promote fragment · `duplicate-panel-name` 0 → rename duplicate panel · `shared-add-button` 2 → split shared Add button · `fragment-panel-name` 1 → rename the fragment panel `<current>` to `<required>`, plus its signature twin when that came out misnamed too (the Add drives both, so renaming only one leaves it broken); the twin is only ever named alongside a data panel already known to be misnamed, never on its own. The verdict is **structural** — every predicate of the archetype re-checked against the tree — not a marker, so a swapped fragment is still noticed and custom logic in its own rule document does not push a panel out of `ok`. `ok`, `title-cleared` and `skip` are not affected; everything else is, **including a `manual-title` already carrying its placeholder**. Idempotent.)_
**The title is a dictionary key, and two things used to stop it being one.** (1) The sweep keyed a title only when it *was* the wording table's English master, so a subject read off an Add button the table does not know — `Ording customer`, AABF_019 — stayed English in every language while its own button read `Auftraggeber hinzufügen`. It now takes the wording from the form's own dictionary, two ways: the dictionary translates the title itself, **matched ignoring case** — the case the sweep writes and the case the form keys need not agree, and `Ordering customer` beside a keyed `Ordering Customer` heading is the whole bug — or it translates the Add button the subject was read off, and the label is that subject inside a pattern this repo owns, so un-applying it gives `Auftraggeber` (`own_wording` and `subject_from_label`, the inverse of `phrase`). Entries are written for **every** title the rule decides, not only the ones a run writes, so a form swept before the entry existed heals on the next pass; an entry that repeats its own master is not written. Additive and idempotent. Corpus 2026-09-02: 385 title/language pairs are unkeyed, 36 of them in 19 forms become translatable. (2) Titles decided on the untouched form were carried through the phases **keyed by panel name**, and names repeat here — the fragment library hands `affrg_PartnertoPartnerGeneric1`, `affrg_PowerofAttorneyGeneric1` and `affrg_BeneficialOwnerGenericBasic1` the same canonical panel `PN_AHGRP`. Renaming a panel to what its fragment demands (the `fragment-panel-name` verdict, 30 panels, 29 of them `PN_AHGRP_AR`) then dropped it onto another panel's key and it inherited that panel's title: the authorized representative came out titled `Legal guardian` (AAAZ/AAGB_019) or `Ording customer` (AABF_019). A disputed name is now dropped rather than decided by whichever panel came first, and both panels re-read their own tree — where the promotion has already carried the title down. One form in the committed corpus is affected without a rename (AAKP_033, two panels named `PN_SGN_CPGRP_ADR`: the second now reads `UBS Europe SE` instead of `Cliente`).
**Fix:** script: `set_repeating_panel.py`  _(seven phases, re-parsing between each: bounds → numbered-title families → wrap bare fragment panels in a UBS panel → buttons → properties → `layout` node → rules. Rule documents carry `_archetype: "repeating-panel"` **and** open with a canonical comment, and either identifies the document as the archetype's own. **One archetype id, both shapes** — the promoted-fragment treatment writes different content, not a different archetype, and giving it an id of its own made every promoted panel carry two of everything, each pass preserving the other's document as somebody's custom logic — which is what lets a second pass rewrite only its own document and preserve anyone else's. Two markers because the field alone did not survive contact: a rule edited in AEM came back without it, the button then read as never-swept, and the next run wiped the custom rules beside it. Prints `{panels, wrapped, removes_created, removes_deleted, titles_deleted, fragments_promoted, layouts_repaired, scripts_wiped, i18n_added, no_layout, remaining, well_formed, bad_rules, ok}`. Simulated over all 304 packages: **304/304 `ok`, second pass byte-identical, zero malformed rule values, zero panels left without a fluid `layout` node**; a re-scan of the output reports 0 `fix`, 0 `bounds-removed`, 0 `promoted-fragment` and only the 25 panels awaiting a person.)_
**Verify:** the fix's own `ok` + a re-scan reporting only `manual-title`/`shared-add-button` rows + a pilot **visual check in AEM: Add numbers the new row where the deleted draws were, Remove shows only on the last row and vanishes at the minimum, Add hides at `maxOccur` and returns after a Remove, a reloaded draft announces its rows 1..n, and no duplicated heading is left above the numbering.**
**Max changed lines:** _(none — a structural rewrite legitimately changes hundreds of lines in one form; bounded by the fix's `ok` and the delta gate instead.)_
**A Remove button one level down is moved up** to be the repeating panel's first child (115 of them, in 80 forms). Its rules reach the panel as `this.parent`, which inside a wrapper panel resolves to that wrapper instead — so the element moves rather than the rules being rewritten around it.
**Ordering:** runs **before** PROBLEM-repeatable-add-label, which reads the panel titles this sweep writes. A panel left without a title yields a bare `Add` there until somebody names it.
**Ordering (position in this file, 2026-08-19):** placed straight after PROBLEM-datepicker-current-date-default, so it runs **before** PROBLEM-configurator-reset-on-change, PROBLEM-repeatable-add-label, PROBLEM-signature-name-fill, PROBLEM-signature-title and PROBLEM-fragment-library-consolidation — every rule that either reads what this one writes or is gated on its marker. The order in this file *is* the order `apply_sweeps.py` applies the fixers in within one pass. PROBLEM-repeatable-add-label was moved down with it (to just after PROBLEM-configurator-reset-on-change) so its own "after PROBLEM-repeating-panel" constraint and configurator-reset's "before PROBLEM-repeatable-add-label" constraint both still hold.
**Ordering (the round trip with PROBLEM-fragment-library-consolidation):** this sweep runs **twice** on a form that holds a market fragment inside a repeatable panel, and that is by design, not a defect. Pass 1 stamps the panel, which is what opens the other rule's archetype gate; that rule then swaps `afforms_germany_all` / `afforms_italy_all` for the shared library. Pass 2 this sweep re-reads the tree — its verdict is **structural**, never a marker — finds the fragment it now holds carries `"promote": true` in `data/repeating_fragments.json`, and redoes its own work as the promoted-fragment treatment. `apply_sweeps.py` resolves the whole thing inside a single invocation because it loops to a fixpoint; nobody has to run it twice. Measured 2026-08-19 on AAAZ_019 (germany, two references to the same fragment under different panels): 3 passes, `applied` = both rules, then a 4th invocation byte-identical. The market libraries hold **no** `promote: true` entry and the shared ones hold 12, which is what makes the treatment change only *after* the swap.
**A title somebody wrote on the panel stands as written.** It is not run through the wording table, which would replace `Coniuge/convivente` with the concept's English master, and not normalised, which would drop the slash. Only a derived title is written as the master the dictionary keys — and only a title that *is* that master is keyed, in either sweep.
**The title is decided once, on the untouched form,** and carried through the phases: the fixer deletes the numbered-title draws before it writes the title, so a panel re-classified afterwards derived its title from whatever evidence was left — a sub-heading inside it, or the fragment's own name on a signature twin.
**A promotion carries the wrapper's title down with the bounds (2026-08-20).**
`phase_bounds` hands the repeating role to the self-repeating fragment inside a wrapper and strips
the wrapper's `jcr:title` / `accessibilityLabel` / `ajilaPanelSubject` so the heading is not drawn
twice — but it gave the fragment only `minOccur`/`maxOccur`, so the title was destroyed rather than
moved. `decided_subjects` carries a title across the phases of ONE run; the chain converges over
several `apply_sweeps` passes, and by the next pass the evidence is gone — the wrapper has no title
any more, the promoted panel never had one, and `resolve_subject` falls through to
`DEFAULT_SUBJECT`. Symptom on AAOV_033, reported by Marco: the legal representative's signature
panel rendered as **`(Repeatable name)`** while the partner panel beside it correctly said *"Legal
representative"* — the same string, on the wrapper, one pass earlier. *"Esto podría ser automatizado,
no? Tenemos toda la información necesaria para adivinar que es de un Legal Representative y además
de esta forma añadir también todas las traducciones."* Fixed by moving the three attributes with the
bounds, and only where the fragment has no title of its own and the wrapper's is not the placeholder;
the dissolve branch re-applies them to the chunk it re-emits, since that branch reads the fragment
back from the original bytes and would otherwise drop the open-tag rewrite. Carrying the STRING is
what carries the translations: the title is a dictionary key and *"Legal representative"* is one the
corpus already has entries for in every language, where a title derived from the partner class would
have needed new entries for a string nobody had translated.
**Where the title comes from,** best evidence first: the family of numbered draws · a title already written on the panel · the Add button's label, as a concept or with the verb stripped · the first thing inside the panel when it is a title rather than an input · the first drawn text inside · the heading above · a word shared by every field label · the container's name. Each source was scored against the two subjects a form states outright — the numbered draws and a hand-written title — with that evidence hidden from the source under test: the button's label is right 88% / 87% of the time, the text inside 76% / 0%, and the three that were dropped (the panel's own name 29% / 21%, the heading above the wrapper 27% / 38%, drawn text in the wrapper 7% / 5%) were wrong two times out of three. The order picks the right subject on 73% / 41% of the panels that state one, against 49% / 25% for the order it replaces; the price is 27 panels with no title instead of 19, and a wrong title is worse than none now that it becomes the row heading, the Add button's label and the DoR heading at once.
**`jumpToFieldButtonVisible` is not this rule's to write (2026-08-21).** It was in `TARGET_ATTRS`, so
every repeatable this rule normalised got an edit button in the summary — and because this rule runs
after [PROBLEM-jump-to-field-button](#) / #22, which owns that attribute and deliberately keeps it off
non-step panels, the stamp always won. 612 repeatables and 128 generic signature panels carried one
after a sweep against 27 and 4 in the pristine corpus. Removed from `TARGET_ATTRS`, which still holds — this rule does not write it.
**`DROP_ATTRS_FRAGMENT` is empty since 2026-08-24:** it used to delete the attribute from a repeatable
carrying a `fragRef`, and under the new placement directive that repeatable is exactly where the
button belongs, so the two rules would have added and deleted it for ever. This rule neither writes
nor removes it anywhere; `phase_bounds` still drops it from a panel it takes the BOUNDS off, which is
right — that panel has stopped repeating.

**A twin the Add drives must be resolved to the panel that actually repeats (2026-08-24).** The
twin list was copied out of the form's *old* click text and checked only against "does a node of
that name exist". Three things then went wrong at once, and all three were silent:

- **The name stays on the wrapper.** `phase_wrap` gives a bare fragment panel a UBS panel and
  `phase_bounds` promotes the bounds down onto the fragment, so the panel the old script named is
  the wrapper and the repeatable is its child. The generated click kept naming the wrapper:
  `addInstance` on a panel with no `instanceManager` throws on the handler's first line and adds
  nothing — the exact defect in the Symptom above, reintroduced by the fix for it. Measured over
  nine swept packages: **15 dead calls in five forms** (AAAN_019, AAGZ_033, AAOR_033, AARG_033,
  AAUQ_033). `resolve_twin` now descends to whatever carries the bounds, and only when **exactly
  one** thing under the panel repeats — two would be a guess, and a guess adds a row to the wrong
  party's block.
- **`repeating_fragments.json` names one `signaturePanel` per entry**, so only one twin per data
  panel could ever be recovered. It is read as a list now (a string, a list and a missing key all
  read the same way), because a form where the same party signs twice needs two and the data file
  is no reason to forbid it.
- **A twin another rule renamed was dropped without a word.** `PN_LRP_Sign` became `PN_Sign_AHG`
  when PROBLEM-fragment-library-consolidation renamed it; the old click still said `PN_LRP_Sign`,
  which no longer existed, so AARG_033's legal-representative Add drove **no** signature at all.
  Nothing in the old text can recover that, so a third source was added: `twins_by_name`, a panel
  whose own name states this data panel (`PN_SGN_CPGRP`, `PN_Sign_AHGRP`, either with a role
  suffix). That is the route that survives a rename, and it is why the naming convention was
  imposed in the first place.

A name **two nodes share is left exactly as it was**: AEM resolves such a call to whichever node it
finds first, which is a real defect, but a different one (`flag:shared-name`, PROBLEM-signature-
panel-ref) and not this rule's to decide. Refusing it here removed the Add's only signature call
from **55 panels in 40 forms** — the row would simply stop appearing. Measured, reverted, and the
reason recorded so nobody does it again.

**New verdict `bounds-missing` — the mirror of `bounds-removed`.** A signature block the form's own
Add calls `PN_LRP_Sign.instanceManager.addInstance()` on **while carrying no occurrence bounds at
all**. The call throws today (a panel has an `instanceManager` only when it repeats), and the
breakage propagates: with no bounds the panel reads as single-instance, PROBLEM-fragment-library-
consolidation gives it the *single* generic name (`PN_Sign_AHG`, not `PN_Sign_AHGRP`), nothing can
then pair it with its data panel, and PROBLEM-signature-name-fill writes it no auto-fill either.
The bounds are copied from the data panel that drives it — the two repeat in step by construction,
which is the whole reason one Add drives both — and only when the form's OWN scripts name it, it is
unique, it carries a `fragRef`, and nothing under it already repeats. **3 panels in 2 forms**
(AARG_033 ×2, ADEC_019 ×1). Fixer counter: `bounds_added`.

**Equal bounds are a decision, not a dead panel (2026-08-24).** `classify` skipped any panel
nothing could add an instance to — no Add button, no script naming it, no `bindRef` — and read the
occurrence bounds as no evidence at all. That is what the signature directive collides with: a form
with one signature gets a Content Panel at `min=max=1` so that THIS rule supplies its title, and the
panel went straight to `skip` with no title, no `dorFieldStyling` and no `headingLevel`. Verified on a
real form before changing anything: AAPD_033's `affrg_italy_Client_Signature` patched to
`min=max=1` came out `skip`. Now `minOccur == maxOccur` counts as intent. Corpus: 32 panels have equal
bounds and **24 of them are skipped today** — nearly all signature blocks at `min=max=2`
(`PN_Sign_LG`, `PN_UBSEurope_Sign`, `PN_UbsEuropeSE`, `RCP_SignatureIndividual`), the same shape one
step along. They get a title and the archetype's properties from now on. The button rules need no
change at either bound: `BT_Add` is `length < maxOccur` and `BT_Remove` needs `length > minOccur`, so
both hide themselves. **Open for a person:** at `min=max=1` AEM draws no "1." beside the title
(confirmed by Marco); at `min=max=2` the numbering presumably does show, and whether two numbered
signature slots read correctly on screen and in the DoR is a visual check nobody has made.

**Corpus fixed: NO.** Simulated over all 321 packages and verified idempotent, but only two pilot
forms were ever committed and the pilot deploy + visual review in AEM are still pending. **128 forms
violate it today** (the 2026-09-02 widening is most of them) against a Skip line naming one. `Runs automatically: yes` is what ENROLS the rule — `apply_sweeps`, `check_regressions` and the intake app all read that one field — and it says nothing about whether the corpus has been swept. Do not read it as done.
**Runs automatically:** no
**Origin:** manual (UBS directive — repeating panels must all behave the same way)
**Tracking:** #TBD
**Status:** **scope widened 2026-09-02 — a panel with no Add is normalised too; 128 forms to re-sweep.** Verdicts on the current corpus: 622 `ok`, 72 `fix`, 88 `title-cleared`, 63 `promoted-fragment`, 53 `manual-title`, 30 `fragment-panel-name`, 2 `bounds-removed`, 2 `shared-add-button`. Simulated over all 321 packages: 154 change, every result well-formed, **second pass byte-identical**, no form loses an input; `blueprint:validate_aem_package_from_file` on AABF_019 8 → 7, no new category (the newly stamped signature block joins the twins the validator already reports for having no Add or Remove of their own, which is by design). Previously: swept 2026-08-18 — simulated 304/304 `ok`, second pass byte-identical; pilots AACQ_019 26→18 (no new) and AAOS_033 18→17 (2 resolved, **1 new and accepted** — see below), **pilot deploy and visual review still pending**
_Promotion moves four things, not one. The bounds go down onto the fragment, **the Add button comes down with them** (left outside it is a level too high — `this.parent.<fragment>` stops resolving, nothing is found that adds an instance, and the next pass reads the promoted panel as `skip` and leaves it with no title, no numbering and no rules), the old repeatable's Remove button goes, since once the bounds have moved no later phase produces a row for that panel, and **the panel that was repeating gives up the numbering, the title and the heading levels** — kept, it renders a second numbered heading above the row that now draws its own. The container is dissolved altogether when it holds nothing but the fragment and its buttons **and no script names it**; the corpus's one promotable wrapper is named by eight, so it stays as the container, which is a perfectly good shape. The Add button lands **after** the row. Numbered titles are deleted **before** the bounds move, or the family is stranded in what is by then a plain container._
_

## PROBLEM-visible-on-init — Visibility rules must also run on Initialize
**Symptom:** a visibility rule only fires when a value it depends on **changes**. On a freshly opened form — or one reloaded from a draft — nothing has changed yet, so the rule never runs and the component keeps whatever `visible` state the author left on the node: panels that should be hidden are showing, panels that should be shown are missing, until the user happens to touch the controlling field. Every visibility rule must therefore also run at **Initialize**. **Origin: manual (UBS directive 2026-08-06).**

**A verbatim copy would be a no-op — the *result* has to become an assignment.** A visibility rule communicates through its evaluated result (`… true; } else { … false; }`); an Initialize rule's result is discarded. So the copy rewrites the result into `this.visible = …`. That is the corpus's own convention, not an inference: **441 Initialize scripts already use `this.visible`**, and so do 15 visibility rules. Everything else in the script — notably the `window.forms.ubs.showAFShowDor(this)` / `hideAFHideDor(this)` calls the engine emits — is copied unchanged, so the DoR follows visibility at init exactly as it does on change.

**Four result shapes, all mechanical.** `bare` **527** — bare `true;`/`false;` statements → `this.visible = true/false;`; a literal inside a ternary is deliberately not matched. `expr` **266** — the value is a trailing bare expression (`this.parent.instanceIndex+1 == …;`) → `this.visible = (…);`. `assign` **15** — already assigns `this.visible` → copied verbatim. `sideeffect` — no boolean result at all (only calls, e.g. `hideAFHideDor(…)`) → copied verbatim, which is what the hand-authored forms already ship. Unclassifiable → `manual`, never auto-fixed; **0 today**.

**Both storage lineages carry visibility rules, and never both on one node:** `fd:rules` **316** (visual rule editor; the value's `script` is the machine-generated code *string*) and `fd:scripts` **501** (code editor; `script` is an *object* with `content`). The copy is always written as a SCRIPTMODEL appended to `fd:scripts` / `fd:init` — the only shape that can express `this.visible = …` (the visual editor cannot) and the shape AEM itself writes. A multi-value `fd:init` holding several independent SCRIPTMODELs is corpus-attested (AAAA_019), so the **80 components that already initialize something else are never rewritten**: the copy becomes an additional value and runs after theirs. 3 components have no `fd:scripts` node at all and get one, placed after their `fd:rules` sibling.

**Why this needed new code.** An AF rule is not an attribute a regex can patch: it is a JCR **multi-value String** property whose values are JSON documents, FileVault-escaped inside an XML attribute (values separated by *unescaped* commas, commas *inside* a value escaped `\,`, a leading `{`/`[` escaped on single-valued properties). Getting that codec wrong silently corrupts every rule on the form, so `decode`/`encode` are byte-exact inverses — verified by round-tripping **all 7767** `fd:rules`/`fd:scripts` values in the corpus (`python3 .claude/scripts/visible_init.py --selftest`, which also unit-tests the four rewrites). The new SCRIPTMODEL's `field` is derived from the XML ancestry rather than copied from the source rule: the stored `field` is **stale on more than half the corpus's rules** (rules get copy-pasted between forms and survive renames). It is editor metadata — the runtime binds `this` to the node the rule hangs on — so a derived value is strictly better.

**Extent: 155 of 286 packages, 808 rules on 805 components.** 9 further rules are already on Initialize (hand-fixed long ago on AAAL_019/AACJ_019) and are skipped — the same check that makes the sweep idempotent. Simulated end to end: **155/155 `ok`**, 808 rules added, re-scan reports **0 affected**, every form well-formed, **no attribute other than `fd:init` changed anywhere**, all display attributes (`jcr:title`/`_value`/`options`/`fd:translationIds`/`bindRef`/`css`/`visible`/`dorExclusion`) byte-identical, and all 80 pre-existing Initialize values preserved verbatim as a prefix.
**Detect:** script: `find_visible_init.py`  _(per component: a visibility rule whose rewritten body is not already an Initialize rule on that same component. `--report` emits the per-instance review surface — component · category · before → after. Affected = exactly what the Fix repairs, so the guard clears itself. Idempotent.)_
**Fix:** script: `set_visible_init.py`  _(quote-aware open-tag scan — rule JSON and rich text contain literal `>` — with byte-offset splices applied back-to-front, so nothing else is re-serialised. Prints `{inner, components, rules_added, by_category, existing_init_appended, scripts_nodes_created, remaining, ok}`; `ok` = the detector's own logic finds nothing left on the result.)_
**Verify:** the fix's `ok` + a re-scan showing 0 affected + a pilot **visual check in AEM: open a form whose first step has conditional panels and confirm the right panels are visible IMMEDIATELY, before touching any field** — that first-load state is the entire point of the sweep and no script can see it. Also confirm nothing that should be hidden is showing.
**Max changed lines:** 160   _(≈1 line per component; 73 is the corpus worst case — AAHQ_019 with 71 rules. Flag anything beyond ~2× that.)_
**Runs automatically:** yes
**Origin:** manual (UBS directive 2026-08-06 — visibility rules never run on a freshly loaded form)
**Tracking:** #98
**Status:** swept 2026-08-06 (155/155 — 808 Initialize copies on 805 components; re-scan of the branch reports 0 affected across all 286 packages, PR #99)

_Ordering: independent of the other in-flight sweeps — it only ever writes `fd:init` on `fd:scripts`, an attribute no other registry entry touches. Enrolment in the CI guard is the `Runs automatically:` field — `yes` here, the rollout being complete._
