## PROBLEM-address-city-country-mandatory — UBS Address fragments: City/Country incorrectly required
**Symptom:** panels embedding a UBS "Address" fragment (Address1, AddressGeneric1, AddressBlock_CountryDD, across the germany/italy/global/ubs fragment libraries) can leave City (`TXT_City_AddressBlock`) and Country (`DD_Country_AddressBlock`) mandatory — via a static `mandatory` attribute and/or the fragment's own `setMandatory(this, true)` Initialize script. On most UBS forms these fields should be optional.
**Target state, per address fragment panel:** the panel's own (guide path, `Initialize`) slot holds **exactly one** enabled entry whose code contains both calls, `this.`-qualified. ONE entry, because AEM's Expression Editor hands back a single SCRIPTMODEL per field+event pair and concatenates two with a bare comma, which throws client-side and takes the whole page's editor data down.
**The address fragment's hides and its mandatory flags live in ONE script (owner, 2026-09-01):** "most of the address fragments need to have all those fields hidden and `TXT_City_AddressBlock` / `DD_Country_AddressBlock` not mandatory, and all of this can be done in the same script." So a panel whose Initialize document carries a `hideAFHideDor` block **and** the two `setMandatory` calls is correct, in any order.

**ROUND 5 (2026-09-01, Marco's Italy report) — two bugs, one root cause: this rule owned a private, wrong FileVault codec.** Decision logic moved to the shared `address_required.py`; the detector and the fixer each had their own copy and the copies disagreed.

1. **The codec unescaped `\,` and nothing else, then json-loaded the whole `[…]` as one document.** A JCR multi-value escapes every backslash too, so a panel script holding a string literal (AAOE_033's `getCountryList(…, \"NATIONALITY_INDIVIDUAL\", this)`) arrived as invalid JSON and `decode_fd_init` returned None. Both predicates then said "no rule here", the panel was reported affected, and the fixer's `decode_fd_init(raw) or []` turned that into an empty array and wrote back its single entry — **destroying every other Initialize script on the panel.** That is the "it tries to modify them and ends up breaking the fragment" half of the report. The codec is now `visible_init.decode/encode` (byte-exact, self-tested over all 7 767 corpus rule values), and an unreadable `fd:init` is reported as `manual` and never rewritten.
2. **"Already correct" was `content.endswith(RULE_CONTENT)` — a POSITION test on a slot two rules write.** PROBLEM-fragment-library-consolidation writes its `hideAFHideDor` block to the same panel's Initialize slot and appends it **last**, so the `setMandatory` pair stopped being the tail the moment that rule ran; this rule re-appended, that rule moved its block back to the end, and the pair added a copy on every pass of `apply_sweeps`' fixpoint loop. The test is now **membership** — the two calls anywhere in the slot's single entry, in any order — which makes the two rules order-independent. `strip_our_lines` removes our statements wherever they sit, so a form carrying a duplicate from the ping-pong converges back to one copy. Comments and string literals are blanked (`visible_init._blank`) before matching, so a commented-out call is neither counted nor cut.
3. **THE OLD ENCODER ESCAPED THE SEPARATOR COMMAS, WHICH BREAKS THE PANEL'S RULE MODEL IN AEM — 63 of 118 address panels in the corpus are in that state.** It was `json.dumps(entries)` + `.replace(",", "\,")`, so the commas *between* array elements were escaped along with the ones inside them. A multi-value separates on UNescaped commas, so AEM reads the whole attribute as **one** value holding `{…},{…},{…}` — not valid JSON, the rule model broken, and the visual editor refusing to open the form. This is the failure CLAUDE.md warns about and the "breaking the rules of the content panel" half of the report. It stayed invisible because the old decoder json-loaded the whole `[…]` as one array, which happens to succeed on its own broken output, and because it cannot happen on a single-entry panel (55 of the 118). **Measured 2026-09-01: 63 panels in 41 forms** — 57 holding 3 concatenated entries, 6 holding 2. **The content of all 63 is correct**: split back apart, every one has exactly one entry in its own slot carrying both calls, so the repair is a pure re-encode (`address_required.split_concat` recovers the entries byte for byte via `json.JSONDecoder.raw_decode`; `visible_init.encode` writes them as a real multi-value). Verdict `encoding`: affected and reported as a regression, because the package on disk is wrong even though the script text is right.

**Known, out of scope: a stored `field` is often stale.** AAEL_019's address panel holds three Initialize documents, two of them stamped with a *different* panel's guide path (copy-pasted between forms, as `visible_init.field_id` documents). The runtime binds `this` to the node the rule hangs on so all three run, but AEM groups by the stored field, so that panel has an Editor-breaking 2-in-one-slot condition of its own. This rule keys its slot on the stored field — the convention the 66 already-fixed forms were written with, so changing it would make every one of them read as unfixed — and leaves the stale entries alone.
**Detect:** script: `find_address_required.py`  _(shared logic `address_required.py`. Per address panel a verdict: `ok` · `fix` (rule missing) · `regression` (2+ entries in the slot, or a round-1/2 unqualified call) · `encoding` (script right, attribute malformed) · `manual` (undecodable — deliberately NOT affected, since the fixer must not rewrite it and flagging it would leave the guard permanently red with no action available). `--form <F>`. Attribute text is read through `visible_init.scan`, whose attrs come from the raw open-tag text: expat hands values back XML-unescaped, so a value holding `&amp;quot;` (ABFH_033) would be unescaped twice.)_
**Fix:** script: `set_address_required.py`  _(byte-offset splices over the raw XML; delegates every decision to `address_required`. Folds our two lines into the slot's single entry (creating the entry, the `fd:init` attribute or the whole `fd:scripts` node as needed), never lets a slot hold 2+ entries, and where only the encoding is wrong leaves every value its own original string so the re-encode changes no script text. Prints `{inner, fixed, regressions_fixed, reencoded, manual, remaining, ok}`; `ok` = well-formed + the detector re-run on the result finds nothing + nothing manual. `--dry-run`.)_
**Verify:** the fix's own `ok` + `find_address_required.py` reporting 0 affected + `unzip -t` + a **visual check in AEM: open the address panel's rule editor** — that is the only place the malformed-multi-value repair is observable (before the fix the editor cannot load the panel's rules at all).
**Measured before rollout (2026-09-01, scratch copies of the 41 affected forms):** 41/41 fixer `ok`, 63 panels re-encoded, 0 manual, re-scan **0 affected**, second run byte-identical (idempotent), every package still `unzip -t` clean, and the recovered entries byte-identical to the originals (AAEL_019: 1 malformed value -> 3 well-formed, `parts == values` exactly).
**Runs automatically:** yes
**Origin:** manual
**Tracking:** #102
**Corpus fixed:** no — **63 panels in 41 forms carry the malformed `fd:init`** and are fixed but not yet committed/deployed; the re-sweep is pending. Detector on master: 41 affected, all verdict `encoding`.
**Status:** swept 2026-08-12 (66/66 re-fixed — 2026-08-11 rollout referenced fields bare, PN_AddressBlock-qualified now). **Round 5 2026-09-01 (hotfix, rollout pending):** shared module, correct codec, membership test, and the malformed-multi-value repair above.

## PROBLEM-configurator-reset-on-change — Changing a form-configurator choice must empty every panel it decides
**Symptom:** the form configurator asks what the form is for (private person / company, individual / legal entity) and each option reveals a different panel. The visibility rule only shows and hides, so nothing is ever cleared: fill the form in as one option, switch to another and come back, and the first option returns with every field still filled and every repeatable row still added. The submission then carries one option's data under another, invisibly, because the panel simply reappears already populated. **Origin: manual (UBS directive).** Scope is the reviewed list of option wordings in `configurator_reset.py::APPROVED_LABEL_SETS` — a choice that reveals panels but offers something else (an order type, an occupation) is a different question and out of scope; the detector reports those under `out_of_scope`.
**Detect:** script: `find_configurator_reset.py`  _(per choice: `add`, `append`, `ok` or a flag. `affected` is broader than the Fix and includes the flags; `fixable` is what the Fix acts on. `--report` shows the driven panels and the generated script; `--first-step-only` restricts to the form's opening step.)_
**Fix:** script: `set_configurator_reset.py` _(byte-offset splice writing a Value Commit SCRIPTMODEL that empties every panel the choice decides — `resetAllPanelInstances` per repeatable, then `resetData()`. The document carries `_archetype: "configurator-reset-on-change"` and opens with `// [configurator-reset-on-change] Generated automatically…`, so a re-run **replaces its own document in place** instead of adding a second one; where a hand-written Value Commit script is there, the reset is appended behind it and that script is left byte-identical.)_
**Skip (8 forms, 2026-08-11):** not checked by the **corpus** guard — the intake app passes `--no-skip`, so a package arriving again is checked in full — nothing for a reset to clear: no two panels are tied to the choice's value (5), or the panels it decides hold no field, repeatable or fragment (2). Each needs a person to confirm that is really so. AAIQ_019 AAMV_019 AAQR_033 AAUQ_033 BAWY_019 BAWZ_033 BBPT_019 AAWW_033 AACH_033.
**Verify:** the fix's `ok` + a re-scan showing only the Skip line + a pilot **visual check in AEM: pick an option, fill its panel and add a repeatable row, switch to another option, then return to the first — it must be empty with the row count back to its minimum.** Also confirm re-selecting the option already chosen clears nothing, that each option still shows and hides what it did, and on an appended form that the script already there still works.
**Max changed lines:** 8   _(one child node or one attribute per choice, plus closing the open tag where the component was self-closing.)_
**Runs automatically:** yes
**Origin:** manual (UBS directive — a configurator choice must never present an option carrying another option's data)
**Tracking:** #107
**Status:** swept 2026-08-12 **Reworked 2026-08-19:** the reset used to recognise itself by comparing script bodies, so any change to the panels a choice decides — a rename, a wrap, a panel gained or lost — read as a missing reset and wrote *another* Value Commit document; a form gathered one more on every run. It now carries the archetype id and the canonical comment, like PROBLEM-repeating-panel, and is replaced in place. The comment is the half that survives AEM's visual editor, which drops fields it does not know. Migration: the 200 documents written with the old `/* emptied on change … */` marker are recognised and rewritten once, leaving exactly one per choice.
_Ordering: only writes `fd:valueCommit`, which no other entry touches. Run after PROBLEM-naming-conventions, whose fixer rewrites `fd:*` script references. Run before PROBLEM-repeatable-add-label: where two same-named repeatables compete for one Add button that rule resolves ownership by byte proximity, so inserting a script node shifts one repeatable between `ok` and `out_of_scope` (both no-action; `affected` and `relabel` unchanged)._

---

## PROBLEM-repeatable-add-label — A repeatable's Add button reads `Add <panel title>`
**Symptom:** the Add button of a repeatable reads a bare `Add`, a label in the wrong language, or one naming a different entity than the panel it adds to — `Add client` on a panel titled `Beneficial owner`. **Only repeatables that have an Add button are in scope.**
**The label is the panel's own title, always.** `Add <title>` / `<Titel> hinzufügen` / `Aggiungere <titolo>` / `Añadir <título>`, in the form's base language — the majority language of its own inline titles. A panel with no title yet gets the bare verb (`Add`, `Hinzufügen`, `Aggiungere`, `Añadir`). The button is overwritten whatever it read before: the wording is decided once, on the panel, by [PROBLEM-repeating-panel](#) — which runs first — so the two can never disagree and the only manual step in the pair is writing a panel title.
**The button follows the title into the dictionary.** Where the wording table knows the words, the title was written as its English master and keyed, so the label is the English phrase (`Add account`) and carries the same entries — including one for the form's own language, which is what makes a German form read `Konto hinzufügen` rather than the master. Where the table knows nothing (`Meldeanschrift`), title and label both stay in the form's own words and key nothing. A package that ships no dictionary for its own language is written in that language directly, since there the master would be the text on screen.
**The Add button is found by ownership, then by scope and proximity.** A form reuses the name `BT_Add` for every repeatable, and two repeatables can share a name, so the name identifies nothing on its own. A button whose script adds an instance of *this* repeatable owns it; failing that, the nearest `BT_Add*` sibling.
**A button two panels claim with different titles is left alone** (`shared`): writing either label would be undone on the next pass. PROBLEM-repeating-panel reports that panel for a person to split.
**Detect:** script: `find_repeatable_add_label.py`  _(per repeatable with an Add button: `ok`, `relabel` or `shared`; affected = any `relabel`)_
**Every button's label is keyed, not only the ones a run rewrites** (2026-09-02). A label that already reads what the rule would write is an `ok` row, and its dictionary entry was never written — so a button somebody corrected by hand before the first sweep (`Add ordering customer`, AABF_019) stayed English in every language, which is exactly what the entry is for. And where the wording table does not know the title, the form's own dictionary may: `own_wording` matches the title against the form's keys ignoring case, and the label is composed from what it finds (`Ordenante` → `Añadir ordenante`). The label text itself is unchanged by either — only the entries. Corpus 2026-09-02: 28 labels in 9 forms gain entries.
**Fix:** script: `set_repeatable_add_label.py` _(quote-aware byte splice setting `jcr:title` and `custom` on every Add button the repeatable owns, plus additive `fd_<label>` dictionary entries — same UUIDv3 element name and escaping as #72, never overwriting an existing key, never writing a locale file the form does not have)_
**Verify:** the fix's `ok` + a re-scan showing 0 `relabel` + a **visual check in AEM** on one form per language: the button reads the panel's title, and the German/Italian/Spanish versions of an English-based form pick the translation up.
**Max changed lines:** omit — two attributes per button plus dictionary entries; gate on `ok` + the re-scan.
**Ordering:** runs **after** PROBLEM-repeating-panel, which writes the titles this rule reads.
**Runs automatically:** yes
**Origin:** manual (owner directive; the dictionary and its four languages were reviewed by the owner)
**Tracking:** #94
**Status:** swept 2026-08-04 (135/135 addressed). **Reworked 2026-08-19:** the label is no longer derived here from the form's text — it is taken from the repeating panel's title, always, and always overwritten. The `kept` and `manual` verdicts are gone with it, and so are the 46 buttons and 39 skipped forms that were waiting on a person: a panel without a title now yields a bare `Add` and the panel is the only thing anybody has to name.

---

## PROBLEM-banking-relationship-default-de — Banking-relationship panel (Germany): default `0319`, disabled
**Symptom:** on **Germany (entity `019`) forms only**, the banking-relationship panel's first field (`TXT_BankingRelationship1`) must default to `0319` and render **disabled** on load — an Initialize rule (owner-supplied) the deployed forms don't carry yet:
```
TXT_BankingRelationship1.value = '0319';
TXT_BankingRelationship1.enabled = false;
```
Italy (`033`) and every other entity are explicitly **out of scope** — the field keeps whatever value/state Italy forms already have; the fix is entity-gated, not fragment-gated, since the same canonical fragment is shared by both markets.
**Depends on [PROBLEM-banking-relationship-fragment](#) / #10 — run it first.** This only applies once the banking panel already carries the canonical fragRef (`.../afforms_ubs_fragmentlib/affrg_BankingRelationship1`); a panel still on a wrong/legacy fragRef variant is left alone here (in scope for #10, not for this entry) — same dependency shape as #29→#10 and #33→#29.
**Detect:** script: `find_banking_default_de.py`  _(affected = Germany form with ≥1 canonical banking panel missing the Initialize rule; non-Germany forms and non-canonical banking panels are never counted)_
**Fix:** script: `set_banking_default_de.py`  _(appends one Initialize SCRIPTMODEL to each matching panel's `fd:scripts`/`fd:init`, creating the node if absent; an existing Initialize rule on the panel, if any, is never rewritten — additive only, same discipline as `set_visible_init.py`. Non-Germany forms report `ok:true`, untouched.)_
**Verify:** must: `TXT_BankingRelationship1\.value = '0319'` ; must-not: none  _(per-panel correctness — which panel carries which rule on a multi-banking-panel form — is asserted by the fix's own `ok`/`remaining` re-scan, not by a file-level grep)_
**Max changed lines:** 3   _(one new fd:init attribute set/insert per panel; a form with N banking panels legitimately changes more — e.g. AANB_019's 3 panels, same multi-panel exception #10 already flagged and verified benign)_
**Runs automatically:** yes
**Origin:** manual (owner-supplied rule, confirmed 2026-08-11)
**Tracking:** #104
**Status:** swept 2026-08-11 (164/164)

---

## PROBLEM-email-phone-component — Email/telephone fields are plain textboxes
**Symptom:** the conversion engine has **no email/telephone template** (`profiles/ubs/aem/` ships only `textbox.xml`), so every field labelled "Email" / "Telephone" / "Mobile phone" / "Fax" is emitted as a plain `controls/textbox`: no format validation, no `autofillFieldKeyword` (`email`/`tel`) for browser autofill, no phonebox styling. 126 instances across 40 of 296 packages (87 node conversions + 39 untranslated dictionary messages). Ground truth is the **corpus, not the engine** — 63 already-correct instances (32 email, 31 telephone) across 26 forms, plus the engine's own fragment library (`affrg_Authorized_Person1`, `affrg_personal-details`); their attribute sets are vendored as `email_phone_labels.CANON`. Where the hand-built fragments disagree with the deployed corpus the corpus wins: telephone `css` is `widget_ajila-forms-ubs-phonebox` (31/31), email drops `css` entirely (31/32).
**Detect:** script: `find_email_phone_component.py --json`  _(a form is affected if a `controls/textbox` has an email/phone/fax label, if an existing `controls/email`/`controls/telephone` deviates from CANON, or if a target-locale dictionary renders the message in English — either an untranslated stub **or an absent `fd_<master>` key on a form that has the component**. The dictionary buckets are what make the rule idempotent; `email_phone_labels.dict_gaps` computes them against the messages the form's post-fix components actually need, so a form with no email field is never asked to carry the email message.)_
**Fix:** script: `set_email_phone_component.py`
**Verify:** must-not: `sling:resourceType="ajila-forms-customers/ajila-forms-ubs/components/controls/(email|telephone)"[^>]*css="widget_ajila-forms-ubs-textbox"`  _(the real gate is the fix's own `ok`: detector clean for the form AND no email/telephone node left on a non-`EML_`/`TEL_` prefix)_
**Runs automatically:** yes
**Origin:** manual
**Tracking:** #125 (coverage gap pass) · #117 (original rollout)
**Status:** swept 2026-08-17 (41/41 fixed + deployed; 0 flagged). Scope grew twice during the sweep: +3 forms when untranslated dictionary stubs were found, then +1 (BAUL_033) from a form merged into master mid-rollout — the detector re-scans the corpus each run, so re-running picks those up automatically. **Coverage-gap pass complete 2026-08-19 (#125, PR #126): 7/7 fixed + deployed** — BAUL_033 (5 node conversions), AAGB_019 (12 dictionary entries), BANS_019 (2, `fr-ch`/`it-ch`), AAAA/AAAL/AAKN/AANF_019 (1 `sp` entry each). Full-corpus re-scan on the branch: **0 affected of 304 packages**, and `check_regressions.py` reports `ok:true` across every swept rule, so the stricter detector (which now also flags an absent dictionary key) leaves the guard green. Only BAUL_033 flagged, on `delta`, for the known `guideTelephone` validator gap documented below.

**Label discrimination (the hard part — anchoring, not keyword matching).** `e-mail` requires the `e` prefix, which keeps "Mailing address confirmation" out; `\bmobil` keeps "immobili"/"automobili" out (a letter precedes `mobil`); `telefon\b` keeps "Telefonische Bestellung" out (the boundary fails before `ische`). The remaining 10 near-misses are an explicit, hand-checked `DENY_LABEL`/`DENY_NAME` list in `email_phone_labels.py` — run `find_email_phone_component.py --report` to see both the conversions and the denied residue side by side. Two denials are judgment, not typos: "Telefonico canale n." is a channel identifier, and ABEP_033's "Titolare / Numero di telefono in caso di domande" packs a holder NAME and a number into one field, so the numeric-only clause would reject valid input. The 386 untitled textboxes are all letterhead/address fields and none classify.

**Ordering dependency — the fixer chains the rename itself.** Changing the resourceType changes the required name prefix (`find_naming_violations.TYPE_PREFIX`: email→`EML`, telephone→`TEL`), so leaving a converted node on `TXT_` would turn the already-swept **PROBLEM-naming-conventions** guard red. `set_email_phone_component.py` therefore re-runs `set_naming_prefix.py` after the type change (which also rewrites references). `AF_`-prefixed letterhead/binding names are exempt in the naming rule and keep their names — that is deliberate, renaming them would break the sender-block bindings. Note the fix's `ok` is scoped to **this** sweep and deliberately does NOT inherit `set_naming_prefix.ok`, which reports False on a pristine BANR_033 too (the owner-accepted duplicate `TTL_Authorized_User`).

**Message localisation (owner decision 2026-08-14) — CONFIRMED WORKING.** 31 of the 32 deployed instances hardcode the English `validatePictureClauseMessage` even in German/Italian forms. This sweep instead puts the message in each form's per-locale dictionary as an `fd_<master>` entry, reusing the `default-translations` mechanism (`default_translations.render_entry`, byte-identical formatting). **Verified empirically on the pilot**, not assumed: fetching the DAM preview with `?wcmmode=disabled&afAcceptLang=<loc>` renders the translated message for every locale the form ships a dictionary for, and English for the rest (AACT_019 `de→de fr→fr it→it`, AAAA_019 `es→sp`, AAGZ_033/BANR_033 `it→it`). So the per-node `fd:translationIds` fallback (BBBO_019 / BBPT_019) is **not** needed. A form with no dictionary at all (AAPC_033, BAQM_033) keeps the English message — both declare `jcr:language="en"`, so that is correct, and the fix never creates a dictionary file a form doesn't already have.

**Untranslated stubs — a present key is NOT a translation (#72 again).** 39 entries across 15 forms already carried `fd_<master>` with the **English text** as `sling:message`, so AEM rendered English in that locale. An "insert if the key is missing" fix silently leaves those broken — AAGZ_033 rendered English in Italian on the first pilot pass despite having both keys. The fixer therefore compares the *message*, not just the key, and rewrites a stub in place; the detector flags one as `dict-stub`. Anything that is neither the English master nor a wording this sweep itself superseded is treated as a human translation and never touched.

**Translation provenance — adopt the corpus wording, don't invent a rival.** Where the corpus already carried an authored translation, that exact phrasing is used, so the same sentence doesn't ship in two wordings: `de` both messages, `sp` both messages (the "Por favor, …" form), `it` both (from the BBPT_019 / AAPC_033 node attributes). Only `fr` had no precedent and is authored here. An earlier pass of this sweep shipped its own `de` phone and `sp` wordings before that survey was done; those strings are listed in `email_phone_labels.SUPERSEDED` so the fixer overwrites them. After the sweep the corpus has exactly **one** wording per language per message — re-check with the per-language tally in the closing comment on #117 before adding any new locale.

**Source types — a whitelist, and why (found in self-review, and by the owner spotting a miss).** The label heuristic converts only from `controls/textbox` and `controls/numericbox`. **numericbox matters**: AAPC_033's two contact fields ("Numero telefonico aziendale", "Numero di fax") are numeric boxes carrying `displayPictureClause="num{(zz,zzz,…zz)}"`, which reformats a phone number as `1,234,567.00` and rejects a leading `+` — strictly worse than the textbox case, and invisible to a textbox-only detector. Surveying *every* `/controls/` type for a phone/email label turns up 25 further matches and **all 25 are false positives**: 20 `panel` (the field's own CONTAINER — `PN_Fax_FCGroup1`, `PN_Email` — matched on its name, or a section title), 2 `textdraw`, 1 `tertiarybutton` ("Aggiungere indirizzo e-mail"), 1 `checkbox` (a consent line mentioning Telefax-Banking), and 1 `textboxMultiline` (AAIQ_019's letterhead `AF_Sender_Additional_References` relabelled "Fax" — a multiline→single-line change is a different, riskier edit, deliberately left alone). Hence `CONVERTIBLE_FROM` is a whitelist, not a blacklist.

**Two over-reaches caught in self-review — both would have shipped silently.**
- **`textIsRich` is NOT enforced.** All 63 reference instances carry `[true,true,true]`, but that is a property of their lineage, not of being an email/telephone component: 44 of the 92 candidate textboxes carry the letterhead lineage's 4-element `[true,true,true,true]`. Forcing 3 elements would have rewritten an unrelated attribute on nearly half the nodes. Beware the general trap — an attribute being unanimous across the reference set is not evidence it *belongs* to the component.
- **`CANON_OPTIONAL` applies only when normalising in place.** It exists to preserve the two deliberate `{0,14}` phone clause variants on nodes that are *already* telephones. Honouring it on a *conversion* would have let a numericbox keep its `num{(zz,…)}` display clause. So `canon_gap`/`rewrite_tag` take an `in_place` flag.

**Disabled fields are excluded.** 10 nodes (all `AF_Sender_State`, the letterhead's Canton/State slot relabelled to show the advisor's phone) carry `enabled="{Boolean}false"`. They are read-only and system-populated, so validation and an autofill hint buy nothing, while `^([+]|00)…` could reject a local-format value ("044 234 56 78") and make the form unsubmittable. The check runs **last** in `classify`, after the label matches — running it first floods the near-miss audit list with all 287 disabled textboxes. Note the editable sibling in the same letterhead (`AF_CA_Phone`, `enabled=true`) **is** converted.

**Coverage-gap pass (2026-08-19, #125) — three holes the first rollout left, and why its own detector reported clean.** A re-audit (independent broad-lexicon scan of all 53k control nodes, three label probes instead of one) confirmed **no over-conversion** — all 150 converted nodes carry a genuine contact label, no email↔telephone mix-up, no disabled node converted — but found 23 missed instances in 7 forms. The `Status: swept` line stays as-is through this pass: dropping it would un-enrol the rule from the CI regression guard for the whole corpus, which is worse than a temporarily incomplete corpus (the guard's PR mode only checks *changed* forms, so the pass's own PR stays green, and the full-corpus backstop only sees the new detector once the PR lands with the fixes).
- **A CamelCase name is not a word list (5 nodes, BAUL_033).** `classify`'s name fallback split on `_` only, so `TXT_NumeroDiTelefonoFisso` never matched `\btelefono\b` — no boundary before `Fisso`. These 5 are the **older lineage's label shape**: the label lives in a sibling `titledraw` (`TTL_NumeroDiTelefonoFisso`) and the field carries no `jcr:title` at all, so the name is the only probe there is. The same form's second contact block *was* converted, so its own twins are the reference. Fixed by splitting camel humps too (`email_phone_labels.name_probe`); measured against all 478 untitled input nodes in the corpus it newly classifies **exactly** those 5, zero false positives. The converted nodes stay untitled — the fix never invents a `jcr:title`.
- **An absent dictionary key is invisible to a stub check (16 entries, 5 forms).** The fixer derived the messages to insert from *this run's conversions*, so (a) **AAGB_019** — 3 email + 3 telephone nodes that were **already canonical** — was never "affected" and got no message at all, rendering English in all 6 of its non-English locales; and (b) **AAAA_019 / AAAL_019 / AAKN_019 / AANF_019** each ship a full `es.xml` **and** a sparse second Spanish `sp.xml`; only the kind converted in that run reached `sp`. The detector could not see either, because it only flagged a *present* key with an English message. Now `add_masters` comes from the **post-fix components** and the detector reports an absent key as `dict-missing`.
- **Two regional locales were never written (2 entries, BANS_019).** It ships **only** `fr-ch` and `it-ch` (no plain `fr`/`it`), and neither is in `default_translations.TARGET_LOCALES`, so both files were skipped and French and Italian readers got the English message. Added as `email_phone_labels.EXTRA_LOCALES` (`fr-ch`→fr, `it-ch`→it) rather than by editing `TARGET_LOCALES`, which drives the already-swept **default-translations** rule — widening it there would silently change that sweep's scope. Collapsing regional variants is the same rule **PROBLEM-metadata-languages** applies.
- **Correctly left alone**, re-confirmed in the audit: the deny-list labels, the 11 disabled `AF_Sender_State` letterhead slots, the 2 multiline `Fax` fields, the `immobile`/`automobili`/`valori mobiliari` false friends, and ABEP_033's `Nome di dominio (ad es. @musterdomain.com)` — a domain, not an address. Corpus labels are EN/IT/DE only (no form is French- or Spanish-labelled), and a broad fr/es lexicon (`courriel`, `téléphone`, `portable`, `correo electrónico`, `móvil`) returns zero unconverted hits — the language-shaped damage was in the **locale map**, not the label wording.

**Two things the gap-pass pilot established (2026-08-19) — expect them on every future run.**
- **`delta` flags every telephone conversion, and it is a validator gap, not a regression.** Converting a textbox to `controls/telephone` changes `guideNodeClass` from `guideTextBox` (known to the engine MCP's validator) to `guideTelephone` (not known), so the delta gate reports "new violation — … has unknown guideNodeClass guideTelephone". It is **pre-existing corpus-wide debt**: the same validator reports the identical complaint on *untouched, already-deployed* packages (AAFK_019 → 3, AAAA_019 → 12). And the value is right by the engine's own intent — `profiles/ubs/aem/telephone.xml` emits `guideNodeClass="guideTelephone"` and `config.toml` sets `css_telephone = "widget_ajila-forms-ubs-phonebox"`, i.e. the engine now **agrees with the corpus-derived CANON** on both. (The engine has since gained `email.xml`/`telephone.xml` templates — the "no email/telephone template" in the Symptom above was true when written, and the built binary's validator still lags them, the documented stale-binary trap.) So: accept a `delta` flag whose only content is `unknown guideNodeClass guideTelephone`; investigate any other delta text.
- **`sp.xml` appears not to be served by AEM — a known unknown, see #17.** AAGB_019 carries our email/phone message in **both** `es.xml` and `sp.xml` after the fix, yet the deployed render gives `afAcceptLang=es` → Spanish and `afAcceptLang=sp` → **English**. So the four `sp`-only gaps (AAAA/AAAL/AAKN/AANF_019) are **correct-by-consistency but not demonstrably user-visible**; `es` is the locale AEM actually serves. Do not claim a rendering improvement for an `sp`-only fix without re-testing. The genuinely user-visible wins of this pass are AAGB_019 (de/de-ch/es/fr/it all verified rendering their translation, previously English everywhere) and BANS_019 (`fr-ch` → French, `it-ch` → Italian, verified).

**Review note (human, per form):** the converted fields must still render correctly — check that a phone field shows the phonebox styling and rejects a non-`+`/`00` value, that an email field rejects a malformed address, and that no label or layout shifted. Fax fields are deliberately converted to the *telephone* component (owner decision), so their error text reads "valid phone number" — that is expected, not a defect.

---

## PROBLEM-signature-name-fill — The host form must author the signer-name auto-fill
**Symptom:** on a signature block the signer's name should pre-fill from the first/last name entered earlier. The market signature fragments do that themselves, through a hardcoded identifier; the shared fragments the consolidation moves them to do not, and `ubs/affrg_SignatureGeneric1` ships its own calc `"enabled": false` because the panels it would resolve belong to the HOST FORM. So after the swap the name silently stops filling — a blank field on a PDF a client signs, with no error anywhere. The host form has to author the fill itself, exactly as UBS's reference form BAVP_033 does, and name the signature panel `PN_Sign_<data panel>` so that it still says whose signature it is once every signature fragment is the same role-neutral one.
**A shared DATA-panel name no longer refuses the fill when the form states the pairing (2026-09-02).** Every name in the generated calc is an identifier the AEM runtime resolves globally, so the rule refused any pair whose data panel was not unique. Since two parties of a class keep the same panel name on purpose (PROBLEM-fragment-library-consolidation, same date: the generic fragment addresses the host panel by it, and the branches holding them are mutually exclusive), that refusal now hits the ordinary case — reported on AABF_019, where the client's signature was left unfilled because a second `PN_CPGRP` exists in the minor's branch. Where the pairing comes from the **Add/Remove wiring** — the Add that adds this signature and that data panel together, which is the form's own statement of who signs — the calc is written, and the runtime resolves the identifier the same way it resolves the fragment's own `removeInstance(PN_CPGRP)`. Where the pairing rests only on a panel name or a fragment contract, an ambiguous target would be a guess and is still refused; a shared name on the SIGNATURE panel is still refused outright, since the calc has to write INTO it. The `via` says which case it was. Corpus 2026-09-02: **4 pairs in 3 forms** gain their fill (AAAL_019, AAIQ_019, AATG_033), the fixer is idempotent on a second run, and AABF_019 ends with both calcs — `PN_CPGRP → PN_SGN_CPGRP` and `PN_AHGRP → PN_Sign_AHGRP` — while the guardian's signature stays correctly unfilled, because a form that captures no guardian data has no name to read.
**Detect:** script: `find_signature_name_fill.py`  _(affected = at least one pair that can be wired and nobody wires yet — exactly what the fixer adds, so the guard clears itself; everything else is `skipped`, never affected)_
**Fix:** script: `set_signature_name_fill.py`  _(renames the signature panel, creates the anchor beside the first signature panel when missing, then appends one Calculate document per pair. Only ever replaces documents carrying the `[signature-name-fill]` marker; anything unmarked is preserved byte for byte, and a document of ours whose write path no longer matches the fragment now referenced is regenerated rather than left stale. Prints `{inner, anchor_created, filled, replaced, renamed, rename_blocked, skipped, ok}`. Verified: idempotent — second pass byte-identical — `unzip -t` and expat clean, codec round-trip OK on 189 rule attributes, and `find_naming_violations.py` stays at 0 because the anchor carries the `TXT_` prefix.)_
**Verify:** the fix's `ok` + a re-scan showing 0 affected for the form + `unzip -t` and a well-formedness parse of every XML in the package + `find_naming_violations.py` still 0 + a pilot **visual check in AEM**: type a first and last name, the signature's Name field fills; add a second row and each signature picks up its own; after the rename the data panel's Add/Remove still moves the signature panel with it.
**Max changed lines:** omit — one anchor field plus one Calculate document per pair, and the rename touches every reference to the panel in the document.
**The wiring source was reading one of the two call shapes this corpus uses (2026-08-24).** There
are two ways to ask for an instance, and they put the panel in opposite places:

    PN_X.instanceManager.addInstance();               <- the panel is the RECEIVER  (202 of 304 forms)
    window.forms.ubs.addInstance(this.parent.PN_X);   <- the panel is the ARGUMENT  (208 of 304 forms)

The second is what PROBLEM-repeating-panel writes; the first is what the hand-built forms say.
`INSTANCE_CALL` matched only the argument shape, so on the **pristine** corpus this rule's first and
best pairing source — the form's own Add/Remove wiring, the author's own statement of which
signature belongs to which party — produced **0 pairs**, and all 100 came from the market fragment's
contract, which the consolidation swap takes away. Both shapes now live in `signature_pairing.py`,
shared with PROBLEM-repeating-panel so the two rules cannot drift apart again.

**Effect: `via: add/remove wiring` 0 → 94, `via: fragment contract` 98 → 6, pairs 100 → 101 across
68 → 70 forms.** The count barely moves and that is the point: the same pairs are now derived from
the form instead of inferred from a fragment that is about to be replaced. Cross-checked pair by
pair — the wiring **agrees** with the contract on 93, **disagrees** on 4, and answers 4 the contract
cannot. In all four disagreements the wiring names the panel that actually exists in the form
(`PN_ARRP` where the contract says `PN_ARP`, `RCP_AHRP` where it says `PN_AHRP`), which `plan` would
have discarded anyway. Two new refusals appear and are correct: `ambiguous wiring: driven together
with PN_AHRP, PN_LGA` — AAAZ_019's legal-guardian trap, now visible from the wiring instead of
invisible.

**One data panel, any number of signature panels.** `twin_pairs` required exactly one signature and
one data panel, so a click that drives **two** signature blocks yielded no pair for either. That is
not ambiguity — it is a form where the same party signs twice, which AARG_033 does for both its
roles (the contract and its annex, each ending "Letto, confermato e sottoscritto"). Both were
refused, and the fragment contract could only rescue one, so `PN_SGN_CPGRP` got a calc and
`PN_SGN_CPGRP_CL` got nothing. The ambiguity that still has to be refused is the other direction —
one signature driven together with two DATA panels — and it is untouched.

**A role suffix still states the data panel.** Under the convention settled 2026-08-20 the second
signature block of a party takes `_<ROLE>` (`PN_SGN_CPGRP_CL` beside `PN_SGN_CPGRP`) and both belong
to `PN_CPGRP`. Read strictly, only the first "already states its data panel", so the rule proposed
renaming the second to a name the first holds, was blocked, and proposed it again every pass.
`SP.data_panel_candidates` tries the **suffixed** data panel first — where one exists it is a
different *party* and the bare one would put the wrong person's name on a signature — and falls back
to the bare one, which is the one-party-signing-twice case.

**Corpus fixed: NO.** 70 forms carry a wireable pair nobody wires; the Skip line lists 68 and is stale (measured before the 2026-08-24 pairing change). `Runs automatically: yes` means wired in, not done.
**Runs automatically:** yes
**Origin:** manual (owner directive — the consolidation must not lose the auto-fill)
**Tracking:** #123
**Status:** **swept 2026-08-19 — enrolled for the intake app, corpus rollout NOT done.** Measured and verified 2026-08-19 (98 pairs / 68 forms); needs no naming exemption — the anchor carries the `TXT_` prefix and `find_naming_violations.py` measures 0 — so enrolment costs nothing beyond the Skip line below. `run_sweep.py --slug PROBLEM-signature-name-fill --pilot` is still the way to roll it out form by form.

Skip lines are the debt a rollout has not cleared yet: the **corpus** guard does not check these forms for this entry, and the intake app passes `--no-skip`, so an incoming package is checked in full and a person sees the violation. Measured **2026-08-19, before the PROBLEM-repeating-panel corpus rollout** — that rollout opens the 609 `deferred` fragRefs, so regenerate this line right after it — `python3 .claude/scripts/find_signature_name_fill.py | python3 -c "import json,sys;print(' '.join(json.load(sys.stdin)['affected']))"`. AAAN_019 AABA_019 AABF_019 AABI_019 AABN_019 AABO_019 AACJ_019 AACR_019 AACX_033 AADI_019 AAFC_019 AAGA_019 AAGB_019 AAGC_019 AAGD_019 AAGF_019 AAGI_019 AAGK_019 AAGL_019 AAGO_019 AAGU_019 AAGY_019 AAGZ_019 AAGZ_033 AAHA_019 AAHH_019 AAIH_019 AAIV_033 AAJY_019 AAOR_033 AAOV_033 AAOW_033 AAOX_033 AAPA_033 AAPG_033 AAPK_033 AAQT_033 AARG_033 AARI_033 AARJ_033 AARZ_033 AASU_033 AASV_033 AATH_033 AATK_033 AATO_033 AATP_033 AATV_033 AATW_033 AAUB_033 AAUQ_033 AAUR_033 AAUS_033 AAWW_033 ABEP_033 ABFH_033 ABRS_033 ACFK_033 ADCL_033 ADCN_019 BABJ_019 BAGE_019 BAGH_019 BAGK_019 BAGZ_033 BAQM_033 BAQN_033 BAUO_019

**The signer-name auto-fill is ours to write, not UBS's — `signature_name_fill.py`.** A signature fragment cannot fill the signer's name by itself: the panels it would resolve belong to the host form, which is why `affrg_SignatureGeneric1` ships its calc `"enabled": false` and writes through global identifiers instead of `this.value` — it is a template meant to be hosted outside the fragment. UBS's own reference form (BAVP_033, supplied as the example to follow) does exactly that: a hidden textbox carrying **one Calculate document per role**, each looping the data panel's instances and writing into the signature fragment's Name field by identifier. So the fill has to be a **Calculate**, and Calculate is an event of something with a value — **0 of the corpus's 362 `fd:calc` rules sit on a panel** — while both ends of the wiring live inside referenced fragments (319 of 329 signature `fragRef` panels are bare). Hence the anchor field: `TXT_Signatures_Name_Calc`, hidden, `dorExclusion`, `summaryExclusion`. Putting the script on the signature panel instead is already in the corpus and is **dead code** (AAGZ_033, AAKP_033 carry a field-shaped script — `this.value`, `this.pristine` — on a panel that has neither, so the guard evaluates false and the body never runs). The script itself is UBS's, verbatim: no `try/catch`, no `pristine` guard, no index clamping. An earlier version added all three and **stopped filling anything** — reading `pristine` on a field inside another fragment's instance has no precedent in the corpus and did not return what was assumed, and the empty `catch` hid the failure.

**It also names the signature panel.** Once the swap has run, every signature fragment is the same role-neutral one, so the panel's own name is the only thing left in the form that says **whose** signature it is — and it is what the next run of this rule reads to re-derive the pairing. The convention is `PN_Sign_<data panel token>` (`PN_AHRP` → `PN_Sign_AHRP`), which has to be *imposed* rather than derived: 79 of the corpus's 82 wired pairs call the panel `PN_Signature…` and the three `PN_Sign_…` disagree with each other (`PN_ARP` → `PN_Sign_ARRP`, `PN_AHRP` → `PN_Sign_Client`, `RCP_LR` → `PN_Sign_AR`). The one place it is stated by design is the generic fragment's own calc: it reads `PN_AHGRP` and writes `PN_Sign_AHGRP`. Verified safe against the naming guard — `find_naming_violations.py` reports 0 on BAVP_033, AAGZ_033 and AAGF_019, all of which already carry `PN_Sign_*` panels. The rename goes through `signature_contract.apply_renames`, so the Add/Remove clicks that drive the panel move with it. It is refused when the target name is taken, and when two signature panels are fed by one data panel and would collide (AAIV_033, AARG_033).

**Extent: 98 pairs across 68 forms** — 29 paired from the form's own Add/Remove wiring, 67 from the fragment contract, 2 from the panel name; 92 renames, 2 already correct, 4 refused. Nothing is hardcoded: the pair comes from the form, the paths from the fragment catalogue matched by R2 role. Everything ambiguous is refused: 30 instances are skipped because two nodes share the signature panel's name (no identifier can address one of them — the same call `find_signature_panel_ref.py` makes with `flag:shared-name`), 2 for ambiguous wiring, 51 because no data panel could be determined, and 5 because a script already fills them — including UBS's own, which is never touched.

_**The data side is also read from the host form, not only from fragments.** 62 of the pairs this rule used to refuse have their name fields loose in the form — no fragment involved — and in a single uniform shape: `TXT_Cognome_<hash>` (label "Cognome") plus `TXT_NomeI_<hash>` (label "Nome/i"). `loose_name_pair` reads them, but only when the panel holds **exactly one** last-name and **one** first-name field, both under the same parent, and neither inside a repeatable panel nested in it. BANR_033's panel carries six people (BAPH…BAPM) and so offers six of each; picking a pair there would fill one person's signature with another's name, so it is refused. The last/first tokens are declared from measured leaf names in the four authoring languages, and LAST is matched and removed before FIRST is tried — otherwise `TXT_Cognome` reads as a first name (it contains "Nome"), and the single combined boxes `TXT_NomeECognome` / `TXT_IntestatoACognomeNome` / `TXT_LastNameFirstNameSCompany` would match both halves instead of neither. **Effect: 37 → 98 pairs across 34 → 68 forms, and 62 more signature instances move `manual` → `swap`.**_

_**Ordering for the name-fill: run it BEFORE the fragRef swaps.** The earlier note here said the opposite; measurement reversed it. The **paths** are written against the fragment the panel references at that moment, and are self-healing rather than order-independent. An earlier version resolved them through `classify` so that both orders produced identical scripts; that was wrong in the only way that matters, because the swap is gated by the repeating-panel archetype and cannot run on any form yet — so all 98 scripts pointed at `PN_Signature_AuthorizedRepresentative.TXT_Name_AuthorizedRepresentative` while the panel still held `germany/affrg_ARSignature1`, whose Name field is `TXT_Name_Sign_AR` at the root. Nothing would have filled and nothing would have said why. Now the write path matches the current fragment (verified on all 98 pairs), and when the swap later moves the Name field the pair is reported `stale` and the next pass regenerates it — measured end to end on AAAN_019: fill → `TXT_Name_Sign_AR`, swap → `stale: 1`, fill again → `replaced: 1` and `PN_Signature_AuthorizedRepresentative.TXT_Name_AuthorizedRepresentative`, re-scan 0, still one document. `apply_sweeps.py` iterates to a fixpoint, so that convergence happens inside a single Run-all-sweeps. What is not safe either way is the **pairing**: for the pairs derived from the fragment contract, the contract belongs to the *market* fragment, and the swap replaces it with a role-neutral one that has none — AAGZ_033's `PN_Sign_LRP` and both of AAIV_033's signature panels wire before the swap and cannot be wired after it. A shared fragment does inherit the contract of the market fragments that route into it when they all agree (`affrg_global_ClientSignature_…` → `PN_AHRP`, `…AuthorizedRepresentative…` → `PN_ARP`, `…BeneficialOwner…` → `PN_BORP`), but `affrg_SignatureGeneric1` is the target of five contracts that disagree, and inheriting there would be a guess. Running the name-fill first also makes R9's gate exact: the panels `wireable_panels` reports are the panels that will actually be filled._

## PROBLEM-fragment-library-consolidation — Market fragment libraries must be emptied into the shared ones

**UBS directive, 2026-08-20 — the target is the GENERIC PARTNER fragment, not the small one.**
This supersedes the similarity-derived target choice below for every partner block and every
signature; everything else in this entry still holds. A form must never reference the small UBS
fragments (Individual Basic, Entity Basic, Address Generic, Form of Address, DOB and Nationality,
Date and Country Incorporation) — they are the parts UBS assembles four generic partner fragments
out of, and a form references the partner fragment: `affrg_ContractualPartnerGeneric1`
(AccountHolder / Lessee / ContractingPartner), `affrg_PartnertoPartnerGeneric1` (FIM /
ControllingPerson / ConnectedParty / AuthRep / FIMAuthRep), `affrg_BeneficialOwnerGeneric1`,
`affrg_PowerofAttorneyGeneric1`. Each contains all six small fragments and the host hides the ones
it does not need. Every signature becomes `affrg_SignatureGeneric1`, which reverses R3's
preference for the role-specific `affrg_global_<Role>Signature_…` family. **Changing the fragment
is not rewriting a `fragRef`:** the panel takes the UBS table's name, becomes the plain AEM
fragment panel, and the content bound to the old fragment is dropped. Policy as data:
`.claude/scripts/ubs_general_fragments.py`; per-form reasoning shared by the detector and the
fixer: `.claude/scripts/general_fragments_plan.py`; the whole table, the measured evidence ladder
and the open questions: [ubs-general-fragments.md](ubs-general-fragments.md). Consequences worth
stating: **composing a person block out of small fragments mostly dissolves** — the generic IS the composition, so
`germany/affrg_AuthRep` (47 instances), `italy/affrg_LegalRepresentative` (38) and the two
repeating person blocks become ordinary swaps — and the route order flips to **ubs first, then
global**, with global left only where UBS names nothing (internal bank use, footnote, infobox).
**Symptom:** `afforms_germany_fragmentlib` and `afforms_italy_fragmentlib` are being retired: every `fragRef` into them has to point at `afforms_global_fragmentlib` or `afforms_ubs_fragmentlib` instead. germany and italy are **peers** — a germany fragment is never pointed at an italy one or vice versa, and `extract_fragment_correspondence.py` enforces that at the data level, so a peer pair is not even present in the candidate table (an earlier version routed germany→italy: a wrong answer that looked plausible because the two market libraries hold near-identical copies, so peer pairs scored highest and crowded out the real targets). Which fragment a `fragRef` becomes is **not** a similarity question — it is read off the UBS table transcribed in `ubs_general_fragments.py`, one row per market fragment, and the reviewer's copy of that routing with all 69 sources is in [ubs-general-fragments.md](ubs-general-fragments.md). Extent across 304 packages with the archetype gate off, so every instance is planned: **511 swap · 12 standalone · 161 manual · 391 ok**, of which 23 swaps are signature panels whose fragment was inlined and 169 instances take their visibility from the form's own Initialize rule (see the blocks below). With the gate in force it is fewer, because a repeatable the repeating-panel archetype has not stamped yet is `deferred` rather than reported — Ordering 1.
**The archetype gate now sees a panel with no button (2026-09-02).** `archetype_gate.coverage` reads the archetype's ownership marker out of the rule JSON on the Add/Remove buttons, so a repeatable that has neither — nothing adds it, so PROBLEM-repeating-panel writes it no buttons — could carry no marker anywhere and was `deferred` for ever: the legal-guardian and UBS Europe signature blocks, **55 panels in the corpus**. Derivation 4 reads instead the property pair only the archetype writes on the panel itself (`ajilaPanelSubject` + `dorFieldStyling="Repeating Panel Numbering"`). Measured: all **683** panels the marker derivations already cover carry that pair too, so it agrees with them everywhere and adds exactly those 55. AABF_019's guardian signature goes `deferred` → planned with this and the repeating-panel widening together.
**A minor is the CLIENT, and the guardian signs without being a party (owner directive 2026-09-02).** The Germany configurator asks who the form is addressed to — `RB_FormularAdressat` = `Private Person | Minderjährige | Firma | GbR` — so the branch a minor's data sits in holds the **contracting party**, one who happens to be under age. `LGA` / `LG` / `LEGALGUARDIAN` moved from `p2p` to `cp` in `PANEL_NAME_LEXICON` for that reason (39 instances in 25 forms carry one of those tokens). The guardian's own signature is untouched by the move — a signature's class comes from the fragment table, not from this lexicon — and in a form like AABF_019 it has **no data panel at all**, which the pairing now says instead of inventing one.
**Signature pairing: the form's own statement first, then position, and never a partner twice.** `_pair_signatures` only counted — the nth signature of a class ↔ the nth partner of that class, falling back to the LAST partner — and counting cannot tell two parties of a class apart, because the role suffixes do not exist yet at that point. On AABF_019 both `p2p` signatures landed on the same partner and then took the same suffix: two panels called `PN_Sign_AHGRP_AR`, which the duplicate guard could only refuse. Now a signature whose own name states its data panel is paired with it first — both conventions are read, the current `PN_Sign_<data>` and the older `PN_<data>_Sign`, on the row as well as the `_Fragment` panel inside it — position settles the rest over what is left, and a signature with no partner left keeps its name and is reported (`no p2p partner in this form`), because pairing the guardians with the representative would have filled his name into their block. The suffix a twin inherits now follows the **partner** it belongs to rather than the shared name, which had given AABF_019's client signature the minor's `_LGA`.
**Two parties of a class keep the SAME name, and the suffix pass had to stop overruling that.** `_keeps_dictated_name` already said it — the generic partner fragment drives the host panel by name from inside (`removeInstance(PN_CPGRP)`), so a suffix silently breaks the Remove button it ships, and the refusal pass accepts the duplicate because the branches holding the two parties are mutually exclusive and AEM resolves the reference by proximity and visibility. But the suffix pass ran first and gave the second party a role token, so the duplicate never survived to be accepted. It now skips a panel whose name is dictated; **signatures still take suffixes**, because `affrg_SignatureGeneric1` names no host panel and PROBLEM-signature-name-fill pairs by name.
**The hide block has to be written per NODE, not per name.** `apply_init` located the panel by its new name on the renamed document and bailed out when the name was not unique ("not addressable — the guards should have caught it"). Once two parties of a class keep the same name on purpose, that bail-out left **both** of them without their `hideAFHideDor` calls and the generic showed every sub-panel the source never covered — reported on AABF_019, where the client's and the minor's blocks are both `PN_CPGRP` (`hidden: 5` where the plan wanted 15). The plan and the document agree on order, so the nth item of a name is now written to the nth node of it, and a count mismatch is still left to the guards.
**Corpus effect of all three, measured 2026-09-02:** `manual` 58 → **45** rows (AAFD_019, AAHG_019, AAHM_019, BARS_019 and BATF_019 leave the review queue, nothing joins it), `swap` 22 → 35, `deferred` unchanged at 61. The whole corpus ends with **6 duplicate-name groups**, all reviewable: two `PN_CPGRP` in AAHG_019, BARS_019 and BATF_019 (client + minor, the AABF shape), two `PN_AHGRP` in AAAL_019, two `PN_CPG` in AAFD_019 and AAHM_019. AABF_019 end to end: **2 manual → 0**, client `PN_CPGRP` + `PN_SGN_CPGRP`, minor `PN_CPGRP` as well (no signature — a minor does not sign), representative `PN_AHGRP` + `PN_Sign_AHGRP`, guardian's signature unpaired and keeping its name. `ubs_general_fragments.py --check` green. **Not covered: the ~21 forms already swapped as `p2p`** (`PN_AHGRP` titled `Legal guardian`) — the fragRef is gone there, so this rule no longer sees them; whether to re-do those is a call for a person.
**Detect:** script: `find_general_fragments.py`  _(one row per library `fragRef`, plus one per signature panel whose fragment was INLINED (no `fragRef` to search for — see the embedded-signature block below; they are listed under `embedded` whatever their verdict), with a verdict: `swap` = component-safe under R1–R8; `manual` = a counterpart exists but something real would be lost, or nothing resembles it, or R7 tripped; `compose` = R5, needs a rebuilt panel; `exception` = R6, reviewed and accepted where it is; `ok` = already shared or not a library fragRef; **`deferred` = the fragRef sits in a repeatable panel the repeating-panel archetype has not stamped yet** (Ordering 1) — counted, listed, and deliberately **not** a violation. `manual` and `compose` are reported as **intake-app errors for a human**, never guessed at. `--form <dir>`, `--show swap|manual|all|none`, `--hides`, `--renames`. Coverage on its own: `archetype_gate.py [--form X] [--verbose]`.)_
**Fix:** script: `set_general_fragments.py`  _(per `swap` row: delete the form's OLD hide script once its evidence has been read into the new visibility block, rewrite the `fragRef`, add `window.forms.ubs.hideAFHideDor(<NAME>)` to that panel's Initialize rule for every component the target introduces, and apply the field renames. `manual`/`standalone`/`deferred`/`exception` rows are never touched. Renames go through `signature_contract.apply_renames` — `name="…"` definitions plus `fd:*` bodies only, never `jcr:title`/`_value`/`options`/`fd:translationIds`. Prints `{inner, swapped, hidden, merged, renamed, manual_left, ok}`; a form with only manual rows reports `ok:true, swapped:0` — not a failure.)_
**Verify:** the fix's `ok` + a re-scan showing `swap: 0` for the form + `unzip -t` and a well-formedness parse of every XML in the package + **the naming guard staying green (see Ordering — it does not today)** + a pilot **visual check in AEM**: the panel renders and prints as it did before the swap, with no field appearing or disappearing.
**Max changed lines:** omit — one swap rewrites a `fragRef`, may add several hide lines, and its renames touch every reference in the document, so the count scales with reference density (AAGA_019: 9 swaps, 1 hide, 5 renames). Gate on the fix's `ok` + the re-scan.
**A role suffix is written once, not twice (2026-08-24).** `_guard_duplicate_names` reaches the same
signature panel through two paths — as a member of its own name group, and through
`twin_of[<its partner's row>]` — and neither pass knew the other had run. `sorted(groups.items())`
puts `PN_AHGRP` before `PN_Sign_AHGRP`, so the twin pass suffixed the panel and its own group
suffixed it again: **`PN_Sign_AHGRP_AR_AR` in 19 forms**, plus `PN_Sign_AHGRP_ADR_AR` (AACR_019),
`PN_Sign_AHGRP_ADR_LRP` (AATH_033), `PN_Sign_AHGRP_AP` (AAAL_019) and `PN_SGN_CPGRP_ADR` (AAOE_033).
Nothing failed loudly — the names are addressable and unique, so no rule complained — but they say a
party that does not exist, and the panel they belonged to went unpaired until
PROBLEM-signature-name-fill learned to strip a suffix. Two guards, because one was clearly not
enough: `_guard_duplicate_names` skips an item already carrying a suffix and marks the ones it
suffixes, and `ubs_general_fragments.with_suffix` returns the name unchanged when it already ends in
that token. Verified on a 20-form sample covering every affected shape: **0 double suffixes, chain
converges, second invocation byte-identical, and 2 more name-calc documents written** because the
panels are now pairable.

**A general fragment is added as Content Panel, not Panel (owner directive 2026-08-24).** AEM's
authoring UI calls `ajila-forms-customers/ajila-forms-ubs/components/controls/panel` "Content Panel"
and `fd/af/components/panel` "Panel"; the corpus has no third panel component. Every panel this rule
swaps now gets the UBS one.

Two things came out of implementing it:

- **only one of the three swap paths set the panel type at all.** The partner/signature path set it;
  `standalone` (a small fragment leaving the market library on its own) and `ok` (the correspondence
  table's own swaps) rewrote `fragRef` and left the panel component alone. Nobody could see it
  because PROBLEM-fragment-panel-aem forced every fragment panel to the AEM one a moment later.
  Caught on AAOR_033, whose single ungated swap goes through the `ok` path. All three set it now.
- **PROBLEM-fragment-panel-aem (#24) is retired, so the Content Panel stands.** #24 used to be
  enrolled and forced every fragRef panel back to `fd/af/components/panel`, so in the full chain the
  Content Panel was reverted on the next pass — measured on AARG_033: 9 shared-library panels, all
  back to Panel. That revert has landed (master, 2026-08-24): #24 is retired and
  PROBLEM-panel-type-ubs / #137 was widened to force **every** panel — `fragRef` ones included — to
  the UBS Content Panel, the same component this rule writes. The two rules now agree instead of
  taking turns, so this change has visible effect as soon as the merge lands. It was verified in
  isolation before that: `set_general_fragments.py` alone on a pristine AAOR_033 leaves the swapped
  panel as Content Panel and every untouched panel as it was. The extra pass the old ping-pong cost
  is still why `apply_sweeps`' `--passes` default went 6 -> 8: AAAL_019 was sitting exactly on the
  old cap.

**Corpus fixed: NO.** Verified end to end over 304 packages in simulation; no form committed. **64
forms violate it today**, and the Skip line below names every one of them on one physical line —
`check_regressions.skipped()` reads only that line, and an earlier version wrapped, so it skipped
**zero** forms instead of 64. `Runs automatically: yes` means the rule is wired in, not that the
corpus is fixed.
**Runs automatically:** yes
**Origin:** manual (owner directive — retire the market libraries)
**Tracking:** #123
**Status:** built and verified over all 304 packages (682 swaps, 1652 visibility calls, 554 renames, 242 calc pairs; detector re-run reports `swap: 0`; chain converges and is idempotent). See `Corpus fixed:` above for what is actually deployed — nothing.

**Skip (64 forms, 2026-08-24):** every form still violating this rule; the rollout has not run.
