The corpus guard does not check these forms for this entry; the intake app passes `--no-skip`, so an incoming package is checked in full and a person sees the row. Regenerate after the rollout with `python3 .claude/scripts/find_general_fragments.py | python3 -c "import json,sys;print(' '.join(json.load(sys.stdin)['affected']))"`. AAAA_019 AAAB_019 AAAI_019 AAAM_019 AAAU_019 AABH_019 AABJ_019 AACC_019 AACM_019 AACP_019 AACR_019 AACT_019 AAEE_019 AAEI_019 AAEP_019 AAFM_019 AAFO_019 AAGA_019 AAGB_019 AAGC_019 AAGX_019 AAHM_af_ab_019 AAHQ_019 AAIU_033 AAIV_033 AAJY_019 AAKH_019 AAKN_019 AALU_019 AALU_033 AAMC_019 AAMG_033 AAMR_019 AAMV_019 AANE_019 AANI_019 AANV_019 AAOE_033 AAPD_033 AAPP_033 AAQT_033 AARG_033 AARW_033 AASV_033 AAWW_033 AAXC_019 ABHM_019 ADEC_019 BABJ_019 BAGI_019 BAHH_033 BAHI_019 BANR_033 BANS_019 BARN_019 BARO_033 BAST_019 BAWY_019 BAWZ_033 BBBO_019 BBBS_019 BBDY_033 BBDZ_019 BBFI_033

**Two sibling rules do the rest of the signature work, and they have their own entries above:**
[PROBLEM-signature-name-fill](#problem-signature-name-fill--the-host-form-must-author-the-signer-name-auto-fill)
authors the auto-fill and names the signature panel;
[PROBLEM-signature-title](#problem-signature-title--a-signature-outside-a-repeatable-panel-needs-its-own-heading)
carries the heading. They are separate entries because `run_sweep.py` and `apply_sweeps.py` read
only the first `Detect:`/`Fix:` pair in an entry, so a second fixer buried in this one would never
run — and because they sit ABOVE this entry in the file, which is what makes `apply_sweeps.py`
run the auto-fill before the swap (see Ordering 2).

**Ordering — four dependencies, two of them currently red.**

1. **The repeating-panel archetype rule runs FIRST, enforced per panel by its ownership marker — not by coordinating branches.** The component diff drops every `ST_*` title draw and `BT_*Remove/Add` button *on the assumption that the archetype supplies the label from the panel name plus the instance number* (`ubs_general_fragments.is_noise`). Run first and a migrated signature block loses its qualifier — "Name of legal guardian" becomes a bare "Name" with nothing to restore it. So: **a repeatable panel whose Add/Remove rules do not carry the archetype's `_archetype` marker is deferred**; a marked one is fair game. The marker lives inside the rule JSON on the buttons, so coverage is derived by `archetype_gate.py` — a marked Remove covers the repeatable it rides inside, a marked Add covers the sole repeatable beside it, the panel's own `addButton`/`removeButton` covers it exactly when present, and **a repeatable panel named inside a marked `click` script is covered too**, which is how the signature twin qualifies: it is driven by the data panel's Add/Remove by global name and by design has no buttons of its own. Everything ambiguous or unreadable defers. Owner decision 2026-08-17: **titles live on the host panel** so the repeatable can append the instance number, exactly as `BAVP_033` already does it — which is what makes a single role-neutral signature fragment acceptable (see [signature-fragment-contract.md](signature-fragment-contract.md)). **Intake sequence:** check → run all sweeps (PROBLEM-repeating-panel is ordered before this one in this file, which is the order `apply_sweeps` applies them in) → check again. The swap *does* invalidate archetype output — the promote variant reads the Remove-button name, the panel names and the first input out of the fragment — and it is `apply_sweeps`' fixpoint loop that repairs it, not a second click: the pass after the swap re-runs PROBLEM-repeating-panel, which reads the tree structurally, sees the shared fragment's `"promote": true` and redoes its own work. **Measured 2026-08-19** on a 6-form harness: AAAZ_019 converges in 3 passes with both rules in `applied`, a further invocation applies nothing and leaves every package byte-identical, and the archetype gate goes from 0/33 covered · 0 open fragRefs to 29/33 covered · 20 open. The cap in `apply_sweeps` was raised from 4 passes to 6 to leave slack. Nothing here has to be coordinated by hand.
**Only the repeating-panel archetype's own id opens the gate.** `_archetype` is a shared convention (`archetype.py`) and other sweeps stamp it too — PROBLEM-configurator-reset-on-change writes it on the very components a form configurator decides. Counting any id as coverage would open the gate on a panel this archetype has never normalised, so `archetype_gate.py` matches `repeating-panel` and nothing else, and reads **both** of the archetype's markers: the `_archetype` field and the canonical `// [repeating-panel] Generated automatically.` first line, which is the half that survives a rule being round-tripped through AEM's visual editor.
2. **The naming guard flags 179 of 304 forms after this fixer runs — an exemption must land with this entry.** Verified end to end, both directions: on pristine AAGA_019/AAOM_033/BANR_033/AAPQ_033/AAAB_019 `find_naming_violations.py` reports **0**; after `set_fragment_migration.py` it reports **1** — AAGA_019's `panelcontainerClient`, `expected: PN`, `bucket: raw`, `confidence: high`. The cause is that the shared libraries do not follow the `PREFIX_` convention, so renaming a host field onto a shared name necessarily produces a non-conforming host component: `PN_Name_Individual`→`panelcontainerClient` (169), `TXT_signature`→`textbox_signature` (138), `TXT_OURef`→`txtBoxOURef` (138), `PN_Client_details`→`panelcontainerClient` (12), `PN_Nome`→`panel_forInternalBankUse` (1) — 458 instances across 179 forms. Without an exemption the already-swept **PROBLEM-naming-conventions** guard turns red and `apply_sweeps.py` reverts every swap — the same failure mode PROBLEM-signature-panel-ref documents and solved with `SIGNATURE_CONTRACT_NAMES`. Mirror it: a `SHARED_FRAGMENT_NAMES` exemption in `find_naming_violations.py`, **derived from `data/fragment_correspondence.json`** rather than hand-listed, on the same principle as the signature contract table. (Note `SIGNATURE_CONTRACT_NAMES` already carries `PN_AHGRP` and `PN_Sign_AHGRP`.)
3. **PROBLEM-signature-panel-ref (#80) has had its premise retired by this rule, and is now dormant.** #80 renames a host's repeatable data panel so that a MARKET signature fragment's hardcoded identifier resolves (`RCP_AHRP` -> `PN_AHRP`, because `germany/affrg_AHSignature1` says `PN_AHRP.instanceManager...`). Every signature this rule touches becomes `ubs/affrg_SignatureGeneric1`, which ships its own calc `enabled: false` and has the HOST author the fill -- that is PROBLEM-signature-name-fill's job, and it writes the pairing from the panel names the UBS table dictates. So there is nothing left for #80 to satisfy. **Measured, and the two do not conflict:** `find_signature_panel_ref.py` reports **0 actionable rows on the pristine corpus and 0 on the swept one**, and its review queue *shrinks* as this rule runs -- 171 `flag:no-target` before, 135 after -- because the swap gives those panels the names the generic expects. It stays enrolled while the market libraries still hold 108 forms; once the rollout empties them it can be retired, and its `data/signature_contract.json` cannot go with it -- `signature_name_fill.py` reads that table to know each signature fragment's name-field paths, which is what lets a swapped panel keep its pairing.

4. **This rule's own swap turns the naming guard red for a second reason.** Ordering 2 is about the renames the swap makes; this is the same mechanism seen from the other end. Ten components in the shared libraries carry non-canonical names (`panelcontainerClient`, `panel_bankingRelationship`, `textbox_signature`, `txtBoxOURef`, `panel_forInternalBankUse`), so renaming a host component onto one of them necessarily produces a violation: AAGZ_033 goes from 0 to 1 after the swap alone, before the name-fill runs at all. Either the `SHARED_FRAGMENT_NAMES` exemption of Ordering 2 covers it — it is derived from `data/fragment_correspondence.json`, so it would — or the shared fragments get renamed upstream at UBS, which is the better fix and the slower one.

**Review note (human, per form):** the swap is invisible when it works — the point is that nothing moves. Check that no field appeared (a component the target introduces and the hide call missed) or disappeared, that a swapped panel still prints identically in the DoR, and on signature panels that the signer-name field behaves as it did before: pre-filled where it was pre-filled, empty where it was already empty. A swap that renders correctly but silently stopped auto-filling a name is the failure this rule works hardest to prevent, and it is only visible in AEM.

---

**The 2026-08-20 fragment export, and the bug it caught.** UBS's `global_fragments_final_20_August`
export was diffed against the vendored catalogue: **81 shared fragments, none added, none removed,
and exactly two component-list changes** — both in `global` fragments this rule never targets
(`affrg_global_BeneficialOwner_Entity_Address_…` lost `dateBeneficialOwnerIncorporation` and
`txtBoxBeneficialOwnerPlace`, `affrg_global_ChangeOfName_…` lost three textboxes). So the routing
table needed no change. What the export *did* answer is something `fragment_correspondence.json`
never could, because it records which components a fragment has and never whether they are shown:

    PN_EntityBasic  PN_FormAddress  PN_DOBNationality  PN_DateIncorporation   visible=false
    PN_IndividualBasic  PN_Address                                            visible

**"Hide what the source did not cover" was therefore only half the rule**, and the missing half was
losing data. A kept sub-panel that arrives hidden has to be SHOWN: `germany/affrg_AuthRep` keeps
EntityBasic + DOBNationality and `italy/affrg_LegalRepresentative` keeps EntityBasic, so without a
show call the representative's company name, date of birth and nationality simply never appeared —
the swap rendering and printing correctly while quietly dropping fields, which is the exact failure
this entry's review note calls the one it works hardest to prevent. Measured over the corpus:
**91 `showAFShowDor` calls — 69 × `PN_EntityBasic` in 65 forms, 19 × `PN_DOBNationality`, 3 ×
`PN_DateIncorporation`.** Vendored as `data/fragment_visibility.json` by
`data/extract_fragment_visibility.py`, which resolves nested fragment references through (the
correspondence extractor deliberately stops at a `fragRef`, which is why the sub-panels' contents
were invisible to it).

One level down, the same question: `affrg_AddressGeneric1` ships `TXT_District_AddressBlock_APAC`
**shown** and no market address fragment has such a field, so a swap grows a
"Province/State/District/Region" box out of nowhere — 19 field-level hides (15 × the APAC district,
4 × `TXT_State_AddressBlock`). **Scoped to `kind: "small"` sources only**, and that limit is
deliberate: a composite partner fragment reaches the same sub-panel through a `fragRef` of its own,
so the catalogue holds an override of one field inside a referenced address fragment rather than the
whole of what it showed — `germany/affrg_AuthRep` is recorded as `PN_Address > PN_AddressBlock >
DD_Country_AddressBlock` and nothing else. Treating that as complete hid the street, the postal code
and the city on 19 instances. The price of the limit is one extra field on screen for a partner
panel keeping `PN_Address`, against silently deleting six; it is the same limit the old fixer had,
and it is what the AEM visual check should look at.

**`SIGN_Signature` ships `visible=false` without `dorExclusion`** — hidden in the form, present in
the DoR. Owner confirmation 2026-08-20: intentional, the form is printed and signed on paper. The
sweep does not touch it.

**Signature panel naming, settled 2026-08-20.** `AAOS_033` and `AAHA_019` disagreed
(`PN_Sign_CPGRP` vs `PN_SGN_CPGRP`); the decision is **the table where it speaks**, so
`PN_SGN_CPG`/`PN_SGN_CPGRP` for the contractual partner and `PN_Sign_AHG`/`PN_Sign_AHGRP` for
partner-to-partner, which is exactly what the hand-built `AAHA_019` carries. That forced a change in
PROBLEM-signature-name-fill: it derives `PN_Sign_<data panel>`, so it read `PN_SGN_CPGRP` as
non-conforming and renamed it every pass — the two rules would never have reached a fixpoint. It now
accepts **both** spellings when pairing by panel name (`SIGN_PREFIXES`) and leaves a name that
already states its data panel alone.

**bindRef is out of scope, and that is the point of the whole directive.** `AAHA_019` carries
`bindRef="/UBSAF_BAVP/PNAHRP"` and `"/UBSAF_BAVP/AccountHolderSignature"` on its panels while the
form has no `schemaRef` at all and the prefix is another form's — copied from `BAVP_033`. Owner
2026-08-20: this feedback tool never sets `bindRef`; the completed form goes to a UBS tool that
assigns it from the XSD, **which is why the UBS fragments are mandated in the first place — they
carry the XSD internally**, and the germany/italy ones do not. The sweep neither writes nor removes
`bindRef`.

**Two parties of the same class in one form — solved with a role suffix (Marco, 2026-08-20).**
The UBS table's convention carries no instance number, so a Germany form with an authorised
representative **and** a legal guardian wants `PN_AHGRP` twice, and one with two contractual
partners wants `PN_CPG` twice. A duplicate name is not cosmetic: every name in a generated script is
an identifier the AEM runtime resolves globally, so neither panel could be addressed, and
PROBLEM-repeating-panel reports the shape as `duplicate-panel-name` in its own right.

UBS's own hand-built forms already answer it, and not with a number: `AAHA_019` calls the guardian's
signature panel `PN_Sign_LG` and UBS Europe SE's `PN_Sign_Responsible` — a short **role token**.
Owner decision: follow that pattern, and **propose it back to UBS as the convention**, on the
grounds that it is a shape that repeats across their forms and works. So the FIRST panel of a class
keeps the plain name, every later one takes `_<ROLE>`, and its signature twin takes the SAME suffix
— which is what keeps PROBLEM-signature-name-fill able to pair them by name (`PN_AHGRP_AR` /
`PN_Sign_AHGRP_AR`). The token comes from `ROLE_SUFFIX` in `ubs_general_fragments.py`, keyed by the
market fragment and using the abbreviations this corpus already puts in panel names (`LG`, `AR`,
`LR`, `AP`, `AS`, `BO`, `CA`, `Responsible`, `ADR`, `FOA`, …). When two panels of a class come from
the *same* fragment — one Germany form has `affrg_IndividualBasic1` as the client and again as the
guardian — the fragment's token collides with itself, so the panel's own name and the wrappers
around it answer instead (`PN_LGA` → `LGA`), which is the very token that told the classifier they
were different parties. Anything that still collides goes to a person: inventing `_2` would be a
convention nobody agreed.

Measured effect: **swaps 525 → 682**, `manual` on the pristine corpus **42 → 17**, and 98 panels
take a suffix (dominated by `PN_AHGRP_AR` / `PN_Sign_AHGRP_AR`). `find_naming_violations` exempts
the suffixed forms too, through `ubs_general_fragments.is_generic_panel_name` rather than a flat
set — flagged, they would make `apply_sweeps` revert the swap.


**A latent defect in PROBLEM-repeating-panel's `phase_bounds` was fixed while building this rule (2026-08-20):** its dissolve branch replaced a wrapper's open tag where it had to replace the whole element and stacked edits on one span, producing malformed XML on **21 of 304 packages**. Unreachable on the pristine corpus; reachable only because every UBS generic carries `promote: true`. Fixed. Details in that rule's entry.

**What the FORM says about visibility outranks the catalogue (Marco's AAOV_033 test, 2026-08-20).**
`SOURCES[...]["keep"]` says which sub-panels a market fragment COVERS. That is a property of the
fragment, identical in every form referencing it, and it is not the same question as what THIS form
shows. AAOV_033 references `italy/affrg_LegalRepresentative` — catalogued as keeping
`PN_EntityBasic` — and then hides half of it with a script of its own:
`PN_EntityBasic.visible = false; PN_EntityBasic.dorExclusion = true; PN_AddressClient.visible =
false; PN_AddressClient.dorExclusion = true;`. So the swap emitted
`showAFShowDor(this.PN_EntityBasic)` and said nothing about `PN_Address` (which the generic ships
shown), and the converted panel grew a company name and a full address block the original showed
neither of. Marco: *"aparecía sin ocultar algunos campos que debían estar ocultos, tanto el de
EntityBasic como el de Address … debemos eliminar el script antiguo, de ahí cogemos que deben estar
ocultos y entonces lo añadimos con las nuevas funciones."* The per-instance truth is therefore
**the catalogue's `keep`, minus what the host hides, plus what the host explicitly shows**, and the
old script is **deleted** once its evidence has been read. Both halves matter: the old statements
name the OLD fragment's components (`PN_AddressClient` has no counterpart in the generic, so it
throws at runtime and takes the rest of the Initialize rule with it), and they sit on the same
(field, Initialize) slot as the block this fixer writes but under a dead `field` path, so
`build_hide_edit` cannot recognise them as the same slot and appends a second document beside
them — the two `PN_AHGRP - Initialize` rules Marco's screenshot shows, one hiding the old
fragment's panels and one hiding the new one's. Measured over the 304 packages: **234 such rule
documents in 111 forms**, and **173 of them hang on the market fragment panel itself**, which makes
the scoping exact rather than a guess (6 more hang on a wrapper above it, 9 are read but left in
place because they also do other work, and the rest belong to a form-configurator branch and are
not touched — those are PROBLEM-configurator-reset-on-change's nodes). Names go through the same
`SLOTS` / `FIELD_MAP` tables a field rewrite uses, and the LAST segment of the dotted path is what
is being hidden: `PN_AHRP.PN_EntityBasic.TXT_CompanyName.visible = false` hides the company name,
not the account holder, and comes out as a field-level call. A document is deleted only when it
hangs on the swapped panel AND every statement in it is a visibility statement this rule
re-expresses; anything else is read for evidence and left alone, with a note on the plan item and a
row in the detector's `notes`. Evidence is **hide-only in the risky direction**: a host `show` adds
to `keep`, a host `hide` removes from it, and nothing is ever inferred from silence.
**What the 2026-08-20 germany/italy/ch export settled.** Three outcomes, all still in force: the four generics are unchanged (verified, not assumed); `_extra_fields` no longer guesses at a fragment's extra fields, it reads them; and the missing-definition list is down to two fragments. `fragment_correspondence.json` is deliberately NOT regenerated from this export — the vendored data has drifted from the zips and regenerating folds 8 unrelated changes into whatever else you are doing. `afforms_ch_fragmentlib` is a fifth library and is correctly left alone.

**Two guards the export made necessary, both measured.** *Existence, not visibility, decides
whether the source has a counterpart.* `italy/affrg_italy_personal_details` ships
`TXT_NomeDellaSocieta` with `visible=false` and shows it from its own client-type radio, so reading
"visible" as "has one" hid the generic's `TXT_CompanyName` for good and would have rendered the
company branch of `RB_CPType` as an empty panel. *And a kept sub-panel is never emptied field by
field:* where every leaf of a panel the source covers would be hidden, the disagreement is about
the panel rather than its fields — `italy/affrg_ClienteRepeatingPanel` keeps `PN_EntityBasic` and
has no company field anywhere in its tree, and hiding the generic's only company field there leaves
a heading above nothing. With both guards the field-level hides across the whole table are exactly
21 calls on 20 sources: `TXT_District_AddressBlock_APAC` (20), `TXT_State_AddressBlock` (5, the
sources whose address block has neither) and `DD_Nationality` (1, `germany/affrg_Authorized_Person1`,
which shows a date of birth and no nationality). *A host hide that covers a whole sub-panel is read
as a panel hide:* AAAN_019's representative panel hides `PN_EntityBasic`, `PN_Address`, `DATE_Birth`
and `DD_Nationality`, and the last two are the whole of `PN_DOBNationality` — read field by field
that came out as `showAFShowDor(this.PN_DOBNationality)` followed by two calls hiding both of its
fields, three lines contradicting each other.
**Where the evidence may come from, and the three misreadings that fixed the boundary.** The
documents in the corpus that say anything about a generic's sub-panels are 200 `fd:init` rules made
of nothing but visibility statements, 58 `fd:init` rules that also do other work, and 29
`fd:valueCommit` / `fd:click` / `fd:visible` rules, none of them clean. Only the first group is read.
A click handler or a configurator branch states a CONDITION, not a default: AAGF_019's
`BT_AddClient` says `hideAFHideDor(PN_AHRP.PN_EntityBasic)` under the comment `/** For Client **/`
and another button shows it again for a company, so reading it as a default hid the only field that
panel had. `dorExclusion = false` is a SHOW — it means include in the Document of Record — and
falling through to "hide" for an unrecognised shape hid all six sub-panels of the CONTRACTING PARTY
on six forms (AAAE_019, AAAL_019, AAGF_019, AAGG_019, ABRS_019, ADDS_019), i.e. a person block
rendering nothing; the four shapes are now named explicitly and anything else is ignored. And a
panel the form both shows and hides is kept, the show winning, because losing a field the user can
fill is the failure this rule works hardest to avoid. What survives all three: **169 instances in
about 100 forms take their visibility from the form's own Initialize rule (378 sub-panel hides), and
170 old hide documents are deleted.** Where applying the evidence would hide EVERYTHING the fragment
covered, the instance goes to a person instead (2: AAHM_af_ab_019, AATH_033) — a market panel named
after one of the generic's own sub-panels is the same identifier to the AF runtime, so
`hideAFHideDor(PN_EntityBasic)` on an ancestor cannot be told from "hide my fragment's entity
block", and a blank person block is not a thing to emit on a guess.
**A row that needs a field the fragment does not have (Marco's AAOV_033 tests, 2026-08-20/21).**
AAOV_033's account holder repeats a market fragment plus a hand-added panel holding Data di nascita
and Luogo di nascita. The promotion hands the repeating role from the wrapper down to the fragment
panel, and that hand-added panel then falls OUT of the row: it renders once, above an Add button that
adds rows without it. **A UBS fragment panel cannot hold added content** — Marco, after the first
attempt nested it there: *"PN_CPGRP es un fragmento de UBS. No puede tener contenido añadido dentro
sin haberlo embebido antes."* So the shape is a content panel around both:

    PN_CPGRP {minOccur/maxOccur}                       <- the row
        BT_Remove
        PN_CPG [fragRef=…ContractualPartnerGeneric1]
        PN_c153fb21 { Data di nascita, Luogo di nascita }
    BT_AddCPG

**The table's two names land the other way round, and that is what makes the shape work.** The
generic ships its own Remove, whose two Click scripts are `removeInstance(PN_SGN_CPGRP);
removeInstance(PN_CPGRP);` and `var lenARRP = this.parent.instanceManager.instances.length; …`.
`BT_RemoveCPG` sits at the fragment's root, so `this.parent` is the panel carrying the `fragRef`: the
second script needs THAT panel to be the repeatable, and here it is not. The first still resolves,
because `PN_CPGRP` is the wrapper — the thing a row IS — so the wrapper takes the table's repeating
name and the fragment inside it the single one. The signature twin then pairs with the row exactly as
on every other form (`PN_CPGRP -> PN_SGN_CPGRP`); keyed off the fragment panel it wrote
`PN_AHRP -> PN_Sign_AHRP` instead. The fragment's own Remove is hidden from the host
(`hideAFHideDor(this.BT_RemoveCPG)`, in the visibility block this rule already writes on that panel)
and PROBLEM-repeating-panel writes a `BT_Remove` on the wrapper — the treatment it already applies to
every non-fragment repeatable.
**What counts as row content**, measured over the swept corpus (47 stranded siblings, 19 repeatables,
16 forms). Three classes are NOT, and each one showed up in the measurement: a node with a `fragRef`
(13 — another instance of a fragment this rule owns, or a stale inlined copy; this is what keeps
AAGB_019's `PN_IndividualBasic_Address` and AAGC_019's `PN_IndividualAddress` out, both copies of the
old fragment's own name-and-address block); a node named after one of the generic's own sub-panels or
after a panel this rule generates (`PN_CPG`, `PN_AHGRP_ADR`); and a heading (11 — a
`titledraw`/`textdraw`, or a panel holding nothing else, since this rule owns headings and one
repeated per instance is a defect). The container also has to be the row's OWN — the Add button that
adds instances of the repeatable is a sibling there — which is what tells a repeatable's wrapper apart
from an ordinary panel holding a repeat next to unrelated fields: AAOV_033's
`PN_LegalEntityContainer` keeps `TXT_NomeDellaSocieta` beside the legal representative's repeat
container, and the company name is never dragged into the representative's row because the Add for
that row lives one level down.
**Two collisions this shape creates, both measured before being fixed.** *The fragment panel's name:*
with the wrapper claiming `PN_CPGRP`, the table's single `PN_CPG` falls to the fragment inside, and a
form that ALSO has a standalone partner of that class already uses it — the duplicate guard refused
both, taking AACR_019 to 9 `manual` rows, AACP_019 to 5 and AAUP_019 to 3. The table is kept where it
can speak (`PN_CPG` when free) and only where it cannot does the panel take `<row>_Fragment`, which
is the converter's own name for this node (`PN_LRP_Fragment`, `PN_ARP_Fragment` are all over the
pristine corpus). *A second Remove:* AAOV_033 has one in the wrapper and one inside the market
fragment panel, and `phase_lift_removes` hoisted the second into the row beside the one this rule
writes; the two rules then took turns creating and deleting it, six passes without converging. A
Remove inside a `fragRef` panel is the fragment's own, or a stale copy the consolidation is about to
empty away, so it is never hoisted — `_inside_fragment_panel`, applied in the detector and the fixer
alike, since each computes its own lift list.
**The name-calc is reactive, and the rename it used to ask for is gone (Marco's AAOV_033 test, 2026-08-21).** Two halves of one defect. `repeating_fragments.json` records `panel: PN_CPGRP` — the name the generic hard-codes for **the panel it repeats in**, since its own internal Remove calls `removeInstance(PN_CPGRP)`. Where the row's own extra content keeps the WRAPPER repeating, that panel IS the wrapper, and PROBLEM-repeating-panel asked for the name on the fragment panel anyway (`rename fragment panel PN_CPG to PN_CPGRP`). Done as instructed, the form ended up with two nodes called `PN_CPGRP`, which no script can address. The check now skips a fragment panel that is not itself the repeatable — the wrapper carries the name, and the fragment inside takes the table's single variant, which that rule does not own.
And the name-calc did not notice. `signature_name_fill.plan()` compared the WRITE path of an existing document of ours against the one it would generate now — which is what makes a fragRef swap safe — but never the READ side, so a calc still saying `PN_CPGRP.instanceManager.instances[i].PN_CPG.PN_IndividualBasic.…` after `PN_CPG` had been renamed away counted as "already filled by our own script" and the rule reported the form done while the name never arrived. A document of ours now counts as done only if **all three** hold: the write path this pass would write, the READ path it would read, and every panel it addresses through `.instanceManager` still present in the form (`panels_addressed`). Anything else and the pair is `stale`, the flag that already makes the fixer rewrite it. Verified both ways on AAOV_033: renaming the fragment panel to a free name regenerates the calc against the new path, and renaming it onto the wrapper's name — the duplicate that was actually created — is refused by the shared-name guard rather than written wrong.
**The guard that came out of this:** `set_repeating_panel` counts the controls the user can fill
before and after (`input_names`) and, if one is missing, writes NOTHING and reports `ok: false` with
`lost_inputs`. Deletions this rule is allowed to make are buttons and the numbered title families; an
input disappearing is always a bug, and moving nodes is exactly the kind of edit that can lose one.
`apply_sweeps` refuses to write a form the fixer reports as not ok, so the failure mode is a reported
error instead of a field nobody notices is gone.
**A `field` path of ours that no longer resolves is rewritten, every pass (2026-08-21).** A rule
document's `field` is the SOM path AEM stores to say which component the rule belongs to, and AEM
requires it absolute. A manual panel rename rewrites 19 of the 20 references by itself — this rule
regenerates the Add/Remove scripts, PROBLEM-configurator-reset and PROBLEM-signature-name-fill
regenerate theirs, and `signature_contract.apply_renames` carries a rename into every `fd:*` body —
but not our own `fd:init` `field` on the swapped panel, because after the swap that panel carries a
UBS `fragRef` and this rule has no further business with it. The body keeps working (it is written
`this.PN_EntityBasic`, never the panel's name, which is the reactivity Marco asked for and the reason
a rename does not break the code); the stored path does not, and a panel with a dead `field` ends up
carrying **two** Initialize documents in the editor. `stale_init_paths` compares the stored path
against the panel's real ancestry on every pass and `refresh_path_edits` rewrites the value in place —
the body is untouched. The detector reports it as a `stale-path` row so the form does not read as
clean while it is true.

**A signature whose fragment was INLINED has no `fragRef` to route (Marco's AAOV_033 test,
2026-08-20).** An earlier instruction was to embed a signature fragment so the party's role could be
written into the field labels, which is why AAOV_033's client signature says *"Firma del titolare
del conto"* and *"Nome del titolare del conto"* instead of the fragment's generic *"Firma"* /
*"Nome"*. The cost is that the panel carries no `fragRef`, so every rule that routes by fragment is
blind to it: no generic fragment, no name-calc, no numbered title — while its twin on the same page,
which kept its `fragRef`, gets all three. Marco: *"debemos intentar identificar que esta firma se ha
embebido y como sabemos que el cliente debe tener una firma (por lo general) debemos usar un
fragmento generic signature y simplemente la regla de repeating panel debe añadir el título … y
debe tener su name-calc y demás."* So `collect_instances` now also yields the panels that ARE an
inlined signature, under the synthetic source `(embedded)/signature`, and they are planned like any
other signature. Detection is by shape, not by name: a panel with no `fragRef` anywhere in its
subtree, holding at least one signature component, whose every other field is one of the four
`affrg_SignatureGeneric1` has (place, date, name) — `SIGNATURE_FIELD_PATTERNS` decides that, the
same table the field rewrite uses. Climbing stops at the panel that contributes the fields, which on
AAOV_033 keeps `RCP_SignatureIndividual` (already the repeatable) and leaves `PN_165356b3` alone,
that being the panel carrying the form-configurator visibility rule. The party is read off the host
with the same evidence ladder a `context` partner block uses, minus the twin rung (a signature IS
the twin): panel name and two wrapper levels, then its own title, then an ancestor's, then a
heading, and finally **elimination** — if every other signature in the form is accounted for and
exactly one partner class has no signature, the unnamed one is that party's. That is the *"por lo
general el cliente tiene una firma"* inference stated as an elimination rather than as an
assumption about the client.
**The scope is deliberately narrow, and the measurement is why.** 190 panels in the corpus match the
inlined-signature shape; **73 can be attributed to a party** (38 by name, 18 by elimination, 13 by a
heading, 4 by an ancestor title) and **117 cannot**. Most of those 117 are not a market signature at
all: `PN_GenericSignature` (23 instances) and `PN_Body` (21) inside a
`PN_affrg_AffrgSignaturegeneric1_…` wrapper are UBS's OWN generic signature fragment, already
inlined by the converter. So an embedded panel is swapped **only when it ends up linked to a partner
block of its class** — which is the case Marco described, and the only one where the swap pays for
itself: the name-calc has something to read and the role has somewhere to go (the panel title).
Everything else embedded is left exactly as it is and reported in the detector's `embedded` list,
because there is no `fragRef` to retire, nothing to fill the name from, and a swap would delete the
labels embedding existed to add. Reported rather than `manual`: 117 new rows for a person would have
buried the 17 real ones.

---

## PROBLEM-signature-hardcoded-family — Hand-numbered signature blocks become one repeatable panel
**Symptom:** a signature block duplicated and hand-numbered — `PN_UBS_Signature_1` beside
`PN_UBS_Signature_2`, `PN_Signature1` beside `PN_Signature2` — is a 1:1 copy of the source PDF:
somebody needed two signature slots and duplicated the block, in 4 of the 6 cases by **embedding**
UBS's fragment so they could edit it. The structure we want is **one** general signature fragment in a
Content Panel at `minOccur = maxOccur = N`; AEM numbers the instances itself (at N > 1 it draws "1."
and "2.", at N = 1 no number — confirmed in AEM 2026-08-24). **6 families in 6 forms: `AAED_019`, `AAIV_033`, `AANN_019`, `BAGS_019`, `BAWY_019`, `BAWZ_033`.** 4 embedded,
2 fragment-referenced.
**Detect:** script: `find_signature_family.py`  _(a family = two or more signature blocks whose names
share a stem and end in consecutive numbers from 1. The number must be one or two digits and the set
must be exactly 1..N — without that, two panels ending in the same hash digits read as a family
(`PN_affrg_AffrgSignaturegeneric1_9d817379` ends in "79"). Also derives the TITLE: the blocks' own
wording with the number stripped, from the panel's `jcr:title`, its wrapper's, or the title draw above
it — `UBS Europe 1` + `UBS Europe 2` -> `UBS Europe`. A sentence is not a heading, so anything ending
in `.`/`:`/`;` or longer than 40 characters is ignored.)_
**Fix:** manual: replace the numbered blocks with one general signature fragment in a Content Panel at
`minOccur=maxOccur=N`, rewire whatever named the deleted panel, and use the title the detector
derived — or name it yourself where the row says TITLE UNKNOWN.
**Verify:** a re-scan reports the form clean + a visual check in AEM: the panel draws its title and
numbers the instances 1..N.
**No fixer, by design.** The change deletes a panel, re-points another and rewires every reference to
the one that went. Six edits by a person are cheaper and safer than a fixer nobody can fully verify,
and the detector already says exactly what to do for each. Revisit if the count grows.
**Titles are usually missing, and that is reported rather than guessed:** of the 6 families **2 yield
a title** (`AAIV_033` -> "UBS Signature", `AANN_019` -> "Gepfrüft/durchgeführt") and **4 yield
nothing**. Those 4 say TITLE UNKNOWN so the intake app asks a person, the same bargain
PROBLEM-repeating-panel makes with `manual-title`.
**Runs automatically:** yes
**Corpus fixed:** NO — 6 families in 6 forms, none done.
**Origin:** manual (owner directive 2026-08-24 — stop the 1:1 PDF copies, use the general structure)
**Tracking:** #TBD
**Status:** flag-only rule, measured and verified 2026-08-24 over all 304 packages.
**Skip (6 forms, 2026-08-24):** the whole affected set — there is no fixer to clear it. THE FORM LIST MUST STAY ON THIS LINE: `check_regressions.skipped()` reads only the Skip line itself. Regenerate with `find_signature_family.py | python3 -c "import json,sys;print(' '.join(json.load(sys.stdin)['affected']))"`. AAED_019 AAIV_033 AANN_019 BAGS_019 BAWY_019 BAWZ_033

---

## PROBLEM-signature-title — RETIRED 2026-08-24 (the panel supplies its own heading)
**Retired.** Every signature component is now a **repeatable Content Panel**, and a form with a
single signature gets `minOccur="1" maxOccur="1"`. AEM draws the panel's title and — confirmed by
Marco in AEM — draws **no** "1." beside it at that bound. So the heading arrives from
PROBLEM-repeating-panel's archetype for every signature, repeatable or not, and nothing has to insert
an h4 by hand.

What this replaces: a rule that inserted one `titledraw` before a non-repeatable signature block (22
headings across 17 forms; 250 signature `fragRef`s, 221 already inside a repeatable, 29 outside).
`signature_title.py`, `find_signature_title.py`, `set_signature_title.py`,
`data/signature_titles.json` and `data/extract_signature_titles.py` are deleted, and
`signature_name_fill.target_key()` went with them — it existed for this rule alone.

**The one thing that had to change for it to work:** `find_repeating_panel` read a panel with
occurrence bounds nobody can add to as a dead panel and skipped it — which is exactly the
`min=max=1` signature. See "Equal bounds are a decision, not a dead panel" under
PROBLEM-repeating-panel.

**Runs automatically:** no — the tooling ignores this entry entirely.
**Status:** retired — no detector, no fixer, **not enrolled**. Kept so the decision stays findable.

## PROBLEM-metadata-languages — Metadata summary control: language list + master language must match the languages the form was issued in
**Symptom:** the hidden metadata control in the summary panel (`sling:resourceType="ajila-forms-customers/ajila-forms-ubs/components/controls/metadata"`, `visible="false"`, inside `summarypanel`) carries two language fields that disagree with the languages the form was actually issued in: `formrange_afmasterlanguage` on the control, and `formrange_language` (the comma-separated list) on every `<entities>` direct-child item. **65 of 304 packages affected** (61 change the list, 27 the master); 239 already correct. Both attributes already exist on all 304 packages, so the fix only ever rewrites a value — it never inserts an attribute or touches node structure.
**The rule (owner-confirmed 2026-08-20 — supersedes the 2026-08-18 rule, see the revision note):** the list is exactly the languages the form was *issued* in, which is exactly the set of source PDFs delivered for it:
```
languages = { source-PDF languages }          forms/issued/<F>/<F>_<LANG>.pdf
            { the region's language }         when the form has NO source PDF at all
master    = that language if there is exactly one; otherwise the language the form's
            own text is written in, when the form ships it; else EN; else the region's
            language (019 -> DE, 033 -> IT) when the form ships that
```
**The master is the language the form was authored in (owner directive 2026-09-02, superseding the region rule for multilingual forms).** A form issued in several languages is written once and translated, so its master is EN — `DE` only when its own texts really are German. Reported on AABF_019, which is written in English, translated into German and Spanish, and was having `DE` written over its correct `EN` purely because it is a 019 package. **The dictionaries are still not the signal, and cannot be**: the engine's own UI strings are English masters and this repo writes English masters for panel titles and Add labels, so a German-authored form legitimately carries a `de` dictionary that translates English keys — measured, "the form's own text is translated into L" contradicts the text itself on AANI_019 and is contaminated on 174 of 321 packages. The evidence is the form's **own inline text**: `base_of` asks `repeatable_add_label.base_language`, the same vote PROBLEM-repeating-panel and PROBLEM-repeatable-add-label use to decide a title's language, so a form cannot be German for one rule and English for another. The reading is reported as `base` on every form, flagged or not, so a disagreement is visible rather than silent. **What it moves: 83 forms, all master-only (81 `DE→EN`, 2 `IT→EN` — AAXC_033, ABFG_033); no language list changes.** Five multilingual forms keep a non-EN master because their text really is German: AAMN_019, AAMP_019 (`DE,EN,SP`), AANI_019, AANR_019, AANS_019 (`DE,EN`) — `Niederlassung`, `Geburtsname`, `Bankbeziehung`. Single-language forms are untouched: one language is still its own master.
Regional variants collapse (`de-ch`→DE, `es`→SP, `en-us`/`en-gb`→EN, `it-ch`→IT, `fr-ch`→FR); the `DEFAULT` pseudo-language is dropped (4 forms carry it). List order is EN-first-then-alphabetical, matching the owner's `EN,DE,IT,SP` example — but order is **cosmetic**: nothing parses this value and the corpus already mixes `DE,EN` with `EN,IT`, so the detector compares language **sets** and never flags a form merely to reorder it. **`master ∈ languages` holds for all 304 packages** under this rule, which the previous one could not manage.
**The dictionaries are deliberately NOT a signal — that was the defect.** Until 2026-08-20 the rule unioned in "locales with a genuine translation", where *genuine* meant a dictionary key no earlier sweep injected. A key is not a translation: the conversion writes a key set for every locale the source carries, and `sling:message` is then left empty or echoing the key. **AAIA_019** is the reference case — `fr.xml` and `it.xml` hold **81 keys each with 0 actual translations**, which is how FR and IT got into the list of a form issued only in DE/EN/SP (`de` 57, `de-ch` 45, `es` 56 real entries; `en`/`sp` hold nothing but #72 residue). The dictionaries also cannot ever name the form's own language — nothing is translated into the language it is written in — so they can only narrow a list, never complete one. `metadata_languages.translated_langs()` survives as an **audit aid only**: the detector reports it on flagged forms, so a form carrying real translations its issued-language list does not name stays visible. **34 of the 65 affected forms are in that state** (AAAI_019 is issued DE+EN but really is translated into IT and SP; AAHD_019 is issued DE/EN/SP and translated into all five). Deciding whether such a form should be *re-issued* with more PDFs or have the translations dropped is an owner call per form, not something this sweep infers.
**The source PDFs sit NEXT TO the package.** `forms/issued/<F>/<F>_<LANG>.pdf` is the only detector input in this repo that is not inside the ZIP, so **any one-form harness must carry those filenames** (bytes are irrelevant — only the `_<LANG>.pdf` suffix is parsed). `scripts/intake/core.py` does this in `_place_source_pdfs`, called from both `_check_harness` and `_bulk_harness`. Without it the intake app reports "remove EN" on a form that is green in the repo, `apply_sweeps` (which runs in the real worktree, where the PDFs exist) writes the language straight back, and the check can **never** go green — the exact loop reported for AAIA_019 on 2026-08-20. Parity test: on a 30-form sample the harness matched the repo verdict 30/30 with the stand-ins and 19/30 without.
**Reference:** the owner's directive above plus the **complete source-PDF delivery** of 2026-08-20 (`OneDrive_1_20.8.2026.zip`, 690 files, `<Country>/<Tranch>/<FORM>_<LANG>.pdf`) — a strict superset of what the repo held (0 repo-only files), which is what makes "exactly the PDFs" a usable rule. 100 files were added, creating 61 form directories; 584 of the 587 pre-existing files were byte-identical. **AAIA_019 → `EN,DE,SP`, master `DE`.**
**Detect:** script: `find_metadata_languages.py`  _(affected = the derived language **set** or any control's master differs. Buckets `ok` / `fix` / `no_metadata` — every form in scope derives a target, so there is no `no_evidence` bucket any more. Emits per-form `current`, `derived`, `pdf_langs`, `from_pdfs`, `why`, plus `translated_langs` as an audit field on flagged forms. `--form <F>`, `--verbose`. Idempotent: reports 0 after the fix.)_
**Fix:** script: `set_metadata_languages.py`  _(quote-aware open-tag rewrite of `formrange_afmasterlanguage` on every metadata control and `formrange_language` on every entity item; edits applied back-to-front so spans stay valid. Parses the result with ElementTree and refuses to write malformed XML. `--dry-run`. Prints `{inner, form, langs, master, pdf_langs, from_pdfs, controls, controls_changed, items, items_changed, well_formed, ok}`; `ok` = the form reads back the derived set and master on every control. Nothing is skipped. Idempotent.)_
**Verify:** the fix's own `ok` (the derived value differs per form, so no single `must` regex can express it) + must-not: `formrange_language="[^"]*DEFAULT`  _(guards the pseudo-language from being written back)_
**Max changed lines:** 8   _(2 attributes per metadata control; `form_diff` counts an in-place edit as remove+add. Single-control forms measure 4 or 2 when only one attribute moves; the 9 two-control forms reach 6 and at most 8.)_
**Runs automatically:** no  _(held un-enrolled from the 2026-09-02 rule revision until its rollout lands, exactly as the 2026-08-20 one was: 83 forms violate the new rule, and enrolling now would put the CI backstop red on all of them. The intake app applies the rule regardless — it runs every entry with a `Fix: script:` — so an incoming form is still repaired. Re-enrol the moment the rollout PR merges.)_
**Origin:** manual (owner directive 2026-09-02: "si tiene múltiples idiomas debería ser EN, solo debería ser DE si los textos y su idioma base es DE"; before that, 2026-08-20: "we just want to ensure that the language list is exactly as in the input pdfs. if there is no pdf at all, only list the master language derived from the region code")
**Tracking:** #121
**Status:** swept 2026-08-20 (rule revised + 65/65 fixed + deployed, 0 flagged; PR #130 merged as `f7054f6`).** Detector reports **0 affected on merged master** (304 ok) and `check_regressions.py` is green, so the CI guard is honestly enrolled.
**Enrolment:** done on 2026-08-20, immediately after PR #130 merged. The `Runs automatically:` field — before it existed, the word "swept" on the `Status:` line — is what enrols a rule in `check_regressions.py` *and* what lets `apply_sweeps.py` repair an incoming form, so the entry was deliberately held un-enrolled from the rule revision until the rollout landed — enrolling earlier would have put the corpus backstop red on all 65. Note what the enrolment now covers: the derivation reads the **source PDF filenames next to each package**, so a form whose PDFs are missing from the repo derives its region's language alone and CI will say so. The 100-file PDF delivery of 2026-08-20 is committed on master (`35f0463`) for exactly that reason.
**Rollout verified in the live AEM, exhaustively — not sampled.** All **66** metadata controls across the 65 forms (AAQD_033 carries two) were read back out of the running instance and compared with the derivation: **66 matches, 0 mismatches, 0 unreachable.** Each node's JCR path is computed from its own package and fetched at `<path>.2.json`; that depth-2 fetch returns the control's `formrange_afmasterlanguage` and its `entities` children's `formrange_language` in one request, which sidesteps both traps from the 2026-08-18 pass (`guideContainer.-1.json` returns HTTP 300 on larger forms, and a QueryBuilder search on `formrange_language` 500s because the property is unindexed).
**Rolled out in 4 chunks of 15/15/15/14** (`--only`) after the pilot, not one `--continue`: run_sweep commits once at the end of a run, so a single 59-form run dying late loses every form it had already deployed. All four chunks were clean — 0 flagged across the whole sweep, `delta:skip` throughout (engine MCP not built; a pure attribute-value rewrite cannot introduce a schema violation, and the fixer parses every edited XML with ElementTree before writing). A 15-form chunk takes about 35 seconds.
**Pilot:** **6/65 on 2026-08-20** — PR #130, all `verify:ok deploy:ok diff:ok`, 0 flagged, `delta:skip` throughout (engine MCP not built; a pure attribute-value rewrite cannot introduce a schema violation). Hand-picked to cover every variant of the revised rule rather than the default first-six: **AAAI_019** (list-only shrink `EN,DE,IT,SP`→`EN,DE`, and it really is translated into IT and SP — the audit case), **AAEI_019** (collapse to a single language, `EN,DE,IT,SP`→`DE`, translated into four), **AAQD_033** (the two-metadata-control form, both normalised to `IT`), **AAFN_033** (master-only, `EN`→`IT`, one IT PDF, no translations), **BAUO_019** (the corpus's only `DE`→`EN` master move, list `EN,DE,SP`→`EN,SP`), **BANS_019** (no source PDF → region fallback, `EN,DE,FR,IT,SP`→`DE`, the largest reduction). **Values read back out of the running AEM**, not trusted from the install return code: each metadata node's JCR path was computed from its package and fetched (`<path>.2.json`) — **7 controls, 7 matches, 0 mismatches** (AAQD_033 counts twice). Remaining: 59.
**Review this pilot in the AEM editor dialog, not the rendered form** (Properties → the metadata control → Language Subset / Master Language): `…/editor.html/content/forms/af/afforms_germany_all/af_aa/AF_AAAI.html`, `…/AF_AAEI.html`, `afforms_italy_all/af_aa/AF_AAQD.html`, `…/AF_AAFN.html`, `afforms_germany_all/af_ba/AF_BAUO.html`, `…/AF_BANS.html`. The two judgement calls to confirm before `--continue` are **BANS_019** (a form with no PDF losing four languages) and **AAAI_019** (a form losing IT and SP from its list while still shipping those translations).
**What the revision changes on the corpus:** 61 lists and 27 masters (`EN→DE` 15, `EN→IT` 11, `DE→EN` 1), 65 forms in total: 38 list-only, 23 list+master, 4 master-only. The 2026-08-18 rollout's own numbers (224 forms) are not a subset of these — that pass wrote the union of PDFs and key-presence dictionaries, so most of its forms are already correct under the stricter rule and only these 65 move.
**The region fallback removes languages, on purpose.** 25 of the 65 affected forms have no source PDF at all and collapse to their region's language alone: BANS_019 `EN,DE,FR,IT,SP`→`DE`, BBBO_019 `EN,SP`→`DE`, BBDZ_019 `EN,DE,SP`→`DE`, and 15 B-series forms `EN`→`DE`/`IT`. That is the owner's instruction and it is information-destroying by design — if any of those forms is genuinely issued in more than its region's language, the remedy is to deliver its source PDFs, not to widen the rule. AAHM_af_ab_019 is a naming artefact rather than a missing delivery: its PDFs arrived as `AAHM_019_*.pdf` and landed in the separate `AAHM_019` directory.
**Duplicate controls.** 9 forms carry **two** metadata controls (the same duplication noted in PROBLEM-metadata-name); both get the same value, which is what the engine does — `profiles/ubs/aem/root.xml:173` writes the whole-form list to every entity, and the corpus has no form with more than one distinct entity. AAQG_033's second control carries a foreign `formrange_code="AAOS"` — a separate copy-paste defect, deliberately **not** touched here.
**Nesting trap.** `<entities>` holds one `item0`, which itself holds `<cdoks><item0>`. A flat `<item\d+>` match hits both and reports two entity items per form; the nested cdok item has no `formrange_language`, so a naive fixer would look for a target that isn't there. `metadata_languages.entity_items()` depth-tracks the direct children. Verified across all 304 packages: exactly one entity item each (nine forms have two `<entities>` blocks), every one already carrying `formrange_language`, and every control already carrying `formrange_afmasterlanguage`.
**This value has no runtime consumer — so a rendered form cannot confirm it.** A full grep of `ajila-forms-ubs` shows `formrange_language` is mapped into `Entity.languageSubset` (`MetadataComponentMapper.java:67`) and never read again; nothing in DoR rendering, CLP, or submission touches it (runtime language comes from `txtLanguage` in the submitted data via `FormDataReader`). The dialog field has no `pattern` or validation either. That makes the change low-risk, but it also means **a pilot cannot be verified by eyeballing the form** — check the value in the AEM editor dialog (Properties → the metadata control → "Language Subset"), not the rendered output. Note the dialog's read-only field above it ("Languages (XLIFF translations)", fed from the dictionary folder by `FormMetadataService.getSupportedLanguages()`) will now legitimately disagree with the editable subset on the 34 forms named above.
**Runner trap hit during the 2026-08-18 rollout — a stray worktree loses the whole batch.** `run_sweep`'s commit step does `git worktree add --force -B sweep/<slug>`, which fails if *any* existing worktree has that branch checked out; the failure is a `sys.exit()` **inside** the `try`, so the `finally` runs `git checkout -- forms/issued` and discards every fixed ZIP. The forms stay deployed in AEM while nothing is committed — silent unless you read the exit line. Recovery is to remove the stray worktree and re-run the same `--only` batch (the fixer is idempotent). Corollary: **roll out in `--only` chunks rather than one `--continue`**; the commit happens once at the end of a run, so a 65-form run that dies at form 60 loses all 60.
**Superseded 2026-08-18 rollout (kept for provenance).** 224/224 fixed + deployed under the union-with-dictionaries rule, PR #122 merged as `741dea3`, verified in the live AEM on a 36-form sample by computing each metadata node's JCR path and fetching `<root>/<path>.2.json` (`guideContainer.-1.json` returns HTTP 300 on larger forms, and a QueryBuilder search on `formrange_language` 500s because the property is unindexed — neither is usable). Its master rule was entity-based *unconditionally except for single-language forms*, and its "no evidence → leave alone" bucket (27 forms) is gone: the region fallback now covers them. The reference form for that pass was AAQT_033 (`jcr:lastModifiedBy="t406261"`, `EN,IT`→`IT` and master `EN`→`IT`), which the current rule still reproduces exactly (one IT PDF → `IT`).

## PROBLEM-visual-editor-rules — Rules must be code-editor JavaScript, not visual-editor rules
**Symptom:** components carry rules authored in the **visual rule editor**: the component's `<fd:rules>` node holds a rule property whose value is a rule-tree JSON (`nodeName:"ROOT"`) with the machine-generated JavaScript in its `script` key. Every rule must instead be a **code-editor** rule — a SCRIPTMODEL value on the component's `<fd:scripts>` node (the shape AEM itself writes for the code editor; 10'000+ corpus-attested values) — with the `fd:rules` node left canonically **empty** (`<fd:rules jcr:primaryType="nt:unstructured"/>`, the corpus norm: 8'627 of them, and PROBLEM-toolbar-nav-handler's blessed target shape). **838 rules in 224 of 304 packages** (fd:visible 324, fd:click 234, fd:valueCommit 142, fd:init 112, fd:calc 23, fd:validate 3); 61 are disabled (`enabled:false`). **Origin: manual (owner directive 2026-08-24).**

**The conversion is mechanical, and behaviour-preserving by construction.** A visual rule stores BOTH the clickable rule tree (editor metadata only) and the machine-generated JavaScript that actually executes; all 838 parse and carry that script (0 bad JSON, 0 missing bodies), and every value's `eventName` matches its property. The move is: same code (the `/** machine-generated */` banner is stripped — it warns about the visual editor, which no longer applies), same event, same property name, `enabled` preserved (`enabled:false` is corpus-attested on fd:scripts — 739 values — so a disabled rule stays disabled instead of being silently activated), and the SCRIPTMODEL's `field` derived from the XML ancestry, not copied (the stored one is stale on copy-pasted rules — same lesson as visible-on-init). After the sweep the rule is viewed and edited in the code editor only; what is lost is exactly the visual-editor representation, which is the point.

**Two shapes on one component is real, and ordering there is the pilot's check.** 379 components carry the same event property in **both** lineages; the converted value is **appended after** the existing code-editor values, and the pre-conversion relative execution order between the lineages is not observable from the package. The corpus's one verbatim duplicate (AACQ_019 `BT_Remove` — a **disabled** visual click rule whose script already sits enabled on fd:scripts) is dropped, not doubled; an **enabled** rule always converts verbatim, twin or no twin (dropping one against a disabled twin would deactivate a running rule).

**Interaction with PROBLEM-visible-on-init (guard-enrolled):** its detector reads `fd:visible` from either node, and its Initialize copies were derived from the same banner-stripped bodies this sweep emits — so a converted form still reads as satisfied. The fixer asserts this per form (`visible_init_invariant`: the set of Initialize bodies visible-on-init would still want is identical before and after) and the 224-form simulation confirms it corpus-wide. No ordering dependency on other sweeps.

**Detect:** script: `find_visual_rules.py`  _(affected = any component whose `fd:rules` node carries a rule property; empty `<fd:rules/>` nodes are canonical and never matched. Buckets `auto` / `manual` (unknown rule property, non-JSON value, eventName contradicting its property — the fixer leaves such a component untouched). `--form <F>`, `--verbose`. Idempotent: the fix leaves only empty `fd:rules` nodes, so a re-scan reports 0. Corpus 2026-08-24: 224 auto, 0 manual.)_
**Fix:** script: `set_visual_rules.py`  _(byte-offset splices applied right-to-left, quote-aware — rule JSON and rich text contain literal `>`. Appends the converted SCRIPTMODELs to `fd:scripts` (created when absent — 0 needed in the current corpus), never rewrites or reorders an existing value, then empties the `fd:rules` node. All-or-nothing per component: a `manual` component keeps its `fd:rules` intact rather than losing an unconverted rule. Prints `{inner, components, rules_converted, by_prop, dedup_skipped, disabled_preserved, scripts_nodes_created, manual, remaining, visible_init_invariant, well_formed, ok}`; `ok` = well-formed + the detector's own re-scan finds nothing + the visible-on-init invariant holds. `--dry-run`; shared logic and `--selftest` in `visual_rules.py`.)_
**Verify:** must-not: `<fd:rules\s+fd:`  _(+ the fix's `ok` — the regex alone proves the visual rules are gone, the `ok` additionally proves the converted SCRIPTMODELs landed and the visible-on-init invariant held)_
**Max changed lines:** _(omitted — the touched lines are few but enormous (each is a full FileVault-escaped rule JSON), so a line budget measures nothing; the real bounds are the fixer's `ok` re-scan and `rules_converted` matching the detector's per-form count.)_
**Runs automatically:** yes
**Origin:** manual (owner directive 2026-08-24 — remove visual editor rules; every rule lives in the code editor as JavaScript)
**Tracking:** #133
**Status:** **swept 2026-08-24 (224/224 fixed + deployed; PR #134).** Detector reports **0 affected on the sweep branch** (304/304 packages clean, 0 visual rules) and `check_regressions.py` is green, so the CI guard is honestly enrolled.

**Rollout verified in the live AEM, exhaustively — not sampled.** Every one of the **224** deployed forms was walked in the JCR and compared against the locally recomputed expected state: **224/224 clean, 0 problems, 10'321 rule values matched** (property, event, `enabled`, normalised body), **741 disabled flags matched**, **0 visual rules remaining anywhere**. Note `guideContainer.infinity.json` returns **HTTP 300** on the larger forms — fall back to a bounded-depth walk with `.2.json` requests per node, or the check silently skips exactly the forms most worth checking.

**Value conservation, corpus-wide: 10'322 → 10'321 (−1).** Comparing master against the branch, every rule value's (property, `enabled`, body) is preserved once the intended banner strip is normalised away; **exactly one form differs — AACQ_019**, the designed dedup of its disabled `fd:click` duplicate. Nothing else moved.

**The 81 non-round-tripping `fd:init` attributes (20 forms) are pre-existing and were never touched.** `visible_init.py --selftest` reports the same 81 codec failures on master as on the branch — unparseable JSON in old `fd:init` values, not caused by this sweep. Checked explicitly that **no form has the fixer rewriting a non-round-tripping attribute** (0 overlaps corpus-wide): the fixer only re-encodes the properties it converts, and every other attribute survives as raw text.

**AACM_019 renders HTTP 500 — pre-existing, NOT this sweep.** An NPE in AEM's own `GuideContainer.createGuideInitializationState`, nothing to do with rules. Proven by the control CLAUDE.md prescribes: installing the **pre-fix** package reproduces the identical 500, and six untouched control forms all render 200. The swept bytes were then restored via `review_pr.py --sweep visual-editor-rules --only AACM_019` (never `aem_install.py` on the local path, which would have left the pre-fix package deployed) and re-read from AEM: 0 visual rules, 41 code-editor values.

**Rolled out in 6 chunks of 40 + the 7-form pilot** (`--only`), not one `--continue`: run_sweep commits once at the end of a run, so a single 217-form run dying late loses every form it had already deployed. All chunks clean, 0 flagged.

**Pilot chosen to cover every variant, and verified by reading AEM back — not from install return codes.** AAOV_033 (the reported form; visible+click; toolbar-nav's reference), AACQ_019 (the corpus's only dedup case), AACC_019 (most rules 23, most disabled 14, 19 both-lineage components), AAHE_019 (the only `fd:validate` form; 0 both-lineage — clean control), BAXT_019 (calc-dominant, 2 disabled), AABT_033 (`fd:init` coverage, 10 both-lineage), AAHM_af_ab_019 (irregular directory name). Deployed JCR walked per form: **0 visual rules remain**, and the multiset of (property, event, enabled, normalised body) — 744 values — **matches the locally recomputed expected state exactly, 0 missing / 0 extra**, disabled counts included. All 7 render HTTP 200, no error markers. (`.infinity.json` returns HTTP 300 on the larger forms; walk with bounded-depth `.2.json` requests.)

**The runtime equivalence question is real, and "same code" does NOT settle it — the two lineages compile to DIFFERENT expressions.** A visual rule is **inlined** with AEM's AST codegen rewriting the branches (`if (…) { Visibility = true; } else { Visibility = false; }`); a code rule is passed to **`eval()`** and its **completion value** is assigned (`Visibility = eval("if (…) { true; } else { false; }")`). They agree only where the body's completion value is the intended result — so this was checked across **all 838** bodies, on the three events where completion value matters: **fd:visible all 324 are bare `true;`/`false;` literals**, **fd:calc all 23 end in a bare expression**, **fd:validate all 3 end in a bare boolean expression**. The other 453 (click, init, valueCommit) are side-effect events whose completion value is discarded. Confirmed in the deployed forms — AAOV's `visibleExp`, BAXT's `calcExp`, AAHE's `validateExp` all compile through the eval path correctly. The eval is a *direct* eval (so `this` and scope resolve as before), and this is the corpus's **dominant** lineage: 662 pre-existing code-editor `fd:visible` rules already run this way in production. **A future rule body whose completion value is not the result would break silently — re-run that shape census before sweeping newly intaken forms.**

**`enabled:false` survives to runtime, proven end to end:** BAXT_019 has 13 calc rules, 2 disabled (`TXT_Accountholder`, `TXT_PoA`), and the deployed form renders exactly **11** `calcExp`.

**Pilot review is HUMAN and still open:** (1) open a converted rule in the AEM rule editor and confirm it shows as a code-editor JavaScript rule; (2) AAOV_033 Tipo → Individual/LegalEntity containers show and hide correctly, on change **and** on first load; (3) **ordering** on the both-lineage components (AACC_019 19, AABT_033 10, AACQ_019 5) — converted rules are appended *after* the existing code-editor rules and the pre-conversion order is not observable from the package; AACQ_019 `BT_Remove` (repeatable remove + DoR mirroring) is the one to watch.

**Replaces PROBLEM-formcalc-to-js (entry removed 2026-08-24, owner directive).** That sweep's work stands — 898 FormCalc DoR-XDP scripts were converted to JavaScript and verified exhaustively in the live AEM (PR #128, merged `eb2cf33`, tracking #127) — but its registry entry, its scripts (`formcalc_js.py`, `find_formcalc_script.py`, `set_formcalc_js.py`) and its CI guard are retired (history in git). Consequence to know: a newly intaken package carrying FormCalc in a DoR XDP rendition is no longer flagged by any guard — the marker, if it is ever needed again, is a `<script>` element with **no `contentType` attribute** (there is no "formcalc" string to grep for).

---

## PROBLEM-nav-save-progress-required — Every form's toolbar needs the "Save Progress" button
**Symptom:** the wizard toolbar must carry the **"Save Progress"** button — a `guidebutton` (`sling:resourceType="fd/af/components/guidebutton"`, `guideNodeClass="guideButton"`, `dorExclusion="true"`, `dorFieldStyling="Default"`, `textIsRich="[true,true,true]"`, `type="Button"`, children `fd:rules` (empty) + `fd:scripts` with `fd:click` → `window.forms.ubs.fwb.saveFormData();`) named `fwbSaveProgress`, as a direct child of `toolbar > items`, **and it must not be hidden**. QA reversed the directive behind [PROBLEM-nav-save-progress-button](#) / #50 — the button is **always required** — so **0 of 304 packages** currently have it (#50 removed it from 119; the other 185 never had it). It is appended as the **last** toolbar child, which keeps [PROBLEM-nav-button-order](#) / #27 green (that rule only asserts the three core buttons come first, Next → Submit → Back).

**Reference = the deployed corpus before PR #51, not the engine.** The conversion engine never emitted this button (`profiles/ubs/aem/root.xml`'s toolbar is next/submit/prev/preview only), so there is nothing to regenerate from — do not "correct" the node against engine templates. All 119 pre-#50 nodes, recovered from `1999a4c^1`, are **byte-identical** apart from three things, each an explicit decision: `jcr:created`/`jcr:lastModified`(`By`) are **dropped** rather than invented (AEM stamps them on the next author save); `jcr:title` is the English master `Save Progress` on every form (11 legacy forms hard-coded a localized title — `Salvare Progressi`, `Fortschritt speichern`, `Salva i progressi` — which the `fd_Save Progress` dictionary entry from [PROBLEM-default-translations](#) / #72 cannot translate); and `visible` is **omitted** so the button renders (49 forms shipped `visible="{Boolean}false"`, all of them Italy `_033` — owner-confirmed the button is shown everywhere).
**The label follows the same language rule.** `Save Progress` is a dictionary key, so it is what a form with a dictionary carries; a package with none gets `Salva progressi` / `Fortschritt speichern` from `data/translations/`, or it shows English on an Italian form. 30 forms carry the master with no dictionary to translate it.
**Detect:** script: `find_save_progress_required.py`  _(affected = the toolbar `items` has no child named `fwbSaveProgress` (`insert`), or has one carrying `visible="{Boolean}false"` (`unhide`). Flagging hidden as well as absent is deliberate — a legacy Italy package arriving at intake would otherwise satisfy a presence-only check while the button never renders. Matches on the child's `name` inside the toolbar, not a raw substring. Reported, never auto-fixed: `title_nonmaster`, `duplicate`. `--form <F>`. Idempotent: a fixed form reports 0.)_
**Fix:** script: `set_save_progress.py`  _(byte-offset insertion (expat spans): appends the canonical node after the last non-`layout` toolbar child, at that sibling's indentation (24 across all 304 forms, computed per form anyway); or, when the button is present but hidden, excises the `visible` attribute from its open tag. `--dry-run`. Prints `{inner, inserted, unhidden, indent, ok}`; **hard self-check** — `ok` requires the output to parse AND the toolbar to hold exactly one non-hidden `fwbSaveProgress`. A form with no toolbar `items`, or with empty `items`, returns `ok:false` + `why` rather than guessing.)_
**Verify:** must: `name="fwbSaveProgress"`  _(+ the fix's own `ok` (well-formed + exactly one visible button in the toolbar) + a re-scan showing 0 affected + a pilot **visual check**: the toolbar shows the Save Progress button, it is the last button in the row, the other buttons render unchanged, and clicking it saves)_
**Max changed lines:** 16 — the insert is exactly 15 added lines (measured on `_019` and `_033`); an unhide changes 1.
**Runs automatically:** yes
**Origin:** manual (QA/owner directive reversing #50; the pre-PR #51 corpus is the confirmed reference)
**Tracking:** #135
**Status:** **swept 2026-08-24 (304/304 fixed + deployed, 0 flagged, PR #136)** — piloted 7 (PR #136) — pilot chosen to cover 7 of the 9 toolbar variants (last child previtemnav / preview / custom-preview x `_019`/`_033` x button-restored / never-had-one): AABH_019, AAAE_019, AABE_019, AACX_033, AAAA_019, AAOV_033, AACB_033. All 7 `fix ok:true`, `delta ok` (engine MCP built for the run — no new AEM violations), `diff` exactly 15 added lines, `verify ok`, `deploy ok`. Verified by **reading the deployed JCR back**, not from install return codes: all 7 have exactly one `fwbSaveProgress`, it is the last toolbar child, `jcr:title="Save Progress"`, no `visible`, `type="Button"`, `dorExclusion="true"`, click handler intact. All 7 render HTTP 200 with the button in the markup. Then rolled out in **8 `--only` chunks of 40 + 17**, not one `--continue` — run_sweep commits once at the end of a run, so a 297-form run dying late would lose every form it had already deployed. Every chunk 0-flagged.

**Rollout verified exhaustively in the live AEM, not sampled.** All **304** deployed forms were read back from the JCR (`toolbar/items.2.json`) and compared against the expected node: **304/304 clean, 0 problems** — exactly one `fwbSaveProgress` per toolbar, **last child** in every case, `jcr:title="Save Progress"`, no `visible`, `type="Button"`, `dorExclusion="true"`, `dorFieldStyling="Default"`, `guideNodeClass="guideButton"`, `sling:resourceType="fd/af/components/guidebutton"`, `textIsRich` present, and the `fd:click` → `saveFormData();` handler intact. Note `textIsRich` comes back from the JCR as a **JSON array** (`["true","true","true"]`), not the FileVault `[true,true,true]` string — a read-back check that compares it as a string reports 304 false problems. The branch's own detector also reports **0 affected / 304 ok**.

**Surfaces pre-existing dictionary debt — the label renders in English on 19 forms.** The button's label comes from the `fd_Save Progress` dictionary entry, and [PROBLEM-default-translations](#) / #72 was **additive-only**: a form that already had that key kept its own message. Corpus-wide, 1'092 of 1'121 non-English dictionary files translate it correctly, but **24 carry a wrong message** — `de-ch` 5 forms and `it` 3 forms and `es` 11 forms hold the literal English `Save Progress`, plus wording variants (`Guardar Progreso` x2, `Guardar el progreso`, `Salva i progressi` x2) — and **5 more lack the key** (`it-ch` 4, `fr-ch` 1). Caught on pilot form AACX_033: its `it.xml` has `sling:key="fd_Save Progress"` / `sling:message="Save Progress"`, so the Italian render shows the English label. This is **not caused by this sweep** — the entries pre-date it — but the sweep makes it visible, because before it there was no button to label. Needs its own entry/sweep (overwrite a default-key message that equals the English master on a non-English locale); do not widen this one.
## PROBLEM-banking-subtitle — First-page title before the banking fragment must be a `subtitle-after-form-title` static text
**Symptom:** on a form's **first page**, a heading that sits **before the banking-relationship fragment** is an `h2` step title, and an `h2` step title is **not visible in the finished DoR/PDF**. It must instead be a plain **static text** carrying `css="subtitle-after-form-title"`, which does render. Target shape (owner-specified 2026-08-24; **no prior instance exists anywhere** — not in the 304 packages, not in this repo, not in the engine — so the shape is the owner's directive, not something read off a reference): `sling:resourceType` `…/controls/titledraw` → `…/controls/textdraw`, `headingLevel="2"` **removed**, the `stepTitle` css token → `subtitle-after-form-title` (**other class tokens kept**), `name` `TTL_*` → `ST_*`. The enclosing `{step}Title` panel is **KEPT**: it is the node that carries `jumpToFieldButtonVisible`, so deleting it would physically remove the jump-to-field button from the form. Keeping vs deleting it makes **no** difference to the regression guard (measured both ways) — that choice is purely about the rendered form. **That panel must not have a title** (owner directive 2026-08-26): it repeats the subtitle as its own `jcr:title` (86 of 89 byte-identical to the draw's text, e.g. AAOV_033 `PN_…Title jcr:title="Attestazione di avvenuta consegna"` wrapping the draw of the same text), so the fix removes the attribute and the subtitle then exists once, in the static text. Nothing else on the panel changes. The panel is found by **nesting, not by name** — 88 are `{step}Title` but AACB_033's is `PN_Titolare` — and each of the 89 holds exactly one child, the draw itself, so no other content loses a heading. On screen this changes nothing (a panel `jcr:title` does not render as a heading). **What it changes in the DoR is not established**: a titled panel without `dorExcludeTitle` is expected to render its title there, and the converted draw still carries `dorExclusion="true"` in 93 of 94 forms, so the DoR heading may be coming from the panel title today and the strip may remove it — the owner chose the panel-title strip alone (2026-08-26) and the draw's `dorExclusion` stays a separate open question.
**Scope (94 of 304 packages).** One rule reproduces every per-form decision the owner made: **0** headings before page-1 banking → not affected (36 forms whose only preceding node is body prose); exactly **1** heading → convert it (91); **>1** headings with exactly one `css="stepTitle"` → convert that one and leave the rest (AAAM_019, AAFK_019, AATH_033); **>1** with zero or several marked → **flag**, a human decides (AAQR_033). Shapes: 75 `titledraw h2 stepTitle` in a title panel · 12 `titledraw h2` unmarked in a title panel · 3 `titledraw h2 stepTitle` loose · 3 `textdraw h2 stepTitle` loose · 1 `textdraw h2 stepTitle` in a panel. **Detection keys on `sling:resourceType`, never the XML tag** — 29 nodes in the corpus have a tag that disagrees with their resourceType (AAPQ_033's `AF_Subject` is tagged textbox-ish but is really a titledraw), so the tag both over- and under-matches.
**Detect:** script: `find_banking_subtitle.py`  _(emits `convert[]` {form,step,node,rt,heading_level,css,in_title_panel,title_panel,panel,panel_title,banking_segment,headings_on_page,text} + `untitle[]` {form,step,node,panel,panel_title,panel_children} + `flag[]` {form,step,reason,nodes} + `already[]`; that is the review surface. `--form <F>`, `--verbose`. The verdict is derived from the form's **state**, not from what a run changed: a form whose draw is already converted but whose wrapper panel still has a `jcr:title` is `untitle` — **affected**, panel-title strip only — which is how the 89 forms converted before the panel-title rule existed (2026-08-26) are detected. `already` (not affected) means draw converted **and** wrapper untitled, so a rebase + `--continue` never re-converts.)_
**Fix:** script: `set_banking_subtitle.py` _(byte-offset splices; delegates detection to the detector so the two can never disagree. The `name` **definition** rename is byte-scoped to the target's own open tag — a global `name="old"` swap renames a same-named sibling too, which is how AASV_033 ended up with a *titledraw* called `ST_*`; `fd:*` rule/script references are rewritten globally with `set_naming_prefix.py`'s exact scoping, so `jcr:title`/`jcr:description`/`_value`/`options`/`fd:translationIds`/`bindRef` are never touched. Only `TTL_` is renamed; `ST_` is already right and a non-standard prefix (AAPQ_033 `AF_Subject`) is reported in `review_name`, not guessed at. The enclosing panel's `jcr:title` cut is scoped to that panel's **own open tag** (quote-aware, so a rich-text `>` cannot end the tag early) — a search over the element would hit a descendant's title instead — and is applied after the draw splice so the earlier offsets stay valid. Prints `{node,new_name,rt,headingLevel_removed,css_kept,title_panel,jump_button_preserved,panel,panel_title_removed,refs_rewritten,review_css,review_name,review_panel,name_shared_with,well_formed,recheck,ok}`; `ok` = the edit landed AND the result re-parses AND the detector now says `already`. Unit tests: `.claude/scripts/tests/test_banking_subtitle.py` (10, plain asserts).)_
**Verify:** the fix's `ok` + a re-scan reporting 0 affected + a pilot **visual check in AEM — specifically the generated DoR/PDF**, since DoR visibility is the entire point of the change and no script can assert it. For the 2026-08-26 panel-title pass the whole edit is one removed attribute: check the diff by hand (`form_diff.py --before <master zip> --after <fixed zip>`) — measured 1 changed line on AAOV_033 and AACB_033, 2 on AAFK_019 where the panel tag shares a line. Anything larger on an `untitle` form means the wrong node was cut. (`Max changed lines` stays omitted for the entry: the conversion path also rewrites `fd:` rule references, which no single number bounds.)
**Max changed lines:** omit — a single open-tag rewrite plus reference edits; gate on `ok` + the re-scan.
**Interacts with two swept rules — exemption is delegated, not duplicated.** Removing the `h2` makes **PROBLEM-step-title-panel** (74 of the 94) and **PROBLEM-jump-to-field-button** (23) re-flag: both look for the step's first `headingLevel="2"` draw, and the first h2 remaining after the conversion is a deeper *section* heading, which they would then "fix" onto the wrong node. Both rules' **detector _and_ fixer** (the fixer matters — `apply_sweeps.py` runs it on every intake and would otherwise undo this sweep every run) now exempt a converted page by calling **`find_banking_subtitle.is_converted_step()`**.
